# TRAIN task: tool_recommendation  (1,500 instances)

## [0] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have predicted protein sequences and need a batch tool to query functional databases and identify protein domains. What tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Interproscan`, a batch tool designed to query the InterPro database and multiple profile/functional databases for protein sequence annotation and functional domain identification.

---

## [1] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a case-control genetic epidemiology study and want to test gene-environment interactions. What tool would you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend 'CGEN'. It is specifically designed for genetic epidemiology case-control data and provides statistical methods to evaluate gene-environment and gene-gene interactions.

---

## [2] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have RNA-seq FASTQ data and need to detect both known and novel somatic fusion genes, translocations, and chimeric transcripts. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use FusionCatcher. It is specifically designed to search for novel and known somatic fusion genes, translocations, and chimeras directly from RNA-seq data.

---

## [3] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am looking for a tool within Biomni that can detect and characterize structural variations in genomic sequencing data. What is available?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Biomni: Detect And Characterize Structural Variations`. It is designed to detect and characterize structural variations (SVs) in genomic sequencing data.

---

## [4] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a tumor-normal paired cancer genomics study and need to normalize somatic copy number variations against a control baseline. What component is suitable?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use CNVkit Theta (Variant Analysis), which builds and applies a tabular reference baseline from control/normal samples to normalize test samples for somatic copy number variation analysis.

---

## [5] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I ran several different taxonomic profilers on my metagenomic data and each returned a completely different tabular output format. What tool can I use to standardize them?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'taxpasta'. Its primary purpose is to standardise taxonomic profiles generated by a variety of bioinformatics taxonomic profilers into a consistent format.

---

## [6] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am curating a 16S rRNA reference sequence database for amplicon classification in QIIME 2 and need a tool to inspect sequence entropy and length distributions. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `qiime2 rescript evaluate-seqs`. It is specifically designed for amplicon sequencing, metagenomics, and ITS reference database evaluation, producing summary statistics on length distributions, unique sequence counts, and sequence/kmer entropy along with interactive visual visualizations.

---

## [7] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am developing an interactive web-based genomic track viewer and need an R/Bioconductor package that can expose Bioconductor data structures directly over a WebSocket connection. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `epivizrData`. It is designed specifically to serve data directly from Bioconductor objects over a WebSocket connection.

---

## [8] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with spatial transcriptomics data and need a tool to deconvolute spatial spots using single-cell transcriptomics data. What tool fits this task?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `SPOTlight`. It is designed for single-cell transcriptomics and spatial transcriptomics to deconvolute spatial spots using a seeded NMF (Non-Negative Matrix Factorization) approach, and it includes visualization tools to assess the deconvolution results.

---

## [9] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which proteomics tool is suitable for Peptide mass fingerprinting (PMF) workflows, mass spectrometry-based protein identification, and tandem MS (MS/MS) analysis to compute mass and m/z values?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `MassCalculator`. It is a proteomics tool designed for peptide mass fingerprinting (PMF) workflows, mass spectrometry-based protein identification, and tandem MS (MS/MS) analysis, capable of outputting human-readable lists, tables, mass values only, or m/z values only.

---

## [10] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on sequence analysis of crosslinking data and need to filter out minor crosslinking events that fall below a certain proportion of the peak read count at each position. Is there a tool for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'Remove spurious' tool from the Sequence Analysis category. It is designed to remove all crosslinking events that combine fewer reads than a designated fraction of the maximum number of reads at that specific position.

---

## [11] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am looking for a statistical framework in R for one-sample and two-sample ChIP-seq analysis of transcription factor binding and histone marks. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `mosaics`. It provides functions for fitting the MOSAiCS and MOSAiCS-HMM statistical models specifically designed for one-sample or two-sample ChIP-seq data.

---

## [12] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** In our cancer transcriptome project, we want to extract discordant mapping reads and unmapped reads from RNA-seq data to perform de novo assembly for fusion gene detection. Which tool fits this task?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `ctat_discasm` (DISCASM). It is a component of the Trinity Cancer Transcriptome Analysis Toolkit (CTAT) designed to extract reads that map discordantly (and optionally unmapped reads) to perform de novo transcriptome assembly.

---

## [13] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are designing a metagenomic assembly pipeline that relies on partition-based error correction. Which tool from the khmer suite handles partition extraction?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'khmer: Extract partitions', which is designated for metagenomic assembly with partition-based error correction and de novo genome assembly.

---

## [14] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on microRNA target identification and want to integrate sequence context scores with microRNA-overexpression fold-change data. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use TargetScore. It is specifically designed to infer microRNA targets by probabilistically modelling the likelihood of microRNA-overexpression fold-changes combined with sequence-based scores using a Variational Bayesian Gaussian mixture model (VB-GMM).

---

## [15] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on gene expression quantification and need a tool to configure and simplify processing single-cell data with alevin-fry. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `Simpleaf Quant` (from SimpleAF). It is specifically designed to simplify and customize the configuration and running of single-cell processing workflows with alevin-fry.

---

## [16] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on an RNA-seq annotation refinement workflow and want to extend the 3' ends of my existing gene models using assembled transcriptomes from another GTF file, without creating new overlaps between genes. What tool can I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `BREW3R.r`. It is specifically designed for transcriptomics workflows to extend 3' end annotations in an input GTF using annotations from a second GTF, while ensuring no new overlaps are introduced between different genes.

---

## [17] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have metabolomics and proteomics feature tables where I need to standardize messy column headers by finding specific name patterns and replacing them. Which tool fits this requirement?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'table rename columns via regex' tool. It allows you to search for regular expression patterns within column names and replace target substrings across metabolomics, proteomics, transcriptomics, or genomics datasets.

---

## [18] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have RNA-Seq data and need a statistical tool for differential expression testing between sample groups. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend rDiff, a statistics tool specifically designed for differential expression testing of RNA-Seq data across sample groups.

---

## [19] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with pairsam files in sequence analysis and need to separate the SAM1/SAM2 alignments from the pairs information. What tool should I choose?

**ASSISTANT:** You should choose Pairtools split. It is specifically designed to restore SAM/BAM files from the SAM1 and SAM2 fields while generating a pairs file without SAM1/SAM2 fields.

---

## [20] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am performing quality control on aligned BAM files from a whole genome sequencing run and need to evaluate nucleotide distribution per cycle and GC bias. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'CollectGcBiasMetrics'. It is specifically built for SAM and BAM files to chart per-cycle nucleotide distribution and evaluate GC bias in WGS, WES, and targeted sequencing datasets.

---

## [21] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have genome sequencing data for bacterial isolates of Pseudomonas aeruginosa and need to perform in silico serogrouping. Which nf-core tool is built for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `pasty`. It is an nf-core tool designed specifically for in silico serogrouping of *Pseudomonas aeruginosa* isolates.

---

## [22] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have protein structure files that were compressed using foldcomp and need a tool to unpack them. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the Foldcomp Decompress tool, which is specifically designed for decompressing foldcomp compressed protein structures.

---

## [23] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with flow cytometry measurements of aquatic samples and need an automated or semi-automated way to distinguish phytoplankton and cyanobacteria cells from background debris using pigment fluorescence channels. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `cyanoFilter`. It is specifically designed to filter out and identify phytoplankton and cyanobacteria populations from particles measured via flow cytometry. It processes pigment and cell complexity information by applying a sequence of one-dimensional gates on predefined channels.

---

## [24] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a pre-processing tool for our structural materials testing experiments. Is CHESS Test Pre-processing Tool relevant for this?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes. The CHESS Test Pre-processing Tool is categorized specifically under Structural Materials Analysis and is intended for materials science data rather than biological or omics data.

---

## [25] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can you recommend a proteomics tool or workflow tailored for high-dimensional cytometry analysis that follows tidyverse design principles?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend the `tidytof` workflow (utilizing `bioconductor/tidytof`). It is designed for high-dimensional cytometry data within the proteomics domain, built upon tidy data principles and fully compatible with the tidyverse and Bioconductor ecosystems.

---

## [26] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working in R and need to import network data from the NDEx platform in CX format and convert it into an iGraph object for graph analysis. Which tool handles this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `RCX` tool. It is designed to create, handle, validate, visualize, and convert networks in Cytoscape exchange (CX) format—the format used by NDEx—and provides built-in conversion to and from iGraph and graphNEL objects.

---

## [27] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a mathematical modeling tool in systems biology to simulate the RAS signaling cycle accounting for GEF and GAP regulation via ordinary differential equations. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `ras_ode_system`, which implements an ODE system for the RAS signaling cycle regulated by GEF and GAP.

---

## [28] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing 16S rRNA gene amplicon sequencing data and need a tool that can produce both heatmap and ordination plots using phyloseq to visualize microbial community structures. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend 'FROGSSTAT Phyloseq Structure Visualisation'. It leverages phyloseq and custom R functions to construct heatmap and ordination plots specifically for microbial community and amplicon analysis.

---

## [29] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which utility package should I use to convert chemical structure data from InChI format into an SDF file?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'InChI to SDF' tool package, which handles conversion from InChI format to SDF (Structure-Data File).

---

## [30] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Is there a tool designed to analyze the impact of DNA or RNA epigenetic chemical modifications on the dysregulation of genes or proteins?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, epidecodeR is designed specifically to analyze the impact of the degree of DNA/RNA epigenetic chemical modifications on gene or protein dysregulation.

---

## [31] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a metagenomics project and need a tool to convert a UniFrac sample mapping file into an OTU table format. What tool is available for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `convert_unifrac_sample_mapping_to_otu_table`, which is tailored for metagenomics data to perform conversions from UniFrac sample mapping formats directly to OTU tables.

---

## [32] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have completed an RNA-seq differential expression analysis with DESeq2 and edgeR, and I need a tool that can generate HTML or PDF summary reports to explore the results. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `regionReport`. It generates HTML or PDF reports to explore sets of genomic regions, and it natively supports creating reports for DESeq2 and edgeR results, as well as base-pair resolution outputs from derfinder.

---

## [33] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Do you have a Graphics utility tool designed to sharpen image filters?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use the 'Filter - Sharpen' tool located under the Graphics domain.

---

## [34] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have digital gene expression count data generated from an RNA-Seq experiment and need to identify differentially expressed genes. Which tool can I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `ctat_edger_differential_expression`, which utilizes the edgeR Bioconductor package designed specifically for the analysis of digital gene expression data from RNA-Seq sequencing technologies.

---

## [35] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a set of genomic intervals and need to extract multi-species sequence alignment blocks for those regions from alignments in MAF format. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Extract MAF blocks' tool from the Genomic Interval Operations suite. It superimposes your genomic coordinates onto multiple alignments (MAF format) from your history or the Galaxy site and excises the matching alignment blocks.

---

## [36] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on molecular dynamics simulations using GROMACS via biobb and need a component to generate position restraints. Which tool handles this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the Genrestr tool from biobb_md (GROMACS wrapper). You can review the syntax and options at https://biobb-md.readthedocs.io/en/latest/gromacs.html#module-gromacs.genrestr.

---

## [37] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** In our ecological study, we grouped sampling sites and need to find indicator species statistically associated with those sample groups using the IndVal approach. What tool can we use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `Indicspecies` tool. It wraps the `multipatt` function from the indicspecies R package, extending the Dufrêne and Legendre (1997) Indicator Value (IndVal) method to identify indicator species across sample groups.

---

## [38] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** In an RNA structural biology project analyzing RNA conformational ensembles, which tool allows me to compare secondary structures partitioned by basepair distance between two reference structures?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use RNA2Dfold. It is designed for RNA secondary structure comparison and ensemble analysis by partitioning the structure space into distance classes based on two reference structures.

---

## [39] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on metabolomics data and need a tool to perform variable selection using LASSO or Elastic Net. What is available?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'LASSO/Elastic Net Variable Selection' tool, which is designed for metabolomics variable selection.

---

## [40] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting an ecological survey and have a GIS shapefile. I need to generate a geographic map showing the percentage evolution rate of dead wood across regions. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Make a map with a shapefile (Ecology)' tool. It is specifically designed to build maps from shapefiles and color geographic areas according to a percentage, matching your dead wood evolution rate case study.

---

**USER:** I need a utility from the Convert Formats section in the system. Which tool matches this designation?

**ASSISTANT:** FFmpeg Format Converter is classified under the Convert Formats category.

## [42] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on an in silico bioengineering project and need a tool to simulate the entire CRISPR-Cas9 genome editing pipeline, including guide RNA design and delivery. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `perform_crispr_cas9_genome_editing`. It is tailored for bioengineering applications and simulates the CRISPR-Cas9 genome editing process, encompassing guide RNA design, delivery, and subsequent analysis.

---

## [43] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on cancer genomics variant calling and want to annotate my variants with functional prediction scores from several algorithms simultaneously. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use SnpSift Dbnsfp. It provides variant annotations by querying dbNSFP, an integrated database of functional predictions from multiple algorithms.

---

## [44] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing single-cell RNA-seq data and need a tool to standardize and validate cell type annotation labels in my metadata. What tool from Expression Atlas is available?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'Cell types - check labels' tool, developed by Andrey Solovyev from the Expression Atlas team at EMBL-EBI. It is designed for single-cell RNA-seq experiments with cell type annotations requiring standardized metadata.

---

## [45] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a list of GenBank UIDs and need to retrieve the corresponding sequence records in FASTA or GenBank format. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use NCBI EFetch. It accepts a list of UIDs for a specified NCBI database and retrieves the corresponding full data records in your requested format.

---

## [46] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on translated protein sequences and need a tool suitable for STOP codon validation and Open Reading Frame (ORF) identification. What tool should I look into?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `checktrans`. It is designed for sequence analysis tasks such as Open Reading Frame (ORF) identification in protein sequences and STOP codon validation in translated sequences.

---

## [47] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool can I use in Galaxy for cell filtering based on quality metrics in a single-cell RNA-seq pipeline?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use 'Scater Filter' (version 1.8.4+galaxy0), which is designed for single-cell transcriptomics (scRNA-seq) quality control workflows and cell filtering based on quality metrics.

---

## [48] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am processing paired-end 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing data using DADA2. Which tool should I use to assemble the sequence table?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `dada2: makeSequenceTable`. It is designed for paired-end sequencing workflows in metagenomics, 16S rRNA, and ITS amplicon sequencing to generate the sequence table.

---

## [49] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need an nf-core compatible Python tool to construct mutational matrices for somatic single base substitutions, double base substitutions, and indels. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `sigprofiler` (specifically `Sigprofilermatrixgenerator`), which is a Python-based tool designed to create mutational matrices for all somatic mutation types including SBS, DBS, and IDs.

---

## [50] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool can I use to detect somatic variants from massively parallel sequencing data, such as exome, whole-genome, or transcriptome sequencing?

**ASSISTANT:** VarScan somatic is designed for somatic variant detection using massively parallel sequencing data, including WGS, exome, and transcriptome datasets.

---

## [51] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a text manipulation utility to inspect line-by-line differences between two plain text pipeline config files. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the standard 'diff' tool. It is a text manipulation utility designed to calculate and display the textual differences between two files.

---

## [52] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I ran Jellyfish to count k-mers in my genomic sequencing reads, but the output is an unreadable binary file. Which tool should I use to convert it into text?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Jellyfish Dump`. It is designed specifically to dump results from a Jellyfish binary k-mer file into a human-readable format.

---

## [53] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need an R/Bioconductor tool to efficiently store, subset, process, and compare mass spectrometry spectra for metabolomics and proteomics experiments. What would you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend the Spectra package. It provides infrastructure for storing, handling, subsetting, processing, visualizing, and comparing mass spectrometry data across metabolomics and proteomics.

---

## [54] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a small genome assembly project with paired-end Illumina reads and Oxford Nanopore long reads, and I want to run MaSuRCA with standard default options. What tool should I choose?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `MaSuRCA simple`, which is specifically tailored for small assembly projects with mandatory Illumina PE reads and long reads (PacBio, Nanopore, or both) under default parameters.

---

## [55] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to inspect and extract source annotations from the second column of my GTF file for transcriptomic analysis. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `pyGetGTFSources`. It is specifically designed to extract source names from the second column of a GTF file in genomics and transcriptomics workflows.

---

## [56] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have run Pfam predictions both on the webserver and using the standalone tool. Is there an R package that can import these Pfam domain predictions into R?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you should use `pfamAnalyzeR`. It is an R package developed to import and analyze Pfam protein domain predictions obtained from both webserver and stand-alone runs into R.

---

## [57] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with very large microscopy images. To save compute, I registered sub-sampled versions that are 4 times smaller, but now I need to adjust the resulting elastic transformation coefficients back to the full image dimensions. Which tool can handle this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Adapt an elastic transformation'. It is specifically designed to transform the coefficients of an elastic transformation according to a real image factor, enabling workflows where registration is computed on sub-sampled images and scaled to full resolution.

---

## [58] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We have array CGH profiles from a cohort of cancer patients and want to perform joint segmentation to identify driver chromosomal imbalances and LOH. What tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `VegaMC`. It is specifically built for array comparative genomic hybridization (aCGH) data to perform joint segmentation and statistically identify driver chromosomal imbalances, including loss of heterozygosity (LOH).

---

## [59] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have bgzipped paired genomic coordinates from a Hi-C experiment and need fast 2D spatial indexing. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `pairix` (available via nf-core). It provides 2D indexing on bgzipped text files containing paired genomic coordinates, specifically suitable for Hi-C, 4C, 5C, and paired-end genomic coordinate analyses.

---

## [60] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Is there an R package that allows me to query the Metabolomics Workbench RESTful API and import study data directly into Bioconductor data structures?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, `metabolomicsWorkbenchR` interfaces directly with the Metabolomics Workbench RESTful API and can retrieve study data formatted into standard Bioconductor structures such as `SummarizedExperiment`.

---

## [61] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have spatial transcriptomics data and need to identify tissue domains that exhibit uniform cell type composition. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `concordexR`. It is designed specifically for identifying spatial homogeneous regions (SHRs) characterized by consistent cell type composition in spatial transcriptomics datasets.

---

## [62] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have multiple homologous genomic sequences that I need to align together into a multiple sequence alignment. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **Clustalo Align**, which runs Clustal Omega to perform multiple sequence alignments on nucleotide or protein sequences.

---

## [63] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can I use PyMuonSuite AIRSS Cluster to find clustering patterns in my single-cell RNA-seq data?

**ASSISTANT:** No. PyMuonSuite AIRSS Cluster is a materials science tool designed for muon site analysis in crystal structures and is not applicable to biological omics data.

---

## [64] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working in R and want to analyze clinical and experimental data from ImmPort studies, but the data is provided in a proprietary format. Is there an R package that can help me load and standardize this data?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `RImmPort`. It is designed specifically for the R environment to provide a standards-based interface to proprietary ImmPort study data, making immunology research datasets ready for analysis.

---

**USER:** I am analyzing 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing data for a comparative microbial community study and need an ordination tool from Mothur. What tool do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Pcoa' tool from the Mothur suite, which is tailored for microbial ecology, 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing, and community composition analysis.

---

## [66] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on population genetics data structures and need a bioinformatics tool specifically designated for handling linkage disequilibrium structures. What can I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'ldblock' package, which specializes in defining data structures for linkage disequilibrium measures across populations.

---

## [67] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are conducting a biodiversity assessment of a tropical forest canopy using high-resolution airborne imaging spectroscopy. Which tool is suited for mapping diversity from this data?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'Map diversity' tool. It implements the methodology developed by Féret and Asner (2014) specifically designed for tropical forest diversity mapping using very high spatial resolution airborne imaging spectroscopy.

---

## [68] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can you suggest a tool under Variant Analysis that specifically handles inserting indel qualities?

**ASSISTANT:** The tool 'Insert indel qualities' is listed under the Variant Analysis category for this purpose.

---

## [69] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am looking for a motif annotation resource that provides a large collection of annotated position frequency matrices from multiple public databases for different organisms. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend MotifDb. It provides over 9,900 annotated position frequency matrices curated from 14 public sources across multiple organisms.

---

## [70] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** In our epigenome-wide association study, our samples contain heterogeneous cell types, meaning the measured signals reflect a mixture of cell profiles. Which tool is tailored for association detection in such mixed EWAS data?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use HIREewas. It is specifically designed for epigenomics and differential methylation analyses in epigenome-wide association studies where measured signals are a mixture of methylation profiles from different cell types.

---

## [71] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am developing tools within the gDR ecosystem and need a utility package that manages default platform constants and handles data structure validation and fitting. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `gDRutils`. It is the dedicated infrastructure package within the gDR platform containing utility functions for fitting data, data manipulation, data structure conversion and validation, along with default constants.

---

## [72] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a metagenomic assembly project where we did Sanger end sequencing of fosmids. I ran blastN to match reads against the assembled contigs. What tool can I use to link the assembled fosmids back to their putative clones?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the Sanger Ends Attacher tool. It is specifically designed for genomics and metagenomics projects with fosmid libraries, parsing blastN output files by sequence similarity to assign assembled fosmids to their putative clones.

---

## [73] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have chromatogram trace files from single-pass Sanger sequencing validation. Which tool can I use to perform basecalling and export the primary sequence to FASTA or FASTQ?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `tracy Basecall`. It is designed for genomics and Sanger sequencing validation workflows to basecall chromatogram trace files and output the primary sequence (representing the highest peak) in FASTA or FASTQ format.

---

## [74] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have massively parallel sequencing data from WGS and RNA-seq experiments and need a tool for variant detection from pileup format. What can I use?

**ASSISTANT:** VarScan pileup is designed for variant detection across massively parallel sequencing data, including WGS, exome, and transcriptome sequencing.

---

**USER:** I need a tool based on the goProfiles R package to conduct Gene Ontology functional profiling and enrichment on my protein list. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend the tool `Compute functional profiles`. It relies on the `goProfiles` R package to perform statistical analysis and functional profiling based on Gene Ontology.

---

## [76] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have several lists of oligo IDs from microarray experiments and want to describe and compare them using Gene Ontology terms. What tool can assist with this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use 'goTools'. It provides wrapper functions specifically designed for the description and comparison of oligo ID lists using the Gene Ontology database.

---

## [77] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to publish and maintain an R/Bioconductor package as an AnVIL workspace. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'AnVILPublish', which is designed to create or update AnVIL workspaces from resources such as R / Bioconductor packages.

---

## [78] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have identified peptides and proteins from a mass spectrometry experiment and want to export them into GFF format for genome browser visualization. Which tool can do this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'Proteomics to GFF' tool, which is designed to export identified peptides and proteins directly into GFF format.

---

## [79] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on transcriptomics research and need an R-based tool to optimize gene expression while taking into account both codon usage bias and mRNA secondary structure. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use GeneGA. It is an R-based genetic algorithm tool designed specifically for gene expression optimization by considering both mRNA secondary structure and codon usage bias, containing information on highly expressed genes across nearly 200 genomes.

---

## [80] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a bioinformatics package to model association between multiple genotypes and multiple traits while incorporating clinical pathways and biological knowledge as latent variables. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend ASGSCA. It provides tools to model and test associations between multiple genotypes and multiple traits using Generalized Structured Equation Models, incorporating genes and clinical pathways directly as latent variables.

---

## [81] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am preparing a fluorescence microscopy preprocessing pipeline to segment cells. Which tool will automatically threshold the entire image histogram into black and white?

**ASSISTANT:** Use the 'Convert to binary' tool. It is suited for fluorescence microscopy and cell segmentation preprocessing, automatically determining the threshold across the image histogram to output a binary mask.

---

## [82] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool can I use to detect positive selection across branches or specific codons given a sequence alignment and phylogenetic tree?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `codeML`. It specifically finds positive selection within branches or codons within a phylogenetic tree and a set of sequences.

---

## [83] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool to perform tensor decomposition-based unsupervised feature extraction specifically on single-cell transcriptomics data. What tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `TDbasedUFEadv`. It is an advanced version of `TDbasedUFE` designed for tensor decomposition-based unsupervised feature extraction and lists single-cell transcriptomics and transcriptomics as its primary applicable omics.

---

## [84] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on epigenomics sequence classification using support vector machines. Which Bioconductor package powers the ledpred workflow?

**ASSISTANT:** The workflow is powered by the `bioconductor/ledpred` package, which provides tools to train SVM models on genomic regions and regulatory features, perform parameter tuning and feature selection, and score test sequences.

---

## [85] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an annotation file with multiple transcript isoforms per gene and I need to keep only the longest isoform for each gene. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Agat Spkeeplongestisoform'. It filters GFF records specifically to retain only the longest isoform per gene.

---

---

## [87] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on an image processing task and need a tool within the Imaging domain to smooth an image while preserving edges via anisotropic diffusion. Which tool is available?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Apply anisotropic diffusion' tool from the Imaging domain.

---

## [88] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a generic list of gene identifiers from zebrafish that I need to annotate using Bioconductor org.db packages. Which tool can I use?

## [89] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to visualize topologically associating domains (TADs) across a specific locus and overlay ChIP-seq, RNA-seq, and TAD separation scores on the same plot. What tool can do this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `hicPlotTADs`. It is a visualization tool tailored to plot topologically associating domains in a designated genomic region and supports adding additional tracks such as gene models, ChIP-seq, RNA-seq, and TAD separation scores.

## [90] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a glycomics study focused on comparative glycan structure analysis and motif discovery. Is there a tool that provides MCAW alignment diagrams and pseudo-KCF probability files?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use `GAP Align Glycans`. It is tailored for glycomics applications such as comparative glycan structure analysis, motif discovery, and multiple glycan sequence alignment, generating MCAW alignment outputs and PKCF files.

---

## [91] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have genomic FASTA files containing lower-case masked regions and need to convert this masking data into a format compatible with makeblastdb. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'NCBI BLAST+ convert2blastmask'. It converts masking information present in lower-case masked FASTA inputs into mask file formats suitable for makeblastdb.

---

## [92] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have coverage data from several normal control samples and need to build a baseline profile for downstream copy number variant (CNV) calling. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Cnvkit Reference`. It is designed specifically to compile a coverage reference profile from a set of normal control sample files for CNV analysis.

---

## [93] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need an assembly tool to combine multiple subreads from individual SMRTbell molecules to generate high-accuracy consensus sequences. What tool is designed for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `CCS` (Circular Consensus Sequencing). It uses a statistical model to combine multiple subreads of the same SMRTbell molecule into a single highly accurate consensus sequence (HiFi read) along with base quality values.

---

## [94] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a multi-sample 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing project and need to aggregate OTU counts across all samples into a single matrix. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Make multisample OTUtable' tool from the Metagenomics suite, which is specifically designed for multi-sample comparative studies and community composition analyses.

---

## [95] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am browsing the Galaxy tools repository for Data Managers and saw iPHoP. Which category does this tool belong to?

**ASSISTANT:** The tool `iPHoP` is categorized under `Data Managers`.

---

## [96] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have identified several hundred ChIP-seq peak intervals in a BED file and need a way to automate taking snapshots of these loci in the Integrative Genomics Viewer (IGV). Is there a tool specifically designed to generate an IGV batch script for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `bedtools BED to IGV`. It takes genomic intervals from BED, bedGraph, GFF, VCF, or EncodePeak files and automatically generates an IGV batch script to capture snapshot images across all defined loci.

---

## [97] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am looking for a bioinformatics statistical method that can perform adaptively weighted Fisher's combined probability tests and compute a variability index across datasets. What tool fits this requirement?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use AWFisher. It is specifically designed to implement the adaptively weighted Fisher's method, featuring fast p-value calculation, variability index estimation, and meta-pattern analysis.

---

## [98] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have sequence files from our sequencing run and need to convert them into a tabular format so my team can inspect them in R and spreadsheet software. What command-line tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `obitab` command from the OBITools suite. It converts sequence files into a tabular file format suitable for opening in spreadsheet programs or R.

---

## [99] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have assembled whole genomes of Helicobacter pylori isolates and need to classify them genetically relative to global ancestral populations. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `GrafGen`. It is specifically designed to classify *Helicobacter pylori* genomes according to genetic distance from nine reference populations.

---

## [100] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to perform clinical pharmacogenomic interpretation of individual patient genotypes according to CPIC guidelines. Which tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend pharmCAT (Variant Analysis). It is designed for pharmacogenomics and clinical genomics, specifically for CPIC guideline-based variant interpretation of individual patient genotypes.

---

## [101] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a bioinformatics tool for de novo prediction of transcription factor binding consensus using a heuristic search approach. What would you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend BCRANK. It provides functions and classes specifically designed for de novo prediction of transcription factor binding consensus through heuristic search.

---

## [102] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to visualize alternative splicing junctions across different RNA-seq conditions directly from my aligned BAM files. What tool do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using the Sashimi plot tool, which generates sashimi plots from BAM files using ggsashimi to visualize splicing events in transcriptomics/RNA-seq data.

---

## [103] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on comparative genomics and sequence alignment analysis across species and need to analyze coverage from a MAF file. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `mafCoverage`, a sequence analysis tool specifically designed for comparative genomics to analyze genome-wide coverage from MAF files sorted by chromosome and tStart.

---

## [104] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are processing DIA mass spectrometry runs generated by OpenSWATH and need to score and perform semi-supervised learning across merged outputs. What tool should we deploy?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'PyProphet merge'. It provides semi-supervised learning and scoring functionality for OpenSWATH results within proteomics and mass spectrometry workflows.

---

## [105] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am setting up a targeted proteomics pipeline from a DDA experiment and need to generate an initial seed list for feature detection. Which tool fits this requirement?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `SeedListGenerator`. It is designed for proteomics workflows, specifically data-dependent acquisition (DDA) experiments and targeted proteomics requiring feature detection initialization.

---

## [106] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a 16S rRNA microbial community profiling project and need to design OTU-specific primers for targeted amplification. Is there a metagenomics tool available for this?

## [107] source=multitask_sft_big

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'SomaticSignatures (Clustering, GenomicVariation, Sequencing)', which provides flexible matrix decomposition algorithms to identify SNV mutational signatures.

---

## [108] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am managing an organism record on Apollo for a genome re-annotation project. I need to completely clear out all the existing annotations before re-opening the genome. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Delete all annotations from an Apollo record'. It is specifically designed for genome re-annotation projects and build transfers to intentionally remove every single annotation associated with an organism.

## [109] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with ChIP-seq and ATAC-seq peak data and need to standardize peak regions into fixed-width windows centered on peak midpoints. Which tool should I use?
