---

## [110] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing RNA-seq alternative splicing and need a dedicated tool to merge or join reference and alternative splicing events. What tool is available for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'Join_ref_alt_splicing_events' tool under the RNA domain, which is specifically designed for joining reference and alternative splicing events.

---

## [111] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool in the variant analysis category provides Bayesian detection for small variants such as SNPs, indels, and complex events?

**ASSISTANT:** Freebayes4Workflow (FreeBayes) is recommended. It is a Bayesian genetic variant detector specifically built to call small polymorphisms including single-nucleotide polymorphisms (SNPs), insertions and deletions (indels), multi-nucleotide polymorphisms (MNPs), and complex events.

---

## [112] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a utility in the Convert Formats section to apply filtering rules on tabular matrix data. What tool fits this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use Matrix Filters, which is designated under the Convert Formats category for processing and filtering matrix data.

---

## [113] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to standardize my BAM files so that their contig ordering matches the exact contig order of our reference genome. What tool can accomplish this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use ReorderSam. It reorders reads in a SAM or BAM file to match the contig ordering of a specified reference file based on exact contig name matching, dropping reads mapped to any contigs not present in the new reference.

---

## [114] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I want to visualize genome-wide expression, CGH, or SNP data along chromosome strands as a heatmap to identify genes of interest. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use ChromHeatMap. It allows you to plot genome-wide data (such as expression, CGH, and SNP data) along each strand of a given chromosome as a heat map to interactively identify probes and genes of interest.

---

## [115] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with patient-derived xenograft (PDX) mouse models and need to assign reads between the primary genome and the host contamination genome to filter out mouse reads. What tool is designed for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Bamcmp' (Bam Compare). It is designed specifically for assigning reads between a primary genome and a contamination genome, such as filtering out mouse reads from patient-derived xenograft (PDX) models.

---

## [116] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have structural variant calls from genomic data and want to detect NUMT insertions. Which tool can identify NUMT events using nuclear-mitochondrial breakend junctions?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use svaNUMT. It is specialized for genomics datasets to detect NUMT events from structural variant calls in GRanges breakend notation by identifying nuclear-mitochondrial breakend junctions.

---

## [117] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a statistical tool in bioinformatics specifically designed for analyzing phenotypic data. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend PhenStat. It is a software package specifically containing methods for the statistical analysis of phenotypic data.

---

## [118] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool to generate boilerplate custom Python scripts for my QIIME microbiome workflow rather than running data analysis directly. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `make_qiime_py_file`. It is specifically designed for metagenomics applications such as QIIME workflow development and custom script generation for microbiome analysis, though it is not suitable for direct data analysis.

---

## [119] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to exclude specific individuals from my PLINK2 population genomics dataset. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Plink2 Remove', which is designed to remove specific samples from a plink2 dataset in population genomics analyses.

---

## [120] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on read filtering before genome assembly and need a tool capable of counting k-mers efficiently in whole-genome sequencing reads. What tool fits this use case?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend KMC filter (K-mer Counter) from the Sequence Analysis category. It is specifically designed for counting k-mers (sequences of consecutive k symbols) in sequencing reads and is well suited for read filtering before assembly and whole-genome sequencing quality control.

---

## [121] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on microbial ecology metagenomics data using the Mothur environment and need a command to filter out specific sample groups. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the Mothur tool `Remove.groups`, which is designed within the Mothur microbial ecology suite for metagenomic workflows to remove specified groups.

---

## [122] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have Oxford Nanopore long-read sequencing data and need a tool to generate a consensus sequence. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `medaka consensus pipeline`. It is a tool suite in the Sequence Analysis category specifically designed to create a consensus sequence from nanopore sequencing data.

---

## [123] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with 3D constructive solid geometry models and need to shift the spatial position of a shape by displacing its coordinates. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Translate shape' tool from Constructive Solid Geometry, which applies translation operations to a shape by adding a displacement to each coordinate.

---

## [124] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on transcriptomic hypothesis testing and need a tool that conducts weighted p-value multiple testing while calculating ranking probabilities and test weights. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend OPWeight. It performs weighted p-value based multiple hypothesis testing and computes ranking probabilities, weights, and significance metrics using a probabilistic relationship framework suitable for genomics and transcriptomics.

---

## [125] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with legacy Illumina 1.5 FASTQ reads that use Phred+64 encoding, but Bowtie requires Sanger Phred+33 format. How can I convert the quality scores?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `Convert Fastq qualities` tool. It converts FASTQ quality scores encoded in Illumina 1.3–1.7 format (Phred+64) to Sanger format (Phred+33) so they can be processed by Bowtie and other Galaxy tools.

---

## [126] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have sets of genomic regions from a ChIP-seq experiment and want to run automated enrichment analysis by testing their overlap against public and custom genomic ranges databases. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use LOLA. It is specifically designed for functional genomics and gene regulation analyses to test the overlap of genomic region sets with public and custom region set databases, enabling automated enrichment analysis.

---

## [127] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with patient-derived xenograft data and need to build a fast, lightweight index from my reference FASTA files to sort host and graft reads. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Xengsort Index'. It provides fast, lightweight, and accurate indexing of reference FASTA files specifically designed for xenograft sorting.

---

## [128] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have RNA-seq alignment data, but I do not know the strandedness configuration of the library preparation. What tool can determine this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use Infer Experiment. It evaluates mapping information against a gene model to speculate on library configuration and determine whether the data is stranded and which strand orientation was used.

---

## [129] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool is suited for single-nucleotide resolution CLIP-seq, iCLIP, or eCLIP data analysis that supports HMM parameter learning across reference contigs?

**ASSISTANT:** **PureCLIP** is designed for RNA-protein interaction mapping, including CLIP-seq, iCLIP, and eCLIP analysis at single-nucleotide resolution. It learns HMM parameters across reference contigs or a specified subset of chromosomes.

---

## [130] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to import local BAM files and chromosome info files into my workflow environment to run SVDetect. Which tool is intended for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Import data' tool under Variant Analysis. It is specifically designed to quickly import a BAM file, a chromosome info file, or an SVDetect output file from your computer to serve as inputs for SVDetect.

---

## [131] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Is there a dedicated pharmacology tool available to query information on FDA drug recalls?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use the `check_fda_drug_recalls` tool, which is designed for pharmacology workflows to check FDA drug recall records.

---

## [132] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have multiple contigs from candidate long-read assemblies and want to cluster them while filtering out incomplete or spurious contigs. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Trycycler cluster. It carries out complete-linkage clustering of contig sequences based on Mash distance and is specifically designed to exclude spurious, incomplete, or badly misassembled contigs.

---

## [133] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a physiology study and need a tool to fit a mono-exponential pharmacokinetic model to my concentration-time data. What tool is available?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the tool `mono_exp_model`, which is designed for physiology applications to implement a mono-exponential pharmacokinetic model.

---

## [134] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I generated a de novo assembly using miniasm from long-read sequencing data. Because miniasm does not polish, the consensus has a high error rate. What tool can I use specifically to polish miniasm assemblies?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `minipolish`. Miniasm builds assemblies rapidly by stitching together long reads without an internal polishing stage, leading to elevated error rates. `minipolish` is designed specifically to polish miniasm-based long-read assemblies.

---

## [135] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with RNA-seq data and need a tool to assess genomic DNA contamination and check whether our library protocol was stranded. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using gDNAx. It is designed for genomics and transcriptomics to provide diagnostics and diagnostic plots for evaluating genomic DNA contamination in RNA-seq data, as well as providing insight into the library protocol and strandedness.

---

## [136] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with Affymetrix SNP6.0 array data and need a tool to preprocess and interactively visualize genomic copy number profiles. What tool would you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `rCGH`. It is built specifically for copy number variation analysis and preprocessing, supporting Affymetrix SNP6.0 array data and offering interactive visualization of genomic profiles.

---

## [137] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with Whole Genome Bisulfite Sequencing (WGBS) data and need an alignment tool in the Next Gen Mappers category that aligns in silico converted reads to an in silico converted genome. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `bwameth` (BWA-meth). It is a Next Gen Mapper designed for bisulfite sequencing alignments (such as WGBS and RRBS) that performs in silico conversion of both the reads and the reference genome prior to alignment.

---

## [138] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Is there an interactive Shiny tool in Bioconductor that allows me to search and inspect AnnotationHub and ExperimentHub data across multiple organisms, and also gives me reproducible code to download them?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, `BiocHubsShiny` provides an interactive Shiny interface to explore AnnotationHub and ExperimentHub resources. It displays resources across multiple organisms using DT/DataTable and generates template code for reproducible downloading.

---

## [139] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a command-line tool to quickly inspect the nucleotide composition of my FASTA/FASTQ sequence files before running downstream genomics workflows. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `seqtk_comp`. It reports the nucleotide composition of FASTA and FASTQ sequences and is designed for sequence quality assessment and nucleotide composition profiling in genomics and metagenomics projects.

---

## [140] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a fast QC tool to generate summary reports for Oxford Nanopore sequencing reads. What tool do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend nanoq (nf-core), an ultra-fast quality control tool designed to produce summary reports specifically for nanopore reads.

---

## [141] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have methylation data and need an analysis package that can generate a comprehensive quality control report. Which tool fits this requirement?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `scmeth`. While some of its functions are specifically tailored for single-cell methylation data, most functions can be applied to any methylation data, and a primary highlight of the workflow is its comprehensive quality control report.

---

## [142] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with raw transcriptomic qPCR data and need a tool to perform normalization and generate diagnostic plots. What tool would you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend qpcrNorm. It is designed for transcriptomics data to normalize high-throughput qPCR experiments, process raw Ct values, and generate diagnostic plots.

---

## [143] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool to filter or convert alignment files based on read groups across SAM, BAM, or CRAM formats. What would you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using `Samtools Getrg`. It is designed to filter and convert SAM, BAM, and CRAM alignment files.

---

## [144] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have completed an RNA-seq differential expression analysis and obtained a list of significant genes. I need a free, peer-reviewed, open-source tool to visualize and analyze biological pathways. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend Reactome. It is a free, open-source, curated, and peer-reviewed pathway database designed to provide bioinformatics tools for the visualization, interpretation, and analysis of pathway knowledge, particularly for differential expression gene lists.

---

## [145] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on biological sequence filtering. Is the Constructive Solid Geometry tool appropriate for this?

**ASSISTANT:** No, it is not appropriate. According to its applicability profile, it is a computational geometry tool that coarsens 3D shapes and is not designed for biological sequence data or any primary omics domain.

---

## [146] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have bisulfite sequencing reads from a WGBS experiment and need to evaluate or filter reads based on bisulfite conversion efficiency. Which tool handles this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `Biscuit Bsconv`. It is designed for bisulfite sequencing data (such as WGBS and scWGBS) to summarize and/or filter reads based on their bisulfite conversion rate.

---

## [147] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with massive chemical libraries in metabolomics and need a tool designed for accelerated structure similarity searching of very large small molecule data sets. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `eiR`. It provides specialized utilities for accelerated structure similarity searching of very large small molecule data sets by utilizing an embedding and indexing approach.

---

## [148] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an initial 3D reconstruction in my cryo-EM project, but I need to refine per-particle defocus values and correct for beam-tilt to push the resolution further. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Ctf Refinement' tool. It is specifically designed in cryo-EM workflows to refine per-particle defocus values, beam-tilt, and higher-order aberrations to improve map resolution.

---

## [149] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are analyzing 3C-seq/Hi-C data across multiple conditions and need to filter out viewpoints that have low sparsity across our matrices. What tool fits this requirement?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `chicQualityControl`. It assesses viewpoint quality based on sparsity thresholds across chromosome conformation capture datasets like 3C-seq and Hi-C, dropping viewpoints that fall below the threshold in any provided matrix.

---

## [150] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have de novo assembled ancient DNA contigs and need to estimate post-mortem damage parameters. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Pydamage Analyze. It is designed specifically for damage parameter estimation in ancient DNA (aDNA), de novo assemblies, and contig filtering.

---

## [151] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We just sequenced a CRISPR-Cas9 targeted mutagenesis experiment and want to analyze the spectrum of variant alleles in our target region. Which tool is suitable?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `CrispRVariants`. It provides specialized tools for analyzing CRISPR-Cas9 mutagenesis sequencing experiments and allows users to localize and evaluate variant allele combinations within a targeted region.

---

## [152] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are studying differential DNA methylation and expression in cancer to find hypermethylated and hypomethylated genes compared to healthy tissue using a mixture modeling approach. What tool should we apply?

**ASSISTANT:** You should apply MethylMix. It is implemented specifically to identify hyper- and hypomethylated genes in disease by fitting a beta mixture model to DNA methylation states and comparing them with normal baseline states.

---

## [153] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a plant genomics study with small RNA-seq data and want to discover novel miRNA genes by integrating deep sequencing reads with genomic features. Which tool is tailored for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the PIPmiR PIPELINE tool. It is specifically designed for novel plant miRNA gene discovery by combining deep sequencing data with plant genomic features.

---

## [154] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can I use Tesseract for processing transcriptomic expression matrices or variant calling?

**ASSISTANT:** No. Tesseract is not applicable to omics research; it is an Optical Character Recognition (OCR) engine designed for text extraction.

---

## [155] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We have breast cancer microarray gene expression datasets across several platforms and need to perform gene mapping and molecular subtype classification. What tool fits this task?

**ASSISTANT:** The R package 'genefu' is recommended. It is tailored for breast cancer transcriptomics and includes functions for gene mapping between different microarray platforms, identification of molecular subtypes, and implementation of published prognostic signatures.

---

## [156] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with pooled population genomic sequencing data and have generated an MPILEUP file. Which tool provides a heuristic SNP calling algorithm against a FASTA reference genome?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `poolsnp`. It is a heuristic SNP caller developed for pooled sequencing datasets that uses an MPILEUP file and a FASTA reference genome to call variants.

---

## [157] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on viral sequencing and need to merge matching reads into clusters for sequence deduplication prior to downstream analysis. What tool fits this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Merge matching reads', which clusters matching reads using the Tamura Nei 93 distance algorithm and is designed for virology/viral sequencing, metagenomics, and sequence clustering workflows requiring deduplication.

---

## [158] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have comparative metagenomic sequencing data from two distinct sampling locations and want to determine whether the abundance of reads mapped to a specific taxon differs significantly between the two sites. What statistical tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the Poisson two-sample test. It is specifically designed for metagenomic, 16S rRNA gene sequencing, and metabarcoding studies to test whether the number of reads mapping to a particular taxon in location 1 differs significantly from location 2.

---

## [159] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are studying post-transcriptional regulation from RNA-Seq data and want to discover novel alternative polyadenylation sites as well as quantify differential APA usage. What tool fits this task?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `InPAS`. It facilitates the discovery of novel alternative polyadenylation (APA) sites and evaluates differential usage of APA sites directly from RNA-Seq data.

---

## [160] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an RNA-seq expression matrix in TSV format and want to compute PCA, t-SNE, and hierarchical clustering on principal components. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Generate PCA, tSNE and HCPC' tool in the Transcriptomics domain, which takes a TSV of RNAseq libraries (columns) and genes (rows) to generate PCA, tSNE, and HCPC analyses.

---

## [161] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with single-cell transcriptomics data and need a way to easily build and combine initial configuration objects before launching iSEE(). What tool can help?

**ASSISTANT:** iSEEfier is designed for this purpose. It provides functionality to quickly and intuitively create, inspect, and combine initial configuration objects that can be directly passed to launch iSEE() with the specified configuration.

---

## [162] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a Systems Biology project and need a tool to identify both tRNA and tmRNA sequences. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'tRNA and tmRNA prediction' tool, which falls under the Systems Biology domain and is designed specifically for predicting tRNA and tmRNA.

---

## [163] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have mapped sequencing data in BAM format and need to compute genotype likelihoods without hard-calling genotypes first. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Angsd Gl`, which is specifically designed to calculate genotype likelihoods directly from BAM files in genomics workflows.

---

## [164] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have two sets of genomic intervals—one containing genes of interest and the other containing regulatory elements. Which tool will allow me to identify the regulatory elements closest to each gene?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `get flanking`. It identifies and prints elements from the second set of genomic intervals that are closest to (flanking) elements in the first set.

---

## [165] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which variant caller can handle mpileup data across multiple sequencing platforms like Illumina, SOLiD, and Roche/454?

**ASSISTANT:** VarScan (specifically VarScan mpileup / VarScan 2) is suitable for this. It is a platform-independent mutation caller compatible with data generated on Illumina, SOLiD, Life/PGM, Roche/454, and similar instruments.

---

## [166] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool should I use to import data directly from the online tRNAdb or mtRNAdb databases into R/Bioconductor?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `tRNAdbImport`, which is tailored to fetch and import records from tRNAdb and mtRNAdb into a Bioconductor `GRanges` format.

---

## [167] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a comparative genomics project and need a tool to detect duplicated genes from whole-genome protein sequences and categorize their duplication mechanisms. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend doubletrouble. It is designed to identify duplicated genes from whole-genome protein sequences and classify them according to their duplication modes.

---

## [168] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need an R-based framework for designing and manipulating CRISPR guide RNAs (gRNAs) across functional genomics projects. What suite should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `crisprVerse`. It is a modular ecosystem of R packages created specifically for the design and manipulation of CRISPR guide RNAs.

---

## [169] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am looking for a sequence analysis tool to handle filtering and trimming preprocessing on genomics and transcriptomics read sets. What tool covers these tasks?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use 'skipseq', a sequence analysis tool applicable to genomics and transcriptomics for quality control preprocessing, sequence filtering, and sequence dataset trimming.

---

## [170] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing data in Mothur and need a tool to calculate alpha diversity metrics and rarefaction curves for individual samples. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the Mothur tool **Collect.single**, which is designed for alpha diversity assessment and rarefaction curve generation in individual microbial samples.

---

## [171] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a time-series imaging experiment in computational pathology and need to export an animated GIF from a series of images. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use pcdl_make_gif. It is tailored for spatial biology and computational pathology to generate a GIF movie from all image files found in a data collection.

---

## [172] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can you recommend a tool in the Metagenomics suite specifically for merging individual Bracken output files?

**ASSISTANT:** The recommended tool is 'Combine Bracken Outputs'.

---

## [173] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We have paired-end RNA-Seq FASTQ reads from cancer patients and need to determine their HLA class I and class II genotypes for an immunotherapy stratification study. What tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend 'seq2HLA'. It is an in-silico method designed for transcriptomics and immunogenomics that takes standard paired-end RNA-Seq reads in FASTQ format, aligns them to an HLA allele Bowtie index, and outputs predicted HLA class I and class II genotypes.

---

## [174] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to find alternative medications for a patient that will not cause adverse drug interactions with their contraindicated drugs. Which tool can help?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Biomni: Find Alternative Drugs Ddinter`. It is designed specifically to find alternative drugs that do not interact with contraindicated medications.

---

## [175] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have amplicon sequencing data in FASTQ format and need a tool from the vsearch suite to perform quality filtering and convert my reads to FASTA format. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Vsearch Fastqfilter` (`vsearch/fastqfilter`). It is specifically designed for amplicon sequences and metagenomics to perform quality filtering and/or convert FASTQ files to FASTA format.

---

## [176] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have histological imaging data of thrombus sections stained with H&E. Which pathology tool can I use to quantify features in these samples?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend 'Biomni: Analyze Thrombus Histology', a pathology tool specifically designed to analyze and quantify histological images of thrombus samples stained with H&E.

---

## [177] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which Bioconductor packages power the TargetSearch workflow for targeted GC-MS processing?

**ASSISTANT:** The TargetSearch workflow is powered by `bioconductor/targetsearch` and its companion dataset package `bioconductor/targetsearchdata`.

---

## [178] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a large set of protein sequences and need to reduce redundancy and cluster protein families. What tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'CD-HIT PROTEIN'. It is designed for proteomics and protein sequence analysis, specifically for protein sequence redundancy reduction, protein family clustering, and building non-redundant databases.

---

## [179] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We collected LC-MS data in data-dependent acquisition (DDA) mode with centroided peaks for a proteomics experiment. What OpenMS tool should we use to detect LC-MS features?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **FeatureFinderCentroided** from OpenMS (TOPP). It is specifically tailored for feature detection in centroided LC-MS data from proteomics and metabolomics experiments.

---

## [180] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an mzML file from an untargeted metabolomics study and need to generate an extracted ion chromatogram (EIC) for a specific m/z value. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `xcms plot eic`. It plots the extracted ion chromatogram (EIC) from an mzML file for a designated m/z value, operating by default at MS level 1 with a 10 ppm tolerance.

---

## [181] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have mapped Ribo-Seq alignments in a BAM file and want to quantify reads mapped to CDS and UTRs while also checking codon frequency. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the RiboProfiling package. It takes a BAM file as input, performs read start position recalibration, quantifies read counts on the CDS, 5'UTR, and 3'UTR, and generates diagnostic plots including codon frequency, coverage assessment, count pairs, and log fold-change.

---

## [182] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool for biological sequence manipulation. Would OpenAlex explorer work?

**ASSISTANT:** No, OpenAlex explorer is not suitable for biological sequence or molecular data processing. It is designed for text manipulation rather than biological sequence analysis.

---

## [183] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with transcriptomic exon array data and need a modeling strategy to normalize the data by accounting for biological and study-specific variables. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `snm` (Supervised Normalization of Microarrays). It is a modeling strategy designed for normalizing high-throughput genomic data, specifically transcriptomics, based on the premise that data is a function of study-specific biological and technical variables.

---

## [184] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a functional genomics tool that can perform enrichment analysis through services like DAVID or GeneTerm and build/visualize functional gene and term networks from clustered enrichment analyses. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `FGNet`. It is designed to perform functional enrichment analyses through providers like DAVID and GeneTerm, and it builds and visualizes functional gene and term networks from clustering across multiple annotation spaces.

---

## [185] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a comparative genomics project and need a tool to import genomic annotation data into a database so it can be viewed interactively in GeneNoteBook viewer. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Build a GeneNoteBook'. It loads genomic annotation and curation data into a MongoDB database, producing a dataset ready for interactive visualization with the 'GeneNoteBook viewer'.

---

## [186] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are sequencing formalin-fixed paraffin-embedded (FFPE) tissue samples and are concerned about false-positive structural variant calls caused by artifactual chimeras. Is there a tool specifically for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use FilterFFPE. It is specifically designed to find and filter artificial chimeric reads generated during the NGS process of FFPE tissues, preventing false-positive structural variant detections.

---

## [187] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing Affymetrix exon array and Human Transcriptome Array 2.0 data for alternative splicing, and I want to account for probe-level measurement uncertainty using Bayesian methods. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `puma`. It supports AlternativeSplicing and Bayesian modeling across Affymetrix GeneChip data (traditional 3' arrays, exon arrays, and Human Transcriptome Array 2.0) by propagating expression uncertainty to downstream analyses.

---

## [188] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I obtained initial gene annotations using Maker and now need to train the ab-initio gene predictor Augustus. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** Use the tool `Train Augustus` (under Sequence Analysis). It is designed specifically to train the Augustus ab-initio gene predictor based on a prior prediction, such as one generated by Maker.

---

## [189] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have bacterial genome sequences and need to identify antibiotic resistance genes. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Rgi Main`. It is specifically designed for bacteria to predict antibiotic resistance from FASTA sequence inputs.

---

## [190] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working in OpenMS and need a search engine adapter to search my mzML spectra against a FASTA database using Comet. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Openmsthirdparty Cometadapter`. It functions within OpenMS to annotate MS/MS spectra using the Comet search engine.

---

## [191] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have gridded environmental and climate NetCDF datasets and need a tool to slice and extract variable values using custom dimension filters. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'NetCDF xarray Selection' from the Ecology domain, which is built to extract variable values under custom dimension conditions on multidimensional climate and geospatial data.

---

## [192] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Are there any tools registered under the 'Combinatorial Selections' classification in the registry?

**ASSISTANT:** `Selenzyme` is registered under 'Combinatorial Selections', though its catalog entry currently contains minimal metadata.

---

## [193] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing data from two clinical cohorts (healthy vs. diseased) and want to test for differentially abundant taxa while adjusting for compositional bias. Which QIIME 2 tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `qiime2 composition ancombc`. It implements Analysis of Compositions of Microbiomes with Bias Correction (ANCOM-BC), specifically designed for 16S rRNA gene amplicon and metagenomic data to identify differentially abundant features across categorical groups while correcting for sampling fractions and compositional bias.

---

## [194] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with Resolve Biosciences imaging data in a spatial transcriptomics project and need to detect duplicate spots near grid borders. What tool fits this purpose?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Mindagap Duplicatefinder`. It is tailored for spatial transcriptomics imaging (specifically Resolve Biosciences data) to mark duplicate spots along gridline edges.

---

## [195] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool in the Sequence Analysis category to split my sequence files into smaller subsets. What tool is available?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'splitter' tool, which is cataloged under the Sequence Analysis category for splitting sequence files.

---

## [196] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am running a metagenomics study and need a tool to calculate Fisher's alpha diversity index. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend the `Vegan Fisher Alpha` tool, available under the Metagenomics category.

---

## [197] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to build an index from a FASTA reference genome so I can align reads using BWA-mem2. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Bwamem2 Index`, which creates a BWA-mem2 index for a reference genome in FASTA format.

---

## [198] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on ChIP-seq data involving histone modification profiling with treatment and control samples. What tool should I use for peak analysis?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use SPAN (Semi-supervised Peak Analyzer), which is specifically designed for ChIP-seq analysis including histone modification profiling and experiments with treatment and control samples.

---

## [199] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have phylogenetic tree data from a taxonomic study and want to generate a publication-ready circular tree diagram with custom annotations. What tool do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend 'Generation, personalization and annotation of tree' (GraPhlAn). It specializes in concise, informative, and publication-ready circular representations of phylogenetic and taxonomic trees.

---

## [200] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have PacBio HiFi reads and need a fast assembler capable of resolving haplotypes for a large genome. What tool do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend Hifiasm. It is a fast haplotype-resolved de novo assembler designed for PacBio HiFi reads that can assemble a human genome in several hours and can handle extremely complex genomes such as the California redwood.

---

## [201] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with count data from single-cell RNA-seq and ChIP-seq experiments and need an algorithm for count normalization, transformation, and batch effect management. What tool fits this?

**ASSISTANT:** Linnorm is designed for normalizing and transforming RNA-seq, single-cell RNA-seq, ChIP-seq count data, or any large-scale count data, and handles clustering and batch effect analysis.

---

## [202] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Is there a tool named gviewcds available for sequence analysis workflows?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, 'gviewcds' is available under the Sequence Analysis tool category.

---

## [203] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have trained a classification model on 16S amplicon sequencing data using QIIME 2 and now have a new feature table of unseen samples. Which tool should I use to predict their classes?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `qiime2 sample-classifier predict-classification` to apply your trained estimator and predict target classes for the new samples in your feature table.

---

## [204] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I'm running an untargeted LC-MS metabolomics experiment with multiple sample classes and need to optimize retention time correction and peak grouping parameters in XCMS. Which tool can automate this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'IPO for group and retcor'. It provides automated optimization of XCMS parameters specifically for peak grouping (`group`) and retention time correction (`retcor`).

---

## [205] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on an immuno-oncology RNA-Seq study comparing two conditions (group A vs group B) and want a package that fits a Bayesian hierarchical model to count data to obtain posterior probabilities. What tool do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **BADER**. It is categorized under DifferentialExpression, ImmunoOncology, and RNASeq, and is designed specifically to fit a Bayesian hierarchical model to RNA-seq counts and return posterior probabilities of differential expression between two groups.

---

## [206] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am an R package maintainer and need to update older serialized S4 instances included in my package. Which tool should I look into?

**ASSISTANT:** You should consider the `updateObject` package. It provides a set of tools built around `updateObject()` specifically designed for package maintainers working with and updating old serialized S4 instances.

---

## [207] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing a metagenomic fosmid library and need to generate separate reports describing the PFAM domains annotated to each protein for each fosmid. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `PFAM output annotator`. It produces synthetic reports in FASTA format detailing the PFAM domains annotated to each protein, generating a separate report for each fosmid.

---

## [208] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a genome-wide nucleosome mapping experiment and need to assess sequence periodicity across the genome by calculating phase distributions. Which tool is suitable?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **Make phasogram**. It is designed for genomics, epigenomics, and nucleosome positioning studies to calculate the phase distribution of sequencing data and identify genome-wide periodicities.

---

## [209] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on peak calling and downstream annotation within an ATAC-seq and ChIP-seq pipeline. Which HOMER utility converts peak position formats into standard BED format?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `homer_pos2bed`, which is tailored for NGS sequence analysis pipelines (including ChIP-seq and ATAC-seq peak workflows) to handle peak position conversions to BED format.

---

## [210] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I just completed a de novo assembly and need to evaluate the gross features and get summary statistics of my contigs. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `assemblystats`. It is designed to generate summary statistics for an assembly or a set of reads, typically run after assembly to assess gross features.

---

## [211] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which genomics tool can I use to carry out a step-by-step methylation analysis on Next Generation Sequencing (NGS) data?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use 'MethTargetedNGS', a genomics tool categorized under Alignment, DataImport, and Genetics that performs step-by-step methylation analysis of Next Generation Sequencing data.

---

## [212] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have short reads generated from a CRISPR/shRNA pooled screen and need an efficient tool to align them against our reference barcode library while accounting for sequencing errors. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend bcSeq. It is an Rcpp-based package with optimized data structures and algorithms specifically designed to align short reads from CRISPR or shRNA screens to a reference barcode library while considering sequencing errors and evaluating matching qualities.

---

## [213] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We have cross-link mass spectrometry data from an experiment probing RNA-protein interactions. Which tool is tailored for searching RNA-peptide cross-links?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use RNPxlSearch. It is designed for proteomics, specifically RNA-protein interaction proteomics and cross-link mass spectrometry experiments (including RNA-peptide and DNA-peptide cross-linking).

---

## [214] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a plant genome annotation project and need a tool dedicated to annotating Short Interspersed Nuclear Elements (SINEs). What do you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `annosine` (AnnoSINE_v2). It is an nf-core tool designed specifically for SINE annotation in plant and animal genomes.

---

## [215] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I want to identify transcriptional regulatory modules using rTRM, but I prefer working with a graphical user interface rather than command line code. Is there a suitable tool?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use `rTRMui`. It provides a web-based GUI interface specifically designed to compute transcriptional regulatory modules with rTRM.

---

## [216] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have called variants from whole exome sequencing data and need to add contextual annotations to the calls. Which sequence analysis tool should I select?

**ASSISTANT:** You should select the 'Variant Annotator' tool. It annotates variant calls with context information and allows you to select specific annotations tailored to your study.

---

## [217] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have methylation data generated from the Sequenom MassArray platform and I need a software package for importing the data, running quality control, and designing primers with amplicon prediction. What do you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend the 'MassArray' package. It is specifically designed for the import, quality control, analysis, and visualization of methylation data from Sequenom's MassArray platform, and it includes detailed amplicon prediction tools for optimal assay design.

---

## [218] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with multiple omics datasets and need a tool to perform multiple co-inertia analysis to integrate and cluster them. What tool do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using 'omicade4'. This package performs multiple co-inertia analysis of omics datasets and is designed for classification, clustering, and multiple comparison analyses.

---

## [219] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have multiple proteomics and RNA-Seq datasets across different conditions and want to identify proteins that exhibit co-abundance patterns. Which tool is tailored for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `XINA`. It is designed for proteomics and transcriptomics analyses to detect proteins displaying similar co-abundance patterns within and across multiple experimental conditions.

---

## [220] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with time-series abundance data from regional ecological surveys and need to plot the abundance over time with an added trend line. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `Plot abundance` tool. It is specifically designed for ecology and biodiversity monitoring to plot abundance time series and add a trend line.

---

## [221] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on genomic variant interpretation and need a tool for protein-level variant annotation against GRCh37 via REST. What tool do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `VEP Rest`. It is designed for genomics and proteomics applications, specifically variant consequence prediction and protein-level variant annotation, querying the GRCh37 VEP REST web service to return protein consequences.

---

## [222] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to estimate differential gene expression from short read sequence count data. What tool should I consider, and what if I have paired data?

**ASSISTANT:** You should consider edgeR, which estimates differential gene expression for short read sequence counts using methods appropriate for count data. If you have paired data, you may also want to consider Tophat/Cufflinks.

---

## [223] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool can I use to perform haplogroup classification on mtDNA sequences?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Haplogrep2 Classify`. It is specifically designed for the classification of mitochondrial DNA (mtDNA) into haplogroups.

---

## [224] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have matched transcriptomic and metabolomic data and want to run a combined pathway enrichment analysis using databases like Biocarta, Reactome, and KEGG. What tool fits this task?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend multiGSEA. It extracts pathway features derived from 8 databases (including KEGG, Reactome, and Biocarta) across transcriptomic, proteomic, and/or metabolomic layers to calculate combined GSEA-based enrichment scores.

---

## [225] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have assembled metagenomic contigs from sequencing data and want a machine learning classifier to identify viral vs. host vs. bacterial contigs. Which tool provides this specific hybrid CNN and Random Forest approach?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `decontaminator` (VirHunter). It employs Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs) alongside a Random Forest Classifier specifically to classify pre-assembled contigs as viral, host, or bacterial.

---

## [226] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool from the HMMER suite can I use to reformat sequence files?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `Hmmer Eslreformat`, which is the sequence file reformatting tool provided in the HMMER ecosystem.

---

## [227] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have microarray expression data and need a software package capable of analyzing and masking problematic probe data. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use maskBAD, a bioinformatics software package designed specifically to analyze and mask microarray expression data.

---

## [228] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on de novo genome assembly using short-read sequencing data (Solexa/Illumina and 454) with the Velvet assembler. Is there an automated tool to optimize Velvet assembly parameters?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you should use `VelvetOptimiser`. It is specifically designed to optimize parameter settings for the Velvet de novo genomic assembler when working with short-read sequencing technologies such as Solexa and 454.

---

## [229] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a bioinformatics software tool that can produce high-resolution Venn and Vennpie plots while allowing me to extract subset details into a data frame. What would you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend VennDetail. It provides a set of functions designed specifically to generate high-resolution Venn and Vennpie plots, as well as extract and combine the details of these subsets with your datasets into data frames.

---

## [230] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool provides a toolbox for sparse contrastive principal component analysis on high-dimensional transcriptomics data?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use scPCA, a specialized toolbox for sparse contrastive principal component analysis (scPCA) applicable to high-dimensional biological data including transcriptomics and genomics.

---

## [231] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am planning to run somatic copy number analysis on tumor-normal paired samples using FACETS. What tool do I need to prepare the input data?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **SNP Pileup for FACETS**. It serves as an optimized wrapper for the `snp-pileup` program, generating the detailed allele counts file required as input for the FACETS Analysis tool.

---

## [232] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a synthetic biology project where we need to process sequencing data to extract and quantify barcode tags and determine lineage relationships. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `analyze_barcode_sequencing_data`. It is specifically designed for synthetic biology applications to analyze sequencing data by extracting, quantifying, and determining the lineage relationships of barcodes.

---

## [233] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a genome annotation project and need a tool for de novo gene prediction in genomic sequences. What tool would you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `GlimmerHMM`. It is tailored for genomics and structural genomics, specifically supporting de novo gene prediction in genomic sequences and genome annotation projects using a Generalized Hidden Markov Model (GHMM) with splice site models from GeneSplicer.

---

## [234] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have untargeted mass spectrometry runs and want to automatically label my detected MS features as either high or low quality based on metrics derived from raw data. What tool can accomplish this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the **squallms** Bioconductor R package. It pulls raw mass spectrometry data to calculate quality metrics and applies a semi-labeled classification approach to label features in bulk as high or low quality.

---

## [235] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an assembly graph in GFA 1.0 format and need to convert the segment sequences into FASTA format. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Dshbio Exportsegments`, which is specifically designed to export assembly segment sequences in GFA 1.0 format into FASTA format.

---

## [236] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a Galaxy Data Manager to fetch or point to reference CTAT lncRNA annotations for our RNA pipeline. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'CTAT lncrna annotations Data Manager' tool, which is designed to retrieve and/or specify the location of CTAT lncRNA annotations.

---

## [237] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have single-cell RNA-seq data stored in an AnnData object. I need a tool to map coarse-grained connectivity and infer developmental trajectories between cell clusters. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using Scanpy PAGA. It accepts AnnData (or Loom) objects and is specifically designed for single-cell clustering studies requiring trajectory inference and mapping coarse-grained cell group connectivity.

---

## [238] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a text manipulation tool for column-based sorting and preprocessing of CSV/TSV files prior to downstream statistical analysis. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend csvtk-sort. It is suitable for any tabular data analysis requiring column-based sorting and preprocessing of CSV/TSV files, and includes options such as handling illegal rows.

---

## [239] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool in the GATK4 suite creates the reference STR table required for estimating Dragstr models?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **Gatk4 Composestrtablefile** (aliases: `composestrtablefile`, `dragstr`, `gatk4`). It identifies low-complexity STR sequences along the reference genome specifically for downstream Dragstr calibration.

---

## [240] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to average and filter fragmentation spectra for features identified by XCMS using an msPurity purityA object. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use msPurity.averageFragSpectra, which is designed to average and filter fragmentation spectra for each XCMS feature using an msPurity purityA object.

---

## [241] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am running parallel computations in R across multiple worker processes and encountering heavy memory overhead from duplicating large datasets. What tool can address this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the R package SharedObject. It stores R objects in a shared memory architecture, enabling multiple R processes to share the exact same data without duplicating memory or incurring costly data transfer overhead.

---

## [242] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a computational chemistry tool derived from AutoDock Vina that is specifically tailored for scoring function development and high-performance energy minimization. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use smina. It is a fork of AutoDock Vina customized specifically to support scoring function development and high-performance energy minimization, maintained by David Koes at the University of Pittsburgh.

---

## [243] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can you recommend an OpenMS TOPP tool for detecting transition groups in targeted SRM/MRM proteomics data?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use MRMTransitionGroupPicker, an OpenMS TOPP tool specifically built for targeted proteomics workflows utilizing SRM or MRM mass spectrometry.

---

## [244] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on single-cell RNA-seq data from a developmental biology time-course experiment and need to reconstruct trajectory graphs. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Monocle3 learnGraph, which is designed for trajectory inference and single-cell RNA-seq studies to construct graph structures across cell partitions.

---

## [245] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a BED file specifying target genomic regions from a whole exome sequencing study and aligned reads. Which tool can I use to compute the read depth across these intervals?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **Samtools bedcov**. It runs the `samtools bedcov` command to calculate read depth across genomic regions defined in an input BED dataset, making it suitable for coverage analysis in whole exome sequencing, targeted sequencing, and whole genome sequencing.

---

## [246] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are looking for an R-compatible software package to analyze paired heavy-light chain immune repertoire data from single cells. What would you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend CellaRepertorium. It offers methods to cluster and analyze high-throughput single-cell immune cell repertoires (incorporating an R interface to CD-HIT) and specializes in analyzing and visualizing paired heavy-light chain data, particularly from 10X Genomics VDJ assays.

---

## [247] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have strand-specific RNA-seq reads that require reverse-complementing to achieve proper sequence orientation. Which tool is suitable?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the Reverse-Complement tool from the Fasta Manipulation suite. It reverse-complements each sequence in your library and properly reverses quality scores if the library is in FASTQ format.

---

## [248] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have generated a haplotype-phased de novo genome assembly and need a genomics tool to evaluate its quality. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend MerquryFK. It is an enhanced version of the Merqury suite designed specifically for genomics applications, including de novo genome assembly quality assessment and haplotype-phased assembly evaluation.

---

## [249] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing small RNA-seq datasets and need a postprocessing tool within the PAQmiR pipeline to quantify miRNAs identified with miRDeep2. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `PAQmiR Postprocess Quantifier`, which is designed for the postprocessing and quantification step in the PAQmiR workflow based on miRDeep2 predictions.

---

## [250] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am processing 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing data and need a tool within the DADA2 framework to remove bimeric chimeric sequences. What tool do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `dada2: removeBimeraDenovo`, which is designed to detect and remove bimeras (chimeric sequences) in amplicon sequencing datasets.

---

## [251] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a Next Gen Mapper tool that can map RNA sequencing reads using reference indexes built either with or without builtin splice-junction gene models. What tool provides this?

**ASSISTANT:** RNA STAR is a Next Gen Mapper that allows you to select reference indexes built either 'with builtin gene-model' (containing splice junction annotations) or 'without builtin gene-model'.

---

## [252] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need an R package in genomics to perform feature extraction and clustering to delineate TAD boundaries from Hi-C contact matrices. What tool fits this?

**ASSISTANT:** SpectralTAD is recommended. It is an R package specialized in Hi-C clustering and feature extraction that identifies Topologically Associated Domains using a sliding-window modified spectral clustering algorithm.

---

## [253] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing single-cell RNA-seq data and need a tool for gene regulatory network inference that allows loading the expression data as a sparse matrix. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `PySCENIC GRN`. It is designed for transcriptomics and single-cell RNA-seq studies requiring gene regulatory network inference, and it provides an option to load expression data directly as a sparse matrix for the GRN inference step.

---

## [254] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an assembled bacterial genome and need to determine its sequence type using Torsten Seemann's MLST software. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Mlst', which runs Torsten Seemann's classic MLST directly on a genome assembly for bacterial typing.

---

## [255] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have matched tumor and normal BAM files and want to identify single nucleotide differences between them. What tool can I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use 'Somatic Sniper'. It is specifically designed to compare a tumor BAM and a normal BAM to identify single nucleotide positions that differ between the two samples.

---

## [256] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with single-cell RNA sequencing data and need a toolkit for quality control and exploring cellular heterogeneity. What tool fits this description?

**ASSISTANT:** Seurat (described under the geecee sequence analysis context) is designed specifically for quality control, analysis, and exploration of single-cell RNA sequencing data to identify and interpret sources of heterogeneity.

---

## [257] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to align raw FASTQ sequencing data against a FASTA reference genome using BWA-MEME. What tool is available for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'Bwameme Mem' tool, which performs FASTQ alignment to a FASTA reference genome using BWA-MEME.

---

## [258] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We just finished running the CellRanger V(D)J pipeline on our single-cell immune repertoire samples. We want to load this data into R, merge it with paired single-cell transcriptomics, and quantify clonotype abundances. Which tool is recommended?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `VDJdive`. It is designed for single-cell transcriptomics and immune repertoire analysis, offering functions to read CellRanger V(D)J data into R, merge with paired single-cell data, and quantify clonotype abundances.

---

## [259] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on an LC-MS/MS quantitative proteomics project with Data-dependent acquisition (DDA) runs and need an OpenMS tool to extract mass traces. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use MassTraceExtractor. It is designed for proteomics and metabolomics applications, specifically supporting LC-MS/MS quantitative proteomics workflows and Data-dependent acquisition (DDA) experiments.

---

## [260] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on metagenomics diversity analysis in Galaxy/QIIME 2 and need to compute principal coordinates from my distance matrix. Which tool handles this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `qiime2 diversity pcoa` tool, which applies principal coordinate analysis (PCoA) to microbial diversity distance matrices.

---

## [261] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with bisulfite sequencing (BS-Seq) data and need an aligner that can map reads using a 3-letter in silico converted genome strategy. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Bwameth Align. It performs alignment of BS-Seq reads using bwameth, mapping them efficiently against an in silico 3-letter converted genome reference to handle bisulfite-induced cytosine conversions.

---

## [262] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a software tool for forward population genetic simulation in asexual populations with a focus on cancer progression and arbitrary epistatic interactions between genes. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `OncoSimulR`. It provides functions for forward population genetic simulation in asexual populations with a special focus on cancer progression, allowing fitness to be modeled as an arbitrary function of genetic interactions (including epistasis and order restrictions) between genes or gene modules.

---

## [263] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have aligned read data from droplet-based single-cell sequencing and need to genotype bi-allelic SNPs to obtain allele-specific pileup matrices. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Cellsnp Mode A (cellsnp-lite). It is a C/C++ tool designed for efficient genotyping of bi-allelic SNPs in single cells and can generate snp x cell pileup UMI or read count matrices for droplet-based single-cell data post-alignment.

---

## [264] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a visualization tool to create a genome-level trellis layout where multiple tracks show multidimensional genomic features grouped across chromosomes. What tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `gtrellis`. It generates genome-level Trellis graphs to visualize genomic data conditioned by genomic categories like chromosomes, displaying multiple tracks that represent different features.

---

## [265] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have Oxford Nanopore sequencing reads and need to generate a consensus sequence. Which sequence analysis tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use medaka (or the medaka variant pipeline), which is specifically designed to create consensus sequences from nanopore sequencing data.

---

## [266] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Is there a tool available for removing DNA contaminants from sequencing reads?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use `Nanolyse`, which is specifically designed for DNA contaminant removal.

---

## [267] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a list of significant candidate genes from a differential expression experiment and need to perform functional enrichment analysis across GO and Reactome. What tool do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend **g:Profiler GOSt (Genomics)**. It performs functional enrichment analysis on gene lists and supports Gene Ontology (GO), KEGG, Reactome, and other databases.

---

## [268] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have assembled DNA sequences and need a genome annotation tool that can detect antimicrobial resistance genes, virulence factors, and determine MLST numbers.

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `catchSequenceInfo`. It integrates ABRicate and MLST to annotate DNA sequences with resistance genes, virulence genes, plasmid genes, allele IDs, and MLST sequence types.

## [269] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have sample count data and the output from DetermineGermlineContigPloidy. Which GATK4 tool should I invoke next to identify germline copy-number variants?

---

## [270] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have clustered metagenomic sequences and now have a multi-FASTA file along with a text list of representative sequence IDs. Which tool can extract only those sequences into a new FASTA file?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `fastaselectclust`. It takes a multi-FASTA file and a list of sequence identifiers to output a FASTA file containing only the selected subset of sequences.

---

## [271] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool should I use to plot consensus matrices from single-cell Hi-C data?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `scHicPlotConsensusMatrices`, which is built specifically for plotting consensus matrices in single-cell Hi-C and single-cell genomics workflows.

---

## [272] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an IUPAC consensus motif for a transcription factor and want to scan genomic sequences using FIMO. What tool can I use to convert the IUPAC string into the required motif file format?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `create_MEME_motif_file`. It converts an IUPAC motif into a MEME version 4 formatted file, making it directly compatible with FIMO and other tools in the MEME Suite.

---

## [273] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a modeling tool in pharmacology to analyze bi-exponential pharmacokinetic profiles. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `bi_exp_model` tool, which provides a bi-exponential pharmacokinetic model for pharmacological studies.

---

## [274] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with an OTU table from a 16S amplicon study and need to remove mitochondrial and chloroplast taxa while keeping specific bacterial phyla. Which tool can handle this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Filter taxa from an OTU table' tool. It allows you to filter operational taxonomic unit tables based on taxonomic metadata, supporting both positive filtering (retaining specific taxa) and negative filtering (discarding unwanted taxa) simultaneously.

---

## [275] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which molecular biology function should I call to detect restriction enzyme recognition sites?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the `find_restriction_enzymes` tool, which is specifically categorized under molecular biology for restriction enzyme identification.

---

## [276] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am running a case-control study with 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing data and want to identify differentially abundant microbial features using ANCOM in QIIME 2. What tool should I run?

---

## [277] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have RNA-Seq transcript-level expression data and want to quantify transcript isoform diversity across samples using statistical metrics like Shannon entropy or the Gini index. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the SplicingFactory R package. It is specifically designed for transcriptomics data to analyze alternative splicing and quantify transcript isoform diversity within or between samples using metrics such as Shannon entropy and the Gini index.

---

## [278] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on computational chemistry simulations and need a tool capable of performing protein-ligand docking. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using the 'rDock docking' tool, which performs protein-ligand docking using the rDock program.

---

## [279] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to generate a DRAGEN hashtable index for my reference genome FASTA file. Which tool can I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Narfmap Hashtable`. It is designed to create a DRAGEN hashtable index directly from a reference genome.

---

## [280] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We have identified several novel plant miRNAs from a small RNA-seq experiment and need a computational tool to identify their putative binding sites across the plant transcriptome. What tool is recommended?

**ASSISTANT:** TargetFinder is recommended. It is tailored for plant biology and small RNA-seq/miRNA analysis to predict small RNA binding sites on target transcripts from a supplied sequence database.

---

## [281] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have aligned reads with unique molecular identifiers and need to cluster them according to their genomic positions and UMI sequences. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Umitools Group`. It groups sequencing reads based on their unique molecular identifier (UMI) and mapping coordinates.

---

## [282] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** In my WGS structural variant study, I want to find genes that span copy number change points or have messy, discordant bin signals indicating potential fusion breakpoints. What CNVkit utility should I apply?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `CNVkit Breaks`. It is specifically built for variant analysis and structural variant detection to flag genes containing rearrangement breakpoints or exhibiting inconsistent copy number signals.

---

## [283] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool can help me determine gene deletions in a biological metabolic model to optimize the yield of a targeted compound?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use 'StrainDesign simulate-deletion'. It is designed for synthetic biology and systems biology applications to identify gene deletions in a biological model to optimize the reaction for a target compound.

---

## [284] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I generated Hi-C contact matrices using HiC-Pro, but my downstream visualization software requires cooler (.cool) format. What tool can perform this conversion?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use 'hicConvertFormat'. It natively supports converting HiC-Pro matrices into .cool format (as well as .h5, Homer, or .ginteractions).

---

## [285] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I generated a BED file from an interval projection script, but some features extend beyond the ends of chromosome boundaries according to my genome assembly chrom.sizes. What UCSC tool should I use to prune these out-of-bounds coordinates?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Ucsc Bedclip`. It is specifically designed to remove lines or trim intervals from a BED file that refer to off-chromosome locations or extend past chromosome boundaries.

---

## [286] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a viral genomics study profiling drug resistance in HIV sequencing data. Which sequence analysis tool should I use to generate a summary report of resistant variants?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Drug Resistance Mutations' tool. It is suited for viral genomics studies (such as HIV, HCV, and influenza) to identify and generate a report of drug-resistant mutations that occur above a specified reporting threshold.

---

## [287] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on genome assembly quality assessment and repeat content characterization for a comparative genomics project. Which sequence analysis tool fits these designs?

**ASSISTANT:** RepeatMasker is suitable for this task. It is designed for comparative genomics and genomics projects focusing on repeat content characterization, genome annotation, and genome assembly quality assessment.

---

## [288] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a neural network-based tool in proteomics to infer host-virus protein-protein interaction networks for emerging infectious pathogens. What tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** DeProViR is recommended. It is a neural network tool categorized under Network Inference for analyzing protein-protein interactions between host and viral proteins.

---

## [289] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are studying clinical isolates of Staphylococcus aureus and need to determine their agr locus types and detect operon variants quickly. What tool do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `agrvate`. It is designed for the rapid identification of *Staphylococcus aureus* *agr* locus types and *agr* operon variants.

---

## [290] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing samples with uneven sequencing depths. Which tool can I use to normalize them by equalizing sample sizes?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `FROGS Abundance normalisation`. It standardizes sample depths by retaining the same number of elements in each sample through random sampling.

---

## [291] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on metagenomic assembly and need an assembly tool that allows the expected coverage of unique regions to be configured automatically.

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `meta-velvetg`, an assembly tool where the expected coverage parameter (`-exp_cov`) can be set to "auto" specifically for metagenomic analyses.

---

## [292] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have interval files in BED format from an epigenomics experiment and need to convert their coordinates into BAM format for a peak calling workflow. Which tool is suitable?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `bedToBam` (Genomic Interval Operations). It is designed for genomics and epigenomics workflows (such as ChIP-seq, ATAC-seq, and RNA-seq coordinate conversions) where genomic intervals need to be converted to BAM format for downstream peak calling.

---

## [293] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I just completed the transdecoder_longorf step for my assembled transcripts. What module should I run next to predict coding regions and generate a GFF annotation?

**ASSISTANT:** You should run Transdecoder Predict, which is designed to be used after transdecoder_longorf to identify candidate coding regions and output a GFF file.

---

## [294] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing mass spectrometry-based single-cell proteomics data processed with MaxQuant. Which tool should I use for processing peptide-to-spectrum match (PSM) and peptide data?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use bioconductor-scp. It builds on the scp R package from Laurent Gatto's lab and provides functions to process mass spectrometry-based single-cell proteomics (SCP) data, including peptide-to-spectrum match (PSM) and peptide levels, particularly for experiments with MaxQuant output.

---

## [295] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am building a machine learning-based spectrum prediction workflow in OpenMS for peptide spectrum matching model training. Which tool is suitable for training theoretical spectrum models?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `SvmTheoreticalSpectrumGeneratorTrainer`. It is designed for proteomics machine learning workflows, specifically for peptide spectrum matching model training and spectrum prediction.

---

## [296] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have multiple replicate coverage tracks from ChIP-seq and ATAC-seq experiments that I need to combine together. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Add (Convert Formats)' tool. It is designed for genomics and epigenomics workflows to combine multiple replicate coverage tracks into a single track, working with input data in Wig or BigWig formats.

---

## [297] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to generate simulated DNA sequence datasets reflecting specific coalescent and demographic histories using msprime. What tool can I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Biomni: Simulate Demographic History'. It uses msprime to simulate DNA sequences based on specified coalescent and demographic models.

---

## [298] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am looking for a Bioconductor-compatible genomics tool designed specifically to annotate and parse structural variants from VCF files. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend StructuralVariantAnnotation, a Bioconductor package that provides a framework and helper functions for analyzing structural variants in VCF format.

---

## [299] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working in Python with biomedical imaging data and have a processed SimpleITK image object in memory. Which tool can I use to write this image out to disk?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'Biomni: Save Image' tool, which is designed to save SimpleITK images to file.

---

## [300] source=multitask_sft_big

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use 'limmaGUI'. It is a Graphical User Interface designed for differential expression analysis of two-color microarray data using the limma package.

---

## [301] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing metaproteomics data and need to download reference bioinformatic databases to expand my direct taxonomic and functional annotations. Which tool can handle this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `metaQuantome: database`. It downloads freely available bioinformatic databases to expand direct annotations for metaproteomics and proteomics studies.

## [302] source=multitask_sft_big

## [303] source=multitask_sft_big

## [304] source=multitask_sft_big

## [305] source=multitask_sft_big

---

## [306] source=multitask_sft_big

## [307] source=multitask_sft_big

## [308] source=multitask_sft_big

## [310] source=multitask_sft_big

## [314] source=multitask_sft_big

## [315] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to train a model for an image-based object detection and segmentation workflow using labeled images. Which tool should I use?
