## [316] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on variant analysis and need a tool within the CRAVAT suite to handle initial variant format conversion. What do you recommend?

---

## [317] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool should I use if I want to save Bioconductor data structures into portable, file-based artifacts with metadata instead of relying on .rds files?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use alabaster.base. It is built to save Bioconductor data structures into file artifacts with associated metadata and load them back into memory.

---

## [318] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on an ecology project and need to calculate a phylodiversity index using a Newick phylogenetic tree and tabular occupancy records. What tool fits this task?

**ASSISTANT:** The PhyloIndex tool from the ecology domain is suited for this task, as it computes phylodiversity indices directly from Newick phylogeny files and tabular occupancy data.

---

## [319] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Is there a GPU-accelerated implementation of the STAR-Fusion algorithm for RNA-Seq fusion calling?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, Parabricks Starfusion uses the GPU to perform fusion calling for RNA-Seq samples utilizing the STAR-Fusion algorithm.

---

## [320] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have RNA-seq quantification for both annotated and novel full-length transcript isoforms. Which R package can I use to statistically identify isoform switching events?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use IsoformSwitchAnalyzeR. It is an R package specifically designed for the statistical identification of isoform switching from RNA-seq-derived quantification of both annotated and novel full-length isoforms.

---

## [321] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a Collaborative Cross (CC) mouse QTL mapping study. Which tool should I use to prepare the covariate data files?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `CCQTL: Make input files`. It is specifically designed for quantitative trait loci (QTL) mapping in Collaborative Cross mouse studies to process sequence analysis and covariate inputs.

---

## [322] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have generated a mappability file using the GEM suite and need to convert it into a bedGraph track for downstream visualization. Which tool can perform this conversion?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Gem2Bedmappability` from the Gem2 suite, which is specifically designed to convert GEM mappability files into bedgraph format.

---

## [323] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool should I use in Galaxy to bridge my functional genomics annotation data into an InterMine instance?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Create InterMine Interchange' (Convert Formats), which converts data into the intermediate format designed specifically for Galaxy-to-InterMine interoperability.

---

## [324] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on Whole Genome Sequencing (WGS) data and need an offline method to score and annotate variant effects. What nf-core pipeline or tool should I consider?

**ASSISTANT:** You should consider the nf-core tool 'cadd'. It provides a CADD scripts release specifically designed for offline variant scoring and annotation in Whole Genome Sequencing (WGS) and Whole Exome Sequencing (WES) analyses.

---

## [325] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have structural variant calls in BEDPE format and need to convert them to VCF format. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Survivor Bedpetovcf', which converts a BEDPE file to a VCF file (currently in beta).

---

## [326] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can you recommend a tool to calculate enrichment of ChIP-seq binding peaks across genomic features?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use CEAS (Cis-regulatory Element Annotation System), a tool designed to characterize genome-wide protein-DNA interaction patterns and calculate ChIP enrichment statistics across important genomic features for both ChIP-chip and ChIP-Seq experiments.

---

## [327] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool to scan genomic DNA sequences for specific IUPAC pattern motifs and report the hits per sequence. What do you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'IUPAC scan and output matches per seq' tool (Sequence Analysis). It scans input DNA sequences for any DNA pattern represented as an IUPAC string and outputs a table listing the matches per sequence name.

---

## [328] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have cohort-scale single-cell transcriptomics data and want to evaluate cell type specific gene expression differences linked to disease state and continuous cell-level covariates. Which tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using 'dreamlet' (categorized under BatchEffect, DifferentialExpression, and Epigenetics). It is specifically designed for cohort-scale single-cell/single-nucleus transcriptomics to study cell type specific differential expression and supports modeling continuous cell-level covariates.

---

## [329] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which SAM-category tool can I use to evaluate the coverage and performance metrics of a whole genome sequencing experiment?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use FastqToSam, which computes metrics to evaluate coverage and performance in whole genome sequencing experiments.

---

## [330] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have multiple CSV and TSV tables generated across multi-omics analyses and need to generate an interactive, visual HTML report. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'datavzrd (nf-core)', which is specifically designed to create visual HTML reports from collections of CSV and TSV tables.

---

## [331] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have genomic coordinates in a BED file and need to extract the corresponding DNA sequences into a FASTA file. Which tool is suitable for this task?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use FastaFromBed. It is designed for sequence analysis and genomics workflows to generate FASTA files with DNA sequences extracted based on coordinates provided in a BED file.

---

## [332] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a list of Human Phenotype Ontology identifiers and need to obtain the human-readable phenotype names for each. Is there a tool for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Biomni: Get Hpo Names` from the database module. It is designed specifically to retrieve the corresponding names for a given list of HPO terms.

---

## [333] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have several profile hidden Markov models created with an older version of HMMER, and I need to convert them into different HMMER formats for compatibility with my sequence analysis pipeline. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `hmmconvert`. It is designed for sequence analysis workflows to convert an input profile file into different HMMER formats, including handling legacy HMMER2 to HMMER conversions.

---

## [334] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have amplicon metagenomics sequencing data and need to merge strictly identical reads. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use 'Vsearch Dereplicate' (vsearch/dereplicate). It merges strictly identical sequences within an amplicon or metagenomics file based on equal sequence length and identical nucleotide composition (case-insensitive, treating T and U as identical).

---

## [335] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing small RNA-seq data from germline tissues to detect ping-pong amplification loops in piRNA populations. What tool can calculate this signature?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Small RNA Signatures' (RNA) tool. It is specifically built for small RNA-seq, piRNA, and siRNA analysis, computing the ping-pong signal based on Brennecke et al. (2008) alongside read overlap classes and z-scores.

---

## [336] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I transferred several FASTQ and BAM files across storage systems, and some were re-sorted. Is there a tool that can verify file integrity regardless of read ordering?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `BamHash`. It computes a checksum for FASTQ or BAM files by summing hash values derived from read names, pair flags (first or last), sequences, and quality scores. Because the hashes are summed, the resulting value is completely independent of read ordering, making it ideal for integrity verification.

---

## [337] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We generated pooled high-throughput sequencing data across multiple populations and want to detect transposable element insertions and absences. Which software package should we use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use TEMP (specifically TEMP Insertion). It is a software package tailored for sequence analysis and population genomics designed to detect transposable element (TE) insertions and absences from pooled high-throughput sequencing data.

---

## [338] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a pipeline step that checks my input filename against a pattern and decides whether to skip a downstream analysis step. What tool can provide that branching logic?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `Regex switch` tool. It evaluates a regular expression pattern against the input file name and outputs two booleans (`matched` and `unmatched`), which can be linked directly to a downstream step's 'Conditionally skip step?' input.

---

## [339] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool to sort my genomic GTF annotation files by chromosome, position, and feature for an RNA-seq pipeline. What nf-core tool should I choose?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `gtfsort`. It is a dedicated GTF sorter that organizes records by chr/pos/feature using a lexicographically-based index ordering algorithm.

---

## [340] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to perform differential abundance analysis on microbiome compositional data while correcting for sampling bias. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use ANCOMBC, which provides differential abundance and bias correction methods (such as ANCOM-BC2) tailored for microbiome compositional data.

---

## [341] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on metagenomics data analysis and need a tool to handle cluster sizing. What tool is available?

**ASSISTANT:** For metagenomics analyses requiring cluster size determination, you can use the tool `count_clustersize`.

---

## [342] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We have segmented single-cell organelles and nuclei from a high-content screening microscopy assay. What tool should we run to quantify signal levels inside these segmented regions?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'MeasureObjectIntensity'. It is specifically designed for microscopy and high-content imaging workflows to calculate intensity features for identified objects such as nuclei, cells, and organelles.

---

## [343] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have PacBio CCS reads and need a tool to strip away polyA tails and eliminate artificial concatemers. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Isoseq Refine. It is specifically designed for PacBio Iso-Seq workflows to remove polyA tails and filter out artificial concatemers from CCS data.

---

## [344] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am configuring reference databases for taxonomic profiling and need a tool from Data Managers to fetch the MetaPhlAn2 reference data. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'MetaPhlAn2 download' tool under Data Managers to acquire the necessary reference database for MetaPhlAn2.

---

## [345] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Is there a helper utility in Galaxy/BioMate to ensure NCBI settings exist before executing sra-tools commands?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use `Custom Sratoolsncbisettings`. It specifically checks for suitable NCBI settings or dynamically generates them on the fly to support utilities like `prefetch` and `fasterq-dump`.

---

## [346] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a small molecule drug candidate and need to profile its essential chemical features. Is there a Biomni tool for this pharmacology task?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use `Biomni: Calculate Physicochemical Properties`, which is specifically designed to calculate key physicochemical properties of a drug candidate molecule.

---

## [347] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We need a sequence analysis variant caller that unifies multiple disparate calling approaches for multi-sample targeted sequencing data. What tool fits this criteria?

**ASSISTANT:** Unified Genotyper is designed for this use case. It unifies the approaches of several disparate callers, supports multi-sample variant calling, and is suitable for targeted sequencing.

---

## [348] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I ran a BLAST search that returned multiple hits per query sequence. I want to keep only the top hit for each sequence without having to rerun the entire BLAST analysis. What tool can do this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use TopHit filter (TopHit_namefilter). It is a simple filtering tool designed to retain only the top hit or first N occurrences of an identifier from existing BLAST results without needing to rerun BLAST.

---

## [349] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing single-cell RNA sequencing data and need to plot quality control metrics across my RNA libraries. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Scater: plot library QC (RNA)`, which is specialized for generating quality control plots across RNA libraries.

---

## [350] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am setting up a DamID fusion protein mapping study to profile genome-wide protein-DNA binding and chromatin interactions. Which tool is tailored for this omics analysis?

**ASSISTANT:** The damidseq tool is specifically suitable for DamID fusion protein mapping studies, chromatin interaction profiling, and genome-wide protein-DNA binding analysis across ChIP-seq and DamID-seq primary omics.

---

## [351] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool in the sequence analysis domain to calculate protein sequence statistics and peptide properties. Which tool fits this requirement?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `pepstats`, which is specifically designated for sequence analysis tasks involving protein statistics.

---

## [352] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am performing transcriptomic analysis on a multi-sample RNA-seq cohort (around 8 samples) and would like to incorporate trusted introns from GENCODE annotations. What tool supports this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `PsiCLASS`. It is tailored for multi-sample RNA-seq experiments and allows supplying a set of trusted introns extracted from GENCODE annotations or generated by RNASTAR.

---

## [353] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to build a custom transcriptome annotation for my organism in SQLite format for downstream visualization. Which tool can do this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `Create TRIPS-Viz annotation`. This transcriptomics tool builds a custom transcriptome annotation stored in SQLite format for your organism.

---

## [354] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Is there a tool capable of performing differential enrichment analysis from high-throughput sequencing data that can leverage both uniquely mapped and multi-mapped reads?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, 'TEtranscripts' is designed specifically for this purpose. It utilizes both unambiguously (uniquely) and ambiguously (multi-) mapped reads to perform differential enrichment analyses.

---

## [355] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have aligned BAM files from a paired-end sequencing run and need to extract alignment summary statistics. What tool can I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use Picard Collectalignmentsummarymetrics, which calculates summary alignment metrics specifically for paired-end BAM libraries.

---

## [356] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on somatic mutation analysis and need a software tool to represent patient point mutations across the standard 96 trinucleotide contexts. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use SparseSignatures. It is designed for somatic mutation bioinformatics workflows and categorizes point mutations into 96 classes defined by the mutated base, the resulting base, and flanking sequence context.

---

## [357] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have chemical structures stored in CML format and need to convert them to SDF. What tool is available for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'CML to SDF' tool, which is designed to convert CML files directly into SDF format.

---

## [358] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am managing a large-scale comparative metagenomic project with multiple sequencing datasets. Is there a sequence analysis tool that gives a global similarity overview across all my samples?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, 'Commet' (COmpare Multiple METagenomes) is specifically designed for sequence analysis in large metagenomic projects to generate a global similarity overview across all datasets.

---

## [359] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool can I use for visual inspection and quality control of ribosome profiling data with P-site mapping?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `riboWaltz-plot`. It is specifically designed for the visual inspection of ribosome profiling and translation genomics data, supporting experiments with P-site mapping.

---

## [360] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are preparing mRNA sequencing libraries that generate reads focused specifically on transcript ends, and standard isoform quantification is biased. Is there a tool to truncate transcriptome annotations to fit this design?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you should use `txcutr`. It is specifically designed for mRNA sequencing library preparation methods that generate reads from transcript ends. It assists isoform usage quantification by generating truncated versions of transcriptome annotations.

---

## [361] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with metagenomic feature tables across an environmental gradient and need a QIIME 2 tool to cluster my features hierarchically into a bifurcating tree using Ward's method. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `qiime2 gneiss gradient-clustering`. It is designed for metagenomics to build a bifurcating tree via Ward hierarchical clustering based on the mean difference of gradients observed across features.

---

## [362] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need the Bioconductor module from the alabaster framework responsible for file handling. Which tool entry should I select?

**ASSISTANT:** You should select `alabaster_files`, which represents the `alabaster.files` Bioconductor module.

---

## [363] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have clustered my single-cell RNA-seq data and want to discover cell-type-specific differentially expressed genes with p-values and ROC scores. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should run `Seurat FindMarkers`. It identifies differentially expressed marker genes across identity classes or clusters in single-cell transcriptomics datasets and outputs a ranked text file containing metrics such as p-values and ROC scores.

---

## [364] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a genetics project and need a tool to fit a linear mixed model for genomic prediction using my genotype and phenotype data. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the tool "Biomni: Fit Genomic Prediction Model" (genetics), which fits a linear mixed model for genomic prediction using genotype and phenotype data.

---

## [365] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need an R/Bioconductor package for transcriptomics that provides a uniform interface and plotting capabilities across edgeR, limma, and DEXSeq for differential bin/exon usage.

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend the diffUTR package. It provides a uniform interface and plotting functions for limma-, edgeR-, and DEXSeq-powered differential bin and exon usage analysis, and also includes an improved version of limma::diffSplice.

---

## [366] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool in genomics and epigenomics to model and understand transcription factor binding using thermodynamic principles. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend ChIPanalyser. It is designed for genomics and epigenomics (ChIP-seq) applications to predict and understand TF binding using a statistical thermodynamic model.

---

## [367] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a data source tool to pull genomic annotations in BED format from Ensembl, including specific biotypes like transcribed_processed_pseudogene and non_coding features.

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Retrieve Ensembl features in BED format'. This tool retrieves annotations from Ensembl and supports diverse biotypes including non_coding, protein_coding, pseudogene, transcribed_processed_pseudogene, and nonsense_mediated_decay.

---

## [368] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on glycoengineering and need a tool to score potential O-glycosylation sites across my sequence. What do you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend 'Biomni: Predict O Glycosylation Hotspots' from the glycoengineering domain, which performs heuristic O-glycosylation hotspot scoring.

---

## [369] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have clustered metagenomic reads using DNAclust, but there are numerous low-depth clusters and singletons that I need to filter out based on cluster size. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `cutClust`. It is tailored for metagenomic clustering workflows to filter DNAclust output by cluster depth, allowing you to easily strip out singletons (e.g., setting cutoff to 2) or larger low-abundance clusters (e.g., setting cutoff to 50).

---

## [370] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a fast graphics tool to inspect family size distributions of all tags aligned to targeted reference regions from a Duplex Sequencing run. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'FSD regions:', a tool under Graphics that provides computationally fast insights into the distribution of tag family sizes from Duplex Sequencing experiments across targeted regions.

---

## [371] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can I use xmlstarlet to run differential expression analysis on my RNA-seq count matrices?

**ASSISTANT:** No. xmlstarlet is a general-purpose XML transformation tool used to convert XML files using an XSL conversion file; it is explicitly not designed for omics data analysis.

---

## [372] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have liquid chromatography-mass spectrometry (LC-MS) proteomics and metabolomics runs that utilize internal calibration standards. Which tool is tailored for this setup?

**ASSISTANT:** The tool `InternalCalibration` is designed specifically for proteomics and metabolomics LC-MS experiments that incorporate internal calibration standards.

---

## [373] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool from the GEM suite to calculate the mappability score profile of my reference index. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Gem2 Gemmappability`, which defines the mappability of a reference sequence.

---

## [374] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a cohort VCF file containing dozens of samples. I need to subset the file to compare cases against controls and isolate specific variant loci. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Select Variants' tool. It is designed to subset large multi-sample VCF files by samples (e.g., cases vs. controls) or by variant and non-variant loci that meet specific criteria.

---

## [375] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with genotype data generated from next-generation sequencing platforms and need an R tool to perform quality control, filtering, and error correction on my calls. What tool fits this use case?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend GBScleanR. It is an R package designed for quality check, filtering, and error correction of genotype data derived from NGS-based genotyping platforms, utilizing Hidden Markov Models.

---

## [376] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an annotated genomic intervals file in standard BED format that I need to convert into indexed bigBed format. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the UCSC tool `Ucsc Bedtobigbed`, which converts files from BED format directly to bigBed format.

---

## [377] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I want to automate Cytoscape network analyses and visualization directly from my R environment without manually clicking through the GUI. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'RCy3'. It enables you to visualize, analyze, and explore networks in Cytoscape programmatically via R, providing function-level equivalents for graphical user interface actions.

---

## [378] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with output files from Cufflinks and need a tool that can manage the data persistently and generate standard visualization plots. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use cummeRbund. It is designed specifically for persistent storage, access, exploration, and manipulation of Cufflinks high-throughput sequencing data, and it includes numerous plotting functions for standard visualizations.

---

## [379] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an integrated .pairsam file and need to extract the .pairs and .sam components separately. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Pairtools Split, which is specifically designed to split a .pairsam file into separate .pairs and .sam files.

---

## [380] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have raw count matrices from a single-cell RNA-seq experiment and need to compute normalized counts per million (CPM). Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use Scater CalculateCPM. It is designed for single-cell RNA-seq and transcriptomics studies requiring normalized gene expression derived from count matrices.

---

## [381] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have metagenomic sequencing data from bacterial samples and need to identify and classify Bacillus thuringiensis serovars aizaiwai and kurstaki. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the variant analysis tool `Bacillus Thuringiensis serovars aizaiwai and kurstaki detection`, which is specifically tailored for bacterial strain identification and Bacillus thuringiensis serovar typing from genomic and metagenomic data.

---

## [382] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have assembled single-contig viral genomes from a metagenomics study. I need an automated pipeline to check for host contamination in proviruses and estimate genome completeness. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `CheckV end to end`. It is a fully automated command-line pipeline designed to assess the quality of single-contig viral genomes, estimate genome fragment completeness, identify host contamination for integrated proviruses, and identify closed viral genomes.

---

## [383] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to check for contamination in my metagenomic read pool by measuring containment against a sketch of reference sequences. What tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `mash screen`. It assesses how well sketched query sequences (`.msh`) are contained within a pool of reads or contigs (FASTA/FASTQ), making it well-suited for contamination detection and metagenomic reference screening.

---

## [384] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an amplicon feature table and a phylogenetic tree. I want to compute qualitative (presence/absence) phylogenetic beta diversity distances between all samples. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'qiime2 diversity-lib unweighted-unifrac', which computes Unweighted UniFrac distances for each sample in a feature table using phylogenetic branch lengths without weighting by relative abundance.

---

## [385] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We have a multi-gigabyte reference FASTA file that we need to divide into several smaller FASTA files so we can distribute jobs across an HPC cluster. What utility should we use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `faSplit`. It is a dedicated utility specifically designed to split a single FASTA file into several smaller FASTA files for downstream distributed computing or parallel analysis.

---

## [386] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a climate and remote sensing toolkit to extract object-based time series from Copernicus Sentinel 2 and Landsat 8 imagery. What tool can I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use EODIE. It is a climate analysis toolkit specifically designed to extract object-based time series from Earth Observation data, including Copernicus Sentinel 2, Landsat 8, and standard GeoTIFF files.

---

## [387] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I performed a de novo genome assembly and need to filter contigs from my FASTA file based on sequence length. Which tool can I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the `Filter contigs` tool, which filters contigs from an assembled FASTA file that are longer than the specified length.

---

## [388] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool can I use to inspect bisulfite sequencing reads and evaluate methylation by measuring C->T conversion at each position along the reads?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Rastair Mbias. It is designed to assess C->T conversion as a readout for methylation on a per-read-position basis in bisulfite/bisulphite sequencing.

---

## [389] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool from the sequence analysis category can I use for centroid-based RNA secondary structure folding?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `kcentroidfold`, which is designated for sequence analysis tasks involving centroid-based RNA folding.

---

## [390] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We just completed several ChIP-seq runs and want to check whether the enrichment signal is cleanly separated from background noise before calling peaks. Which tool is suitable?

**ASSISTANT:** plotFingerprint is ideal for this task. It assesses the strength of ChIP enrichment by measuring how clearly the signal separates from background across samples.

---

## [391] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I ran Nonpareil on several metagenomic datasets and now have multiple .npo output files. Is there a tool that can plot all their redundancy curves together into a single PNG image?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'Nonpareil Set' tool. It is specifically designed to take multiple Nonpareil `.npo` files and visualize their metagenome redundancy curves combined into a single PNG image.

---

## [392] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have several bacterial query genome assemblies and want to calculate pairwise distances between each query and reference assembly for comparative genomics. What tool should I choose?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `0.9.7 Dev21`. It is designed for genomics, bacterial genomics, and comparative genomics to calculate pairwise distances between each query and reference assembly combination.

---

## [393] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have segmented multi-channel imaging data and need to perform single-cell quantification to get an intensity table compatible with histoCAT. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the MCQUANT tool. It is designed for single-cell quantification given a segmentation mask and a multi-channel image, producing a CSV output structured to align with histoCAT.

---

## [394] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have multiple FASTQ files (R1, R2, and index reads I1/I2) and I want to convert them into an unmapped BAM file while allocating bases via read structures. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `fgbio FastqToBam`. It takes one or more FASTQ files (optionally gzipped) such as R1, R2, I1, or I2 and generates an unmapped BAM (or SAM/CRAM) using read structures to allocate read bases.

---

## [395] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with transcriptomic data and want to model transcription factor activity using ChIP-seq molecular signatures in R. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the R package `xcore`. It is specifically designed for transcription factor activity modeling by integrating user gene expression data with known molecular signatures. You can also utilize its accompanying data package, `xcoredata`, which provides pre-constructed molecular signatures from publicly available ChIP-seq experiments.

---

## [396] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with whole blood DNA methylation data and want to dissect intra-sample heterogeneity to determine cell-type proportions. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'EpiDISH' package. It is designed for epigenomics to infer proportions of a priori known cell types in mixed samples, specifically supporting Epigenetic Dissection of Intra-Sample-Heterogeneity in whole blood.

---

## [397] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with next-generation sequencing data from genetically heterogeneous viral samples and need to reconstruct haplotypes. What tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'Reconstruct haplotypes' tool from the ShoRAH suite. ShoRAH is specifically designed to analyze genetically heterogeneous NGS samples and perform haplotype reconstruction and error correction.

---

## [398] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I want to test whether quantile normalization is appropriate for my ExpressionSet data across tumor and normal groups. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use quantro. It provides a data-driven test to evaluate the assumptions of quantile normalization on eSet objects using sample group-level classifications.

---

## [399] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have aligned read families from a duplex sequencing experiment and need to generate single-strand and duplex consensus reads. Which tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **Du Novo: Make consensus reads**, which is designed for duplex sequencing experiments to create single-strand and duplex consensus reads from aligned read families.

---

## [400] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a genomics study and need to identify structural variants from paired-end sequencing data using discordant read pair analysis. Which tool can I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the '8_ident_SV' tool. It is designed for genomics applications to perform structural variant identification specifically using discordant read pair analysis on paired-end sequencing data.

---

## [401] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to characterize the size distribution of exons and introns from my transcriptomics annotations. What tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'get sizes' tool from the Convert Formats category. It determines sequence and annotation size distributions, making it suitable for genomic feature characterization like exon and intron size analysis.

---

## [402] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an NCBI taxonomy ID and need to pull down all corresponding nucleotidic and peptidic sequences directly from NCBI. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Get FASTA from taxon' tool. It retrieves all nucleotidic and peptidic sequences from the NCBI database for a given taxonomy ID.

---

## [403] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am configuring a Galaxy instance for Trinity CTAT workflows and need to install the reference Centrifuge index files from the official FTP repository into Galaxy's tool data tables. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** Use the 'CTAT Centrifuge Indexes Data Manager'. It is built specifically to fetch the Centrifuge index from the official FTP source and register its location in Galaxy's reference data tables.

---

## [404] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We want to analyze protein-small molecule interaction profiles from cell lysates using mass spectrometry data, preferably with an interactive Shiny interface. What tool fits this?

**ASSISTANT:** The `Doscheda` tool is tailored for this application. It handles quantitative chemoproteomics mass spectrometry data, provides normalization and visualization capabilities, and includes a Shiny application to run the analysis pipeline.

---

## [405] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am looking for an R package to compute semantic similarity between gene groups based on their Gene Ontology annotations. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend 'GOSemSim', an R package designed for semantic comparisons of Gene Ontology (GO) annotations. It provides quantitative methods to compute similarities between individual genes and groups of genes.

---

## [406] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool can I use to assign restriction fragments to pairs in a Hi-C or genomic pairs dataset?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the tool `Pairtools Restrict` (part of the pairtools suite), which is specifically designed to assign restriction fragments to pairs.

---

## [407] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can I use Image Tileify to process microscopy TIFF stacks or analyze gene expression heatmaps?

**ASSISTANT:** No. Image Tileify is a general image processing and graphics distortion tool. It is not designed for biological data analysis and is explicitly not suitable for omics or quantitative scientific image workflows.

---

## [408] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with transcriptomic RNA-seq data and need a tool to test the statistical significance of clusters. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use SigFuge, an algorithm specifically designed for testing the significance of clustering in RNA-seq transcriptomics data.

---

## [409] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a family-based whole genome sequencing study to identify candidate disease variants that follow Mendelian inheritance patterns. Which GEMINI tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use GEMINI's built-in tool for autosomal recessive or dominant inheritance. Provided that familial relationships among your samples are defined in your loaded GEMINI database, this tool will identify and report variants matching autosomal recessive or dominant inheritance patterns.

---

## [410] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool should I use for demultiplexing single-cell sequencing samples based on cell hashing data?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Htodemux. It is specifically designed for single-cell hashing-based deconvolution and sample demultiplexing from cell hashing data.

---

## [411] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with single-cell transcriptomics data in an iSEE application and want to configure custom panel classes and modes. What tool provides these extensions?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `iSEEu` (the iSEE universe). It extends the base `iSEE` package with additional panel classes and preconfigured modes specifically tailored for applications such as single-cell transcriptomics and bulk transcriptomics.

---

## [412] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with ribosome profiling (Ribo-seq) data and need an R package that can compute optimal P-site offsets and generate diagnostic plots. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `riboWaltz`. It is an R package designed for translatomics and ribosome profiling data that calculates optimal P-site offsets and provides diagnostic analysis and visual inspection.

---

## [413] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with HUMAnN output from a metagenomic profiling study and need to separate the stratified community pathway abundance table into unstratified and stratified components. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Split stratified table' tool from the HUMAnN pipeline. It is specifically designed for metagenomics and metatranscriptomics to process pathway presence/absence and abundance tables, allowing you to split stratified microbial community data.

---

## [414] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have run HH-search on my sequences to find structural homologs and want to filter the results to predict protein-protein interactions. What tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'SPRING Min-Z'. It is designed for sequence analysis and structural proteomics, taking HH-search or HH-blits homology results and filtering them through the SPRING protein interaction cross-reference using the min-Z metric.

---

## [415] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are studying a large consanguineous pedigree with many genetic markers. Our analysis fails with Lander-Green-based algorithms because the pedigree is too large. What tool can handle multipoint parametric linkage analysis under these conditions?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Swiftlink`. It is specifically designed to perform multipoint parametric linkage analysis on large consanguineous pedigrees that cannot be analyzed by Lander-Green algorithm-based software due to pedigree size and dense marker sets.

---

## [416] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with fluorescence microscopy images that have multiple stacked channels. Which tool can separate each channel into its own grayscale image?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the `ColorToGray` module. It supports splitting color and channel-stacked images by extracting each channel into an individual grayscale image via its 'Split' option.

---

## [417] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a multiple sequence alignment in FASTA format and need to obtain a single consensus sequence from it. Which EMBOSS tool can do this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Emboss Cons' (cons). It calculates a consensus sequence from a multiple sequence alignment using sequence weights and a scoring matrix.

---

## [418] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I ran bisulfite sequencing pipelines on my samples and have multiple bedGraph files. What tool should I use to aggregate them into methylation and coverage matrices?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use methrix. It is designed to summarize diverse bedgraph files from bisulfite sequencing pipelines into unified methylation and coverage matrices for downstream epigenomic analysis.

---

## [419] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a comparative microbiome analysis using 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing data across multiple samples. Which tool can I use to filter my OTU table?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `filter_otu_table`. It is designed for metagenomics, 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing, and microbial community analysis, specifically for comparative microbiome studies with multiple samples.

---

## [420] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing 16S rRNA amplicon data and need to filter my ampvis2 object based on specific taxonomy. Which tool can perform this subsetting?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **ampvis2 subset data**, which subsets the data in ampvis2 objects based on taxonomy and outputs the subsetted object.

---

## [421] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have single-cell RNA sequencing data from multiple experimental runs with strong batch effects. I want an MNN-based batch correction method. What tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `batchelor`. It implements batch correction methods for single-cell RNA sequencing data, specifically methods based on detecting mutually nearest neighbors (MNN) as well as linear regression variants on log-expression values.

---

## [422] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with 16S rRNA amplicon distance matrices and want to test whether sample groups differ significantly using a permutation-based MANOVA equivalent. What QIIME 2 tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `qiime2 diversity adonis`. It implements the ADONIS permutation-based statistical test from R's vegan package, partitioning sums of squares of multivariate distance matrices analogous to MANOVA.

---

## [423] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need an NVIDIA Clara Parabricks GPU-accelerated module that handles alignment, sorting, duplicate marking, and BQSR calculation simultaneously. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Parabricks Fq2Bam. It is an NVIDIA Clara Parabricks GPU-accelerated tool designed specifically for alignment, sorting, BQSR calculation, and duplicate marking.

---

## [424] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool should I use to visualize quality control metrics and inspect numerical variant distributions from sequencing variant data?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the Histogram (Variant Analysis) tool. It is suited for genomics and variant analysis, specifically for distribution analysis of numerical variant metrics and quality control visualization of sequencing data.

---

## [425] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We have matched tumor-normal whole exome sequencing data from oncology patients and need to assess somatic microsatellite instability (MSI). What tool fits this task?

**ASSISTANT:** You should consider 'Msisensorpro Msisomatic' (MSIsensor-pro). It evaluates microsatellite instability (MSI) in cancer sequencing data and explicitly supports whole exome sequencing (WES), whole genome sequencing (WGS), and targeted gene panels.

---

## [426] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to calculate genomic mappability within non-overlapping windows from a BigWig file as part of an HMMcopy CNV pipeline. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Hmmcopy Mapcounter`. It is the `mapCounter` utility from the HMMcopy suite specifically designed to generate mappability scores in non-overlapping genomic windows from a BigWig file.

---

## [427] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I conducted large-scale hypothesis testing in a transcriptomic study and have a distribution of p-values. Which BioNet tool can I use to fit a Beta-Uniform mixture model to these p-values?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Fit a BUM model' tool from the BioNet package. It is specifically designed to fit a Beta-Uniform mixture model to p-value distributions from large-scale hypothesis testing across genomics, transcriptomics, or proteomics.

---

## [428] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to perform alchemical free energy calculations using GROMACS. Which tool provides this functionality?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the Alchemical Run tool, which is designed to execute alchemical free energy calculations utilizing GROMACS.

---

## [429] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to convert k-mer sequences into MEME format following an SVM-based sequence classification workflow. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Kmer To MEME`, which converts kmers into MEME motif format for downstream sequence analysis.

---

## [430] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am assembling an RNA-seq workflow using TopHat2 and Cufflinks. Is there a web service tool to help summarize and rename the output files from these upstream tools?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use `HistorySummary`. It is specifically designed for transcriptomics pipelines using TopHat2 for alignment and Cufflinks/Cuffquant for transcript assembly and quantification, allowing renaming of their respective outputs.

---

## [431] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I want to perform dimension reduction and ordination on a metagenomic contingency table from a microbiome study, including both constrained and unconstrained analyses. Which tool matches this?

**ASSISTANT:** The RCM package is designed specifically for this purpose. It provides dimension reduction and exploratory visualization for metagenomics/microbiome datasets using empirical Bayes dispersion estimation and flexible response functions in unconstrained and constrained settings.

---

## [432] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have mapped reads in a BAM file and want to calculate coverage across the entire genome, formatted as BedGraph. Which tool can do this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `bedtools Genome Coverage`. It calculates genome-wide interval coverage from BAM or BED files and outputs the results in BedGraph format.

---

## [433] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool under variant analysis can I use to evaluate results and schemas for cgMLST or wgMLST?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use chewBBACA NSStats, a variant analysis tool from the chewBBACA suite specifically intended for evaluating core genome and whole genome MultiLocus Sequence Typing schemas and results.

---

## [434] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have assembled bacterial contigs and want to identify AMR genes, point mutations, and plasmid types in a single analysis. What tool do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend 'Staramr Search'. It evaluates genome contigs across genomics, amr, plasmid, and mlst domains by screening against the ResFinder, PlasmidFinder, and PointFinder databases.

---

## [435] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on single-cell data integration across datasets that have partially overlapping, mosaic feature sets. Is there a Bioconductor workflow suited for mosaic integration?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the Bioconductor workflow 'stabmap_singlecell', which is designed specifically for StabMap single-cell mosaic integration.

---

## [436] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an idXML file from an OpenMS run and need to update and refresh the protein references linked to my peptide hits. Which tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Openms Peptideindexer'. It is specifically designed to refresh the protein references for all peptide hits within OpenMS idXML data.

---

## [437] source=multitask_sft_big

---

## [438] source=multitask_sft_big

## [439] source=multitask_sft_big

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `ezBAMQC`. It performs rapid quality assessment on NGS BAM files from transcriptomics/RNA-seq experiments and calculates correlation across samples when multiple BAM files are provided.

---

## [440] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a microbial ecology study evaluating OTU quality and need a tool from the Mothur suite. What would you suggest?

## [441] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We have paired-end short-read whole-genome sequencing data and need to identify large balanced inversions and interchromosomal translocations at breakpoint resolution. What tool is recommended?

---

## [442] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with short circular genomes and need a tool that can perform both de novo assembly and heteroplasmy or variance calling. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use NOVOPlasty. It is specifically designed as a de novo assembler and heteroplasmy/variance caller for short circular genomes.

---

## [443] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a cross-sample comparative flow cytometry study with population clustering and need a tool to aggregate population event counts and marker MFIs. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Generate overview information' tool (Flow Cytometry Analysis). It is designed specifically for multi-sample flow cytometry studies with population clustering and cross-sample comparative analysis, processing event counts relative to reference population patterns and marker MFI across populations.

---

## [444] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a molecular biology function that can determine whether a particular genomic position falls inside any defined region. What tool is available?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the tool `is_position_covered`, which checks if a given position is covered by any region.

---

## [445] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which sequence analysis tool aggregates Hi-C contacts with recommendations to filter near-diagonal background using median TAD size?

**ASSISTANT:** The recommended tool is `hicAggregateContacts` (Sequence Analysis). It specifically recommends setting the minimum range farther from the diagonal than the median TAD size to avoid background interaction artifacts.

---

## [446] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are conducting image-based phenotyping and need a tool to push our segmented regions of interest (ROIs) and summary measurement tables back into OMERO. What should we use?

**ASSISTANT:** The uploadROIandMeasuresToOMERO tool is designed specifically for this task. It uploads ROIs and associated measurement results tables directly into OMERO.

---

## [447] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I ran differential expression using edgeR and DESeq2. What tool can produce interactive HTML plots from these results?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `Glimma`. It produces interactive htmlwidgets versions of popular RNA-seq analysis plots directly from the outputs of limma, edgeR, or DESeq2.

---

## [448] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on an NMR metabolomics dataset generated on a Bruker spectrometer. Which tool can read these raw Bruker files?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use NMR_Read, which reads Nuclear Magnetic Resonance Bruker files and originates from the PEPS-NMR R package.

---

## [449] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I want to perform quality control and evaluate read clipping patterns on my RNA-seq alignment files (BAM/SAM). What program can estimate this?

**ASSISTANT:** Use 'Clipping Profile'. It is specifically designed for RNA-seq quality control workflows and read mapping assessment to estimate the clipping profile of reads from SAM or BAM files containing soft-clipped ('S') mappings.

---

## [450] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on metabolic network structural analysis and need a tool to create compound graphs from SBML models while merging compartments. What would you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend Sbml2CompoundGraph. It is designed for metabolomics and systems biology applications, specifically metabolic network structural and graph-based topology analysis, and supports merging compartments using unambiguous names or compartment-suffixed identifiers.

---

## [451] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a DNAStringSet alignment and want to perform codon-based analysis and calculate pairwise distances using custom score matrices. Which tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** MSA2dist is suited for this analysis. It takes DNAStringSet or AAStringSet inputs, utilizes custom scoring matrices to calculate pairwise distances across all sequences, and conducts codon-based analysis.

---

## [452] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on fungal genome annotation and need a pipeline to compare annotations across genomes. What tool would you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend 'Funannotate compare'. It is part of the Funannotate genome annotation pipeline, built specifically for fungi (while also supporting higher eukaryotes), and is designed for comparative genome annotation analysis.

---

## [453] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have predicted plant protein sequences and need to identify transcription factors and classify them into families according to the PlantTFDB schema. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'planttfhunter' (Classification, FunctionalGenomics, FunctionalPrediction). It identifies plant transcription factors from protein sequence data and classifies them into families and subfamilies using the PlantTFDB classification scheme and pre-built hidden Markov model profiles.

---

## [454] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on Ligase Chain Reaction (LCR) based DNA assembly and need a tool to help design bridging oligos for assembling my synthetic DNA parts. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Ligase Chain Reaction Genie (LCR Genie). It is specifically designed to support the design of bridging oligos needed for annealing individual synthetic DNA parts into multi-gene plasmid assemblies.

---

## [455] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am setting up a whole genome sequencing alignment pipeline and need a Data Manager utility to prepare index files for my FASTA reference genome. What Picard tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Picard index', which serves as a data manager to create reference indices for FASTA files in genomic sequencing workflows.

---

## [456] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a multi-study meta-analysis of microbial communities using shotgun metagenomics and 16S data, and I need a tool to handle batch correction and meta-analysis across feature abundance tables. What tool fits this?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `mmuphin`. It is tailored for metagenomics, microbial community profiling, 16S rRNA gene sequencing, and shotgun metagenomics, specifically supporting multi-study meta-analyses using feature abundance matrices (such as taxa or gene abundance across samples).

---

## [457] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have generated multiple individual taxonomic profile outputs from sylphtax/taxprof during a metagenomic analysis. What tool can I use to combine them into one table?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `Sylphtax Merge`. It specifically merges multiple taxonomic profiles generated by sylphtax/taxprof into a single TSV table.

---

## [458] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on microscopy-based phenotyping and need a Galaxy tool to perform cell counting and morphology assessment. What tool would you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'Perform YOLO image labeling' tool in Galaxy. It is designed for microscopy-based phenotyping, cell counting, and morphology assessment, utilizing a pre-trained YOLOv8 model from Ultralytics to perform object detection or instance segmentation on your images.

---

## [459] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have multiple assembled genomes and want to visualize syntenic blocks and structural rearrangements across chromosomes. What tool is suitable for this?

## [460] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We generated high-throughput barcode sequencing data in a synthetic biology project and need to extract the barcodes, quantify their frequencies, and determine lineage relationships. Which tool should we use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Biomni: Analyze Barcode Sequencing Data' from the synthetic_biology category. It is specifically designed to analyze sequencing data to extract, quantify, and determine lineage relationships of barcodes.

---

## [461] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool that extends Porechop to handle adapter sequence processing in Oxford Nanopore reads.

---

## [462] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have gas chromatography-mass spectrometry (GC-MS) data processed with xcms from a comparative metabolomics experiment with multiple sample classes. What tool can I use to plot the TIC and BPI chromatograms?

---

## [463] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with PIMA and need a tool to generate Circos visualization plots for my analysis. What tool is available?

---

## [464] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a list of genomic coordinates and want to filter out any entries that do not meet a certain length threshold. Which tool can filter coordinates or sequences by size?

---

## [465] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on transcriptomics data and need a tool for Single Cell Fluidigm spot detection, classification, and clustering. What do you recommend?

## [466] source=multitask_sft_big

## [467] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have segmented biological structures in my microscopy images and need to benchmark the accuracy of the segmentation masks. Which tool should I use?

## [468] source=multitask_sft_big

## [469] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to evaluate microsatellite instability (MSI) across homopolymers using paired tumor and normal sequencing data. Which tool is suitable?

---

## [470] source=multitask_sft_big

## [471] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on de novo genome assembly quality assessment and need to calculate k-mer frequency distribution using a precomputed k-mer countgraph. What tool should I run?

---

## [472] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have deep sequencing RNA data and need a tool that can map reads to the reference genome and discover both known and novel miRNAs. What do you suggest?

---

## [473] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am performing cell segmentation on fluorescence microscopy images and need to adjust an automatically determined threshold by adding an offset. Which tool is designed for this?

---

## [474] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I just completed variant calling on a whole exome sequencing dataset and want to evaluate standard QC metrics such as Ti/Tv ratios, percentage of variants in dbSNP, and genotype concordance. What tool should I use?

---

## [475] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We have accumulated huge volumes of raw FASTQ sequencing files and need a tool to perform fast, lossless compression on them.

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using 'Spring Compress'. It provides fast, efficient, and lossless compression tailored specifically for FASTQ files.

---

## [476] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to rename my sequencing reads using a custom prefix followed by a numerical counter, while making sure paired-end read designations are kept intact. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Rename sequences' tool. It renames sequences with a numerical counter while preserving a sequence name prefix, and it uses the last character in the sequence name to distinguish pairs when paired-end reads are provided.

---

## [477] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool that can download an mmseqs-formatted database to prepare for sequence indexing and clustering. What do you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the tool 'Mmseqs Databases'. It is specifically designed to download an mmseqs-formatted database for indexing, clustering, and searching workflows.

---

## [478] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a multi-sample structural variant discovery study and need a tool to merge SVs across samples. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** JasmineSV (Jointly Accurate Sv Merging with Intersample Network Edges) is specifically designed for merging structural variants (SVs) across multiple samples in structural variant discovery and population-level genotyping studies.

---

## [479] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have metabolomics results with identification and quantification data stored in mzTab-M format. Which tool should I use to verify format compliance?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'mztab-m validate', which checks mzTab-M files for format compliance using the jmztab-m validator.

---

## [480] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on data-dependent acquisition (DDA) LC-MS proteomics workflows and need a tool specifically tailored for decharging mass spectra.

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Decharger, which is designed for proteomics applications, particularly liquid chromatography-mass spectrometry (LC-MS) experiments and data-dependent acquisition (DDA) workflows.

---

## [481] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working in R/Bioconductor and need to query multiple annotation packages that have completely different database schemas. Is there a meta-framework tool that unifies queries across them?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `OrganismDbi` package. It provides a simple, unified meta-framework interface across multiple annotation packages with different schemas by leveraging the standard `select` method implemented across them.

---

## [482] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need an R package to execute Multiple Sample Peak Calling (MSPC) on my ChIP-seq peak datasets. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `rmspc` package, which runs MSPC (Multiple Sample Peak Calling) software directly within R.

---

## [483] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a bioinformatics tool that can perform motif matching, counting, and motif enrichment using position frequency matrices, specifically one that accounts for self-overlapping motifs and supports higher-order background models. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **motifcounter**. It provides motif matching, motif counting, and motif enrichment functionality based on position frequency matrices (PFMs), with distinct features that account for self-overlapping motifs and incorporate higher-order background models.

---

## [484] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with single-cell RNA-seq pseudotime trajectories and want to identify genes that exhibit sharp, switch-like activation or repression along cell differentiation paths. Which tool is tailored for this?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `switchde`. It is specifically designed for single-cell transcriptomics trajectory analysis to detect and infer switch-like differential gene expression across pseudotime or developmental paths.

---

## [485] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am clustering thousands of viral contigs and scaffolds, but pairwise alignment is computationally too expensive. Is there a tool that filters out dissimilar pairs before alignment?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use `Vclust Prefilter`. It creates a pre-alignment filter that reduces the candidate genome pairs by filtering out dissimilar contigs and scaffolds prior to full alignment.

---

## [486] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am designing a pre-processing step for raw FASTQ reads in a metagenomics project and need to discard reads with mean or median quality scores below a given threshold. What tool fits this purpose?

**ASSISTANT:** The `Quality control` tool in Sequence Analysis is specifically designed for this. It filters out reads whose mean or median quality score does not meet the specified cutoff (which defaults to 30).

---

## [487] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are running molecular dynamics trajectories to analyze protein conformational dynamics and need to compute and visualize dihedral angles between four specific atoms. Which tool should we use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Dihedral Analysis' tool from computational chemistry/structural biology. It calculates and plots the dihedral angle defined between two intersecting planes across four specified atoms.

---

## [488] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting transcriptomics analysis and want to test gene sets not only for mean differential expression but also for shifts in scale and net correlation structure. What tool supports this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use GSAR (Gene Set Analysis in R). It is a statistical software package designed for transcriptomics that tests gene sets under specific alternative hypotheses, evaluating differential expression, scale, and net correlation structure.

---

## [489] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I want to perform SVM-based classification and regression on amino acid and nucleotide sequence data using specialized biological sequence kernels. What package do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend the 'kebabs' package. It provides kernel-based analysis of DNA, RNA, and amino acid sequences using SVM-based methods with built-in kernels such as spectrum, mismatch, gappy pair, and motif kernels.

---

## [490] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have generated a recalibration table for my variant dataset using Sentieon. Which tool should I use to apply the score cutoff and filter the variants?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Sentieon Applyvarcal`, which takes the recalibration table and applies the necessary filtering score cutoff to your variants.

---

## [491] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need general utilities in R for metagenomics and microbiome analysis. What tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** The `microbiome` tool (microbiome R package) provides utilities specifically for microbiome analysis within metagenomics and genomics.

---

## [492] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a phylogenetics dataset and want to find which codon sites in my gene are experiencing pervasive diversifying selection across the phylogeny. Which tool is designed for this?

**ASSISTANT:** For identifying sites subject to pervasive (i.e., consistently across the entire phylogeny) diversifying selection, you should use HyPhy-FUBAR.

---

## [493] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tools are incorporated in the MsBackendMassbank pipeline for managing mass spectrometry library spectra?

**ASSISTANT:** The workflow incorporates the following tools: bioconductor/msbackendmassbank, bioconductor/spectra, bioconductor/mscoreutils, bioconductor/rsqlite, bioconductor/rmariadb, and bioconductor/biocstyle.

---

## [494] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing NanoString GeoMx spatial data and want to overlay visualization layers like tissue segments and gene expression directly onto an OME-TIFF image. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use SpatialOmicsOverlay. It is designed for NanoString Technologies GeoMx data to easily graph annotations—ranging from tissue segments to gene expression—directly on top of an OME-TIFF image.

---

## [495] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have protein FASTA files and need to convert them into a CSV format compatible with Prosit for peptide analysis. What tool is available?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the Proteomics tool 'ConvertFastaToPrositCSV', which converts FASTA sequence files into the Prosit CSV format.

---

## [496] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to annotate and retrieve glycan structures from KEGG for glycoprotein proteomics and glycolipid lipidomics studies. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'GAP Get KEGG glycans' web service tool, which supports glycan structure retrieval and annotation across glycomics, proteomics (glycoprotein analysis), and lipidomics (glycolipid analysis).

---

## [497] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a genome-wide study on DNA methylation, including CpG islands and batch effect adjustments. What tool facilitates this comprehensive analysis?

**ASSISTANT:** RnBeads is recommended. It facilitates comprehensive analysis of various types of DNA methylation data at the genome scale and addresses CpG island annotations and batch effects.

---

## [498] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a standard bench protocol to anneal two complementary DNA oligonucleotides, but I do not want to perform a phosphorylation reaction. Which tool provides this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the Biomni tool 'Get Oligo Annealing Protocol' (molecular_biology), which returns a standard protocol for annealing oligonucleotides without phosphorylation.

---

## [499] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on an outbreak investigation involving Salmonella enterica serovars Heidelberg and Enteritidis. Which tool can perform in-silico subtyping on my whole-genome sequencing reads using 33 bp k-mer schemes?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Salmonella Subtyping' tool. It subtypes Salmonella enterica subsp. enterica serovar Heidelberg and Enteritidis genomes using in-silico 33 bp k-mer SNP subtyping schemes developed by Genevieve Labbe et al., supporting whole-genome sequencing reads and FASTA assemblies.

---

## [500] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** During the assembly preprocessing stage, I need to filter out contaminant reads by aligning them to a contaminant template using RTG. What tool handles this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use Mapf, which filters reads for contaminant sequences by mapping them against the contaminant template using rtg mapf.

---

## [501] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with metagenomics data and need to group similar sequences into OTUs based on a similarity percentage. What tool can I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'Perform OTU picking' tool under the Metagenomics domain, which clusters sequences into operational taxonomic units based on your specified similarity threshold.

---

## [502] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with single-cell mRNA sequencing data from an apparently homogeneous population of cells. I need an R tool that uses a Bayesian framework to quantify cell-to-cell expression heterogeneity while explicitly accounting for strong technical noise. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend BASiCS (Bayesian Analysis of Single-Cell Sequencing data). It is specifically tailored for single-cell transcriptomics to uncover cell-to-cell gene expression heterogeneity in seemingly homogeneous populations while modeling and decomposing high levels of technical noise.

---

## [503] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on gene function prediction using network connectivity and guilt-by-association approaches. Can you recommend a tool tailored for this?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `EGAD`. It provides highly efficient tools specifically designed to calculate functional properties of networks using guilt-by-association methods for gene and functional prediction.

---

## [504] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool for analyzing and comparing multiple related RNA-Seq experiments. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend Sleuth, which is specifically designed for the analysis and comparison of multiple related RNA-Seq experiments.

---

## [505] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an observation NetCDF file and need to generate an ocean climatology via spatial interpolation. Which ecology tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `DIVAnd`. It is designed to take an observation NetCDF file and create climatology through spatial interpolation of ocean observations.

---

## [506] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which machine learning tool can I use to identify sample similarities and cluster them using scikit-learn algorithms?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'Numeric Clustering' tool, which provides clustering algorithms from scikit-learn to identify similarities among samples and cluster them accordingly.

---

## [507] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with transcription factor ChIP-exo data from a non-model organism and need a peak caller that determines binding sites at high resolution without requiring species-specific parameter tuning. What tool do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `Peakzilla (ChIP-seq)`. It is specifically designed to identify enrichment and transcription factor binding sites from ChIP-seq and ChIP-exo experiments at high resolution and accuracy, performing equally well across any species without needing species-specific parameter adjustments.

---

## [508] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have FASTA sequences from a metagenomics project, but my downstream analysis tool requires FASTQ formatted files with quality scores. Which tool can handle this format conversion?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the FASTA-to-FASTQ tool. It converts FASTA files into FASTQ files by adding a static quality score (defaulting to 40 for each base).

---

## [509] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have high-redundancy PacBio HiFi reads from a complex microbial community. Which de novo metagenome assembler is suited for this data?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Hifiasm_meta`. It is a de novo metagenome assembler derived from hifiasm designed specifically for PacBio HiFi reads across metagenomics and microbial community genomics projects, particularly on high-redundancy datasets.

---

## [510] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have single-cell immune profiling data generated with BD and MiXCR for TCR and BCR repertoires. Which toolkit can analyze these single-cell immune receptor formats?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `scRepertoire`. It is specifically built for processing and analyzing single-cell T-cell receptor (TCR) and immunoglobulin (Ig) data and natively supports BD, MiXCR, 10x, AIRR, Omniscope, TRUST4, and WAT3R formats.

---

## [511] source=multitask_sft_big
