**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'CellRanger ATAC' wrapper in the Epigenetics domain, which was developed by scientists at GenAP to facilitate running CellRanger within Galaxy.

---

## [512] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working in genomics and need to simulate codon genetic sequences undergoing various degrees and patterns of Darwinian selection using Sewall Wright's fitness landscape paradigm. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `scoup`. It is a molecular evolutionary framework designed to simulate codon genetic sequences subjected to different degrees and/or patterns of Darwinian selection based on Sewall Wright's fitness landscape paradigm.

---

## [513] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which computational chemistry and structural biology tool can I use for protein mutation studies and structure-function analysis?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the Mutate tool (from biobb-model), which is designed for computational chemistry, structural biology, and protein engineering applications such as protein mutation studies.

---

## [514] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool can I use to project single-cell RNA-seq data onto a continuous cell cycle space using transfer learning?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `tricycle`. It is designed for single-cell transcriptomics to infer and visualize cell cycle processes by exploiting transfer learning, projecting new scRNA-seq data onto a pre-learned, biologically interpretable cell cycle space.

---

## [515] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are planning an RNA-seq experiment and want to simulate different sequencing depths by subsampling our high-throughput count data to evaluate statistical power for differential gene expression. Which tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using `subSeq`. It is specifically designed for subsampling high-throughput sequencing count data to support experiment design and differential expression analysis in genomics and transcriptomics.

---

## [516] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are studying X-chromosome inactivation patterns and need an LDA implementation tailored to binary classification of genomic data as in Carrel et al., 2006. What tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** The 'Perform LDA' tool is suited for this. It implements the Linear Discriminant Analysis module described in Carrel et al., 2006 (PMID: 17009873) and is applicable to binary classification problems in genomics and gene expression analysis, such as X-chromosome inactivation pattern studies.

---

## [517] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have tabular data from a multi-group comparative study and need to stratify the table by specific categorical variables. Which text manipulation tool can split it?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `csvtk-split` from the Text Manipulation suite. It is specifically suited for stratifying tabular data by categorical variables across multiple columns.

---

## [518] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool to evaluate whether my RNA-seq reads map primarily to coding regions, UTRs, introns, or ribosomal sequences. What tool can provide this distribution breakdown?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `CollectRnaSeqMetrics` (SAM). It calculates metrics describing the alignment of RNA-seq data across distinct functional genomic loci: coding, intronic, UTR, intergenic, and ribosomal regions.

---

## [519] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am managing a multi-sample RNA-seq project and have dozens of separate quality control outputs from various tools. Is there a tool that can aggregate all these results into a single web report?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use MultiQC. It aggregates results from bioinformatics analyses across multiple samples into a single report that can be viewed as an interactive web-page, making it suitable for multi-sample sequencing and RNA-seq quality control workflows.

---

## [520] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** In QIIME 2, I need to generate permuted categorical sample metadata groups from an existing column for validation testing. Which tool is suitable?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'qiime2 metadata shuffle-groups', which is specifically designed to create categorical metadata columns by shuffling the values of an input column.

---

## [521] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing ChIP-Seq data across multiple conditions and need to identify differential peaks. However, standard peak callers only focus on read counts, whereas I suspect our treatments induce differences in peak shapes. What tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use MMDiff2. It is specifically designed to detect statistically significant differences between read enrichment profiles in ChIP-Seq samples by using kernel methods (Maximum Mean Discrepancy, MMD) to account for differences in peak shape rather than relying solely on total read counts.

---

## [522] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We used OpenSWATH to process our DIA proteomics run, but now we need an R tool to perform FDR estimation, filtering, and format conversion for downstream statistical analysis packages. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `SWATH2stats`. It is specifically designed to take SWATH data generated by OpenSWATH and apply annotation, filtering, and FDR estimation, formatting the data so it can be seamlessly ingested by downstream statistical packages.

---

## [523] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** My pipeline produced SAM/BAM alignment files from a whole genome sequencing run that need standard fix-ups. Which tool can read these files and perform the cleanups?

**ASSISTANT:** CleanSam is designed for this task. It reads SAM or BAM files and performs various fix-ups on the alignment data.

---

## [524] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have called variants in a sample and want to benchmark my callset against a high-confidence gold standard truth VCF. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Gatk4 Concordance`. This tool is specifically designed to evaluate the concordance of an input evaluation VCF against a validated truth VCF.

---

## [525] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool can I use to demultiplex raw Illumina sequencing reads from a RAD-seq library and check barcode and cutsite integrity?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Stacks: process radtags'. It inspects raw Illumina reads to verify barcodes and RAD cutsites, demultiplexes samples, and can correct errors within allowed tolerances.

---

## [526] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a set of FASTA headers from a metagenomics project that need batch renaming and standardization based on a mapping table. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Change fasta header' tool (Fasta Manipulation). It is specifically suited for batch renaming and sequence header standardization across genomics, transcriptomics, proteomics, and metagenomics datasets using a two-column replacement table.

---

## [527] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to build a variation graph combining my reference FASTA genome and variant calls from a VCF file. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Vg Construct`. It is designed to construct sequence graphs directly from a reference file and variant calls (such as a VCF) or from a multiple sequence alignment file.

---

## [528] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on bibliometric analysis in bioinformatics and need a tool dedicated to processing and analyzing co-citation data. What tool do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend CoCiteStats. It is a collection of software tools specifically designed for dealing with and analyzing co-citation data in bioinformatics.

---

## [529] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a large multi-sequence FASTA file and need to pull out a subset of specific sequences for downstream analysis. What tool can handle this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use 'Fasta Extract Sequence' from the Fasta Manipulation suite, which is designed to extract specific sequences from multi-sequence FASTA files.

---

## [530] source=multitask_sft_big

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Biomni: Quantify Corneal Nerve Fibers`, which is designed to quantify the volume and density of immunofluorescence-labeled corneal nerve fibers.

---

## [531] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have metagenomic shotgun sequencing data and want species-level taxonomic composition including bacteria, viruses, archaea, and eukaryotes. What should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use MetaPhlAn2. It performs microbial community profiling from metagenomic shotgun sequencing data at species-level resolution across Bacteria, Archaea, Eukaryotes, and Viruses.

---

## [532] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a quality control tool that applies sliding-window quality trimming to both ends of my paired-end RNA-seq reads. Which tool matches this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use 'Quality trim paired-end reads with Sickle (Sequence Analysis)', which performs sliding-window quality and length trimming on paired-end reads for RNA-seq and genomic datasets.

---

## [533] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have numeric expression measurements across several samples from a transcriptomics study and want to plot a standard R heatmap. Which tool is suitable?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend the **Heatmap** tool under Graphics. It uses the `heatmap` function from the R statistical package to plot heatmaps from numeric data values contained in the columns of your dataset.

---

## [534] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have proteomics results stored in an SQLite database and need to convert them to JSON. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use JSONExporter. It is designed for proteomics workflows that require data format conversion from SQLite to JSON.

---

## [535] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** How can I quickly check summary metadata, such as sequence count, total sequence length, and creation date, for a local BLAST database before running alignment?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the `NCBI BLAST+ database info` tool. It inspects BLAST databases using `blastdbcmd -info` and returns summary statistics including sequence count, total length, and database date.

---

## [536] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a genomics quality control tool that can evaluate read length, Phred scores, alphabet frequency, and DNA k-mers directly on both compressed and uncompressed FASTQ files. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend seqTools. It is designed for genomics quality control and can analyze read length, Phred scores, alphabet frequency, and DNA k-mers on both uncompressed and compressed FASTQ files.

---

## [537] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an unsorted SAM file and need per-scaffold coverage metrics without spending time sorting it first. What tool should I run?

---

## [538] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have completed several LC-MS/MS proteomics runs and want to perform exploratory quality control and detect possible batch effects. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `msmsEDA`. It is specifically designed for exploratory data analysis to assess the quality of LC-MS/MS experiments and visualize the influence of experimental factors such as batch effects.

---

## [539] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with single-cell RNA-seq data and want to identify which specific cells have active expression of predefined gene signatures. What tool is recommended?

**ASSISTANT:** AUCell is recommended for this task. It is designed for single-cell transcriptomics to identify individual cells showing active gene sets, modules, or signatures.

---

## [540] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I evaluated a set of metagenomic samples using a QIIME 2 sample classifier. I need a tool to generate a confusion matrix and calculate accuracy between the predicted and true sample values. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `qiime2 sample-classifier confusion-matrix`. It computes accuracy and constructs a confusion matrix comparing true versus predicted classes for samples evaluated with a sample classifier.

---

## [541] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a fragmented de novo genome assembly and need to order and orient the contigs against a reference genome. What assembly tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use RaGOO. It is an assembly tool designed for genomics workflows, specifically for de novo genome assembly improvement, contig ordering and orientation using a reference genome, and scaffolding fragmented assemblies.

---

## [542] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have de novo assembled plant transcripts and need to process them into putative coding sequences for evolutionary gene family analysis. Which tool from PlantTribes handles this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `AssemblyPostProcessor` from the PlantTribes suite. It is designed specifically to post-process de novo assembled transcripts into putative coding sequences.

---

## [543] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have mapped paired NGS reads from a Hi-C experiment and need to identify distal genomic interactions that occur significantly more frequently than expected by chance. Which tool fits this analysis?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `GOTHiC`. It is a Hi-C analysis package that takes mapped paired NGS reads as input and applies a cumulative binomial test to detect significant interactions between distal genomic loci.

---

## [544] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing genomic reassortment in viruses and need a tool that can help me visualize these events. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `seqcombo`. It is categorized under Alignment, Software, and Visualization, and it provides specialized functions for visualizing virus reassortment events.

---

## [545] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have acquired untargeted GC-MS metabolomics profiles and need to search reference mass spectra to identify biologically active metabolites. What tool should I choose?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `Golm Metabolome Database search spectrum` tool. It facilitates searching against reference mass spectra from the Golm Metabolome Database (GMD) to annotate and identify biologically active metabolites quantified via gas chromatography-mass spectrometry (GC-MS).

---

## [546] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have overlapping paired-end FASTQ reads from an amplicon sequencing experiment and I want to merge each pair into a single read. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use Bbmap Bbmerge. It is specifically designed for overlap-based merging of paired FASTQ reads into single reads.

---

## [547] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing Hi-C sequencing data across three experimental conditions, with multiple replicates per condition. Which tool can handle joint normalization and differential interaction detection for this setup?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend 'multiHiCcompare'. It provides joint normalization and difference detection for multi-group Hi-C experiments containing more than two groups and multiple samples per group.

---

## [548] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool to perform hierarchical cell type annotation on my human single-cell genomic data using neural network models. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using 'Biomni: Annotate Celltype With Panhumanpy'. It is designed for genomics data to perform hierarchical cell type annotation using panhumanpy and the Azimuth Neural Network.

---

## [549] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a whole-exome sequencing (WES) project and need a tool for variant annotation. What tool fits this primary omics area?

**ASSISTANT:** For variant annotation in Whole Exome Sequencing (WES) studies, 'SnpSift Annotate' is well-suited. It is categorized under genomics and variant analysis.

---

## [550] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We conducted a crystal violet staining assay in our microbiology lab to measure biofilm accumulation. Which tool can analyze this data?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `quantify_biofilm_biomass_crystal_violet`. It is specifically built for microbiology experiments to quantify biofilm biomass from crystal violet staining assay data.

---

## [551] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on proteomics data and need to search phage sequences without encountering excessive runtimes. What tool and strategy should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use PhageCOAST - Search for proteomics analysis. When setting up your run, be sure to utilize taxonomic filters, as proteome-wide queries are sensitive to proteome and database size and can otherwise result in slow searches.

---

## [552] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool to calculate the proportion of G and C bases for sequence composition analysis and quality control in a metagenomics study. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Compute GC content' tool, which is suitable for sequence composition analysis, quality control of DNA/RNA sequences, and genome characterization studies in genomics and metagenomics.

---

## [553] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool can analyze hybrid capture targeted DNA sequencing to normalize coverage, segment the genome, call copy number alterations, and determine tumor purity and ploidy?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Purecn Run`. It executes the PureCN workflow to normalize, segment, determine purity and ploidy, and call copy number alterations from targeted DNA sequencing.

---

## [554] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have tabular quality control metrics from a genomics study and want to filter out rows based on a numeric cutoff like 'frequency>4'. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `csvtk-filter`. It allows you to specify column names or numbers and evaluate numeric expressions (such as 'frequency>4') across tabular datasets.

---

## [555] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have short Illumina DNA sequencing reads (100-150 bp) and need to align them to a protein reference database like NCBI-NR much faster than BLASTX. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use DIAMOND. It is designed for aligning short DNA sequencing reads against protein reference databases such as NCBI-NR and operates around 20,000 times faster than BLASTX in fast mode on 100–150 bp reads.

---

## [556] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We have collected DNA methylation profiles from pediatric tumor samples and need an easy-to-use classifier to assign them to biologically defined subtypes. What tool can we use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use MethPed. It is specifically developed as a diagnostic classifier toolbox that uses DNA methylation profiles to classify pediatric tumors into biologically defined subtypes.

---

## [557] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are running cancer transcriptome RNA-Seq analysis and need a fast method to detect aberrant transcripts and gene fusions from chimeric alignments generated by STAR. What tool fits this?

**ASSISTANT:** Arriba (specifically Arriba Get Filters) is tailored for this use case. It is designed to quickly detect aberrant transcripts and gene fusions using chimeric alignments produced by the STAR RNA-Seq aligner.

---

## [558] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have annotated VCF files from a whole exome sequencing (WES) study and need to build a GEMINI database for querying rare variants. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `vcf2db`. It is designed to take annotated VCF files and generate GEMINI-compatible SQLite databases, fitting genomic variant analysis workflows such as WES and WGS.

---

## [559] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to calculate steady-state metabolic reaction fluxes on my reconstructed genome-scale metabolic network model. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Biomni: Perform Flux Balance Analysis' from the systems_biology domain, which performs Flux Balance Analysis (FBA) on genome-scale metabolic network models.

---

## [560] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Is there a tool available for single-cell transcriptomics that can represent and summarize the entire single-cell profile of an individual sample for sample-level quality control?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, `GloScope` is designed for this purpose. It summarizes the entire single-cell profile of a sample, enabling sample-level bioinformatic analyses such as visualization, quality control, and estimating sample distances or distributions.

---

## [561] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool can correlate two sets of high-dimensional transcriptomic/genetic probe-level data with multiple clinical endpoints?

**ASSISTANT:** CCPROMISE is recommended. It handles probe-level analysis and performs canonical correlation between two high-dimensional genetic data forms to detect associations with multiple endpoints.

---

## [562] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which sequence analysis tool can process a BED file together with its specified genome build to generate sequence profiles?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Sequence Profiles' tool, which takes a BED file and the file's corresponding genome build as its inputs.

---

## [563] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have two separate FastQ files containing paired-end reads and need to interleave them into a single file. Which tool can do this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `Seqtk Mergepe`. It is built to interleave paired-end reads from FastQ files.

---

## [564] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a proteomics search pipeline in OpenMS and need a tool that can run X! Tandem with a default XML configuration file while overriding specific parameters directly from the interface.

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `XTandemAdapter`. It integrates X! Tandem searches into OpenMS, supports custom or default configuration files (such as `OpenMS/share/CHEMISTRY/XTandem_default_config.xml`), and ensures adapter parameters take precedence over configuration file settings.

---

## [565] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a simple text manipulation tool to extract only the last 50 lines of a large tabular dataset. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `Select last` tool from the Text Manipulation suite, which outputs a specified number of lines from the end of a dataset.

---

## [566] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool for pathway abundance comparisons across samples in a microbial community profiling study using metatranscriptomic data. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend 'Barplot (Metagenomics)'. It is applicable to metatranscriptomics and metagenomics, and is suited for microbial community profiling studies and pathway abundance comparisons across samples.

---

## [567] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We have two sequencing datasets from two samples and need to discover structural variations both within and across them. What tool can accomplish this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use lumpy-sv. It specializes in structural variation analysis and is designed to search for structural variations inside and across two sequencing datasets from two samples.

---

## [568] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am experiencing severe memory and performance issues when running SPARQL queries across my genome annotations. Is there an alternative option designed to handle this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `MATSPARQL`. It is designed specifically for situations where you encounter memory or performance problems by querying each genome independently.

---

## [569] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need an R package to monitor cloud compute expenditures and categorize task expenses for our projects on the AnVIL/Terra platform. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend AnVILBilling. It is an R package designed to monitor AnVIL-related costs by querying daily exported BigQuery billing tables, categorizing tasks and expenditures, and visualizing expense profiles over time.

---

## [570] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are conducting an ImmunoOncology study and need an R/Bioconductor package dedicated to preprocessing and analyzing protein mass spectrometry data. What tool fits this purpose?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `PROcess`. It is designed for processing protein mass spectrometry data within proteomics and metabolomics applications, including ImmunoOncology studies.

---

## [571] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have run Kraken2 to classify reads from metagenomic samples, but I need to re-estimate species and taxonomic abundance accurately. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Bracken (Bayesian Re-estimation of Abundance with KrakEN). It is specifically designed to re-estimate taxonomic abundance of metagenomic samples analyzed by Kraken or Kraken2.

---

## [572] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a GTF file and need to convert it into a HOMER-style annotation file for annotatePeaks. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Converts GTF to Annotations file for Homer (Sequence Analysis)`. It converts a GTF file into a HOMER-style annotation file compatible with `annotatePeaks`.

---

## [573] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool in the single-cell suite should I use if I need to produce visual embeddings colored by metadata columns in .obs or gene expression from .var_names?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Scanpy plot' tool for Single Cell data, which allows you to color plots using observation annotations (`.obs`), variable annotations (`.var`), or gene expression (`.var_names`).

---

## [574] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am designing a tiered metagenomics survey study and need to select samples efficiently to reduce costs. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the microPITA 'Run' tool in the Metagenomics domain, which is specifically designed for sample selection in tiered studies to minimize costs and maximize sample efficiency.

---

## [575] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We received an expression matrix with unidentified ID strings and need a function in our genomics workflow to check if they conform to Ensembl identifier syntax. What tool does this?

**ASSISTANT:** Use `check_ensembl_patterns`. This tool is specifically designed to evaluate whether gene identifiers conform to standard Ensembl naming conventions.

---

## [576] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a client tool to query RNA-seq expression count data by genomic region or gene name using the Snaptron web services. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `snapcount`. It serves as a client interface to Snaptron webservices, allowing queries by gene name or genomic coordinates to retrieve raw expression counts from RNA-seq sample alignments and summarized expression metrics.

---

## [577] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have single-cell Hi-C contact matrices saved in a .scool file that need matrix balancing and normalization. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `scHicCorrectMatrices`. It is specifically designed to correct all Hi-C matrices stored in a `.scool` file using HiCExplorer's Knight-Ruiz (KR) algorithm.

---

## [578] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with an OBO ontology and need to extract all descendant IDs under a specific term ID. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Get the descendent terms' tool from the Ontology Manipulation suite. It retrieves the descendent terms as a list of IDs starting from a specified existing term ID in an OBO ontology.

---

## [579] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have variant calls saved in BCF format, but my downstream tools require standard uncompressed or text VCF format. What utility should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use bcftools view from the SAMtools/BCFtools suite to convert your BCF file into VCF format.

---

## [580] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with metabolomics metadata structured in the Investigation / Study / Assay (ISA) tab-delimited format. Which tool should I use for data import and annotation?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Risa`. It supports the Investigation / Study / Assay (ISA) tab-delimited format for managing, annotating, and importing metadata in metabolomics and mass spectrometry workflows.

---

## [581] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have collected continuous blood pressure recordings from a physiological study and need an automated method to derive hemodynamic indices. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `analyze_hemodynamic_data`. It is specifically designed for the physiology domain to analyze raw blood pressure data and calculate key hemodynamic parameters.

---

## [582] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We just ran a multiplexed quantitative proteomics experiment using TMT tags and need a tool to analyze the isobaric reporter ion intensities. Which tool fits this analysis?

**ASSISTANT:** The recommended tool is `IsobaricAnalyzer`, which specifically supports quantitative proteomics designs utilizing isobaric labeling methods like TMT and iTRAQ.

---

## [583] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have completed a draft genome assembly using long reads, but I need to polish it using high-accuracy short reads. What tool is suited for this task?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Pypolca Run', a genomics short read polisher specifically designed for polishing long read assemblies.

---

## [584] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an updated genome assembly for my species and need to map gene models from an existing GFF3 annotation file onto it. Which tool is recommended for this?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using 'Liftoff'. It is specifically designed to accurately map gene annotations in GFF or GTF format between assemblies of the same or closely related species.

---

## [585] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool for genome annotation analysis to identify essential genes in a single condition based on TA insertion sites using a Gumbel-based method. What tool do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `TRANSIT Tn5Gaps`. It is categorized under Genome Annotation and is loosely based on the Gumbel analysis method to determine gene essentiality in a single condition via gene-by-gene analysis of TA site insertions.

---

## [586] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on transcriptomics and proteomics datasets in Galaxy and need an interactive environment where I can run custom R scripts and generate exploratory visualizations. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the RStudio Interactive Tool in Galaxy. It provides a dedicated environment for statistical analysis, data visualization, and exploratory scripting in R across genomics, transcriptomics, proteomics, and general bioinformatics workflows.

---

## [587] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have short-read DNA sequence data and need an alignment tool that optimizes memory usage and speed for genomic alignment. What tool would you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Align to the Genome with Bowtie'. It is specifically engineered to be an ultrafast and memory-efficient aligner for short DNA reads against reference genomes.

---

## [588] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We have 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing ordinations from two different methods where only a subset of samples are paired. Which QIIME 2 plugin can transform one ordination into the other?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **qiime2 diversity partial-procrustes**. It transforms one ordination into another using the paired samples as transformation anchors without requiring complete sample pairing across both datasets.

---

## [589] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am designing a metabolomics LC-MS/MS data-dependent acquisition experiment and want to calculate the anticipated purity of precursor ions based on XCMS features. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `msPurity.purityX`. It calculates the anticipated precursor ion purity of selected precursors using XCMS-determined features from a prior LC-MS dataset.

---

## [590] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool that can perform highly sensitive protein sequence searches using hidden Markov model alignments. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Hhsuite Hhsearch. It is specifically designed for sensitive protein sequence searching based on the pairwise alignment of hidden Markov models (HMMs).

---

## [591] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have completed an RNA-Seq experiment and have a list of differentially expressed (DE) genes. I want an R-based tool to perform network enrichment and gene set analysis to interpret the biological pathways. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend 'pathlinkR'. It is an R package specifically built to facilitate the analysis of RNA-Seq results by taking a list of differentially expressed (DE) genes and performing network and gene set enrichment analyses to aid in biological interpretation.

---

## [592] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which web services tool can I use to retrieve CFG array data for glycan array screening studies?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use 'GAP Get CFG array', a web service tool tailored for retrieving CFG array data for glycomics and glycan array studies.

---

## [593] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with Xenium spatial transcriptomics data in Bioconductor and need a lightweight class that uses SpatialExperiment for assay/sample data and parquet for coordinates. Which tool or package provides this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `xenLite`. It defines a relatively lightweight class in Bioconductor for managing Xenium data, utilizing parquet for spatial coordinates, `SpatialExperiment` for assay and sample data, and addressing serialization and cloud storage.

---

## [594] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool that can parse SBML or BioPAX models to construct genome-scale biological networks and detect active paths across different representations.

**ASSISTANT:** You can use NetPathMiner. It is a framework designed for network path mining using genome-scale networks, capable of constructing network graphs from SBML, BioPAX, or KGML files and identifying active paths.

---

## [595] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have high-dimensional experimental data containing missing values and noticeable batch effects. I need an adjustment tool that can perform corrections efficiently using parallelization. What tool fits this?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend BERT. It provides efficient batch-effect adjustment for datasets with missing values. It organizes batch-effect corrections into a tree of pairwise computations, enabling parallel processing across sub-trees.

---

## [596] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have wide-format quantitative lipidomics data from a multi-group comparative study and want to evaluate feature distributions across samples. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Generate Distribution of Features within Samples. (Metabolomics)', which is suited for lipidomics, metabolomics, and proteomics wide-format data from multi-group comparative designs.

---

## [597] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We just ran graphclust_align_cluster on RNA structures and now need to aggregate and filter the alignment metrics across individual clusters. What tool should we use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Aggregate and filter alignment metrics' tool. It is specifically designed for RNA transcriptomics and structural RNA analysis to process and filter alignment metrics produced from individual clusters, such as those generated by `graphclust_align_cluster`.

---

## [598] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have metagenomic sequencing reads and their matching BLAST output files. I need to convert them into RMA format for MEGAN taxonomic and functional analysis. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'MEGAN: Generate RMA files', which processes FASTA or FASTQ reads together with their associated BLAST files to produce MEGAN RMA files.

---

## [599] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to import and manipulate biological network models encoded in SBML directly in R. What package is suitable?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **SBMLR**. It belongs to the GraphAndNetwork/Pathways categories and provides an SBML interface directly within R.

---

## [600] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a multiple sequence alignment in FASTA format and need to compute a pairwise SNP distance matrix. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Snpdists`, which calculates a pairwise SNP distance matrix directly from a FASTA sequence alignment.

---

## [601] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are benchmarking a metagenomics analysis pipeline and need to simulate both shotgun and amplicon sequencing reads from known reference FASTA files. What tool fits this task?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Grinder. It is specifically built for sequence analysis workflows to generate simulated random shotgun and amplicon sequencing libraries from reference FASTA files.

---

## [602] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have RNA-seq sequencing reads and want to quantify transcript abundances quickly without doing a traditional full alignment. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Kallisto Quant'. It uses pseudoalignment to compute equivalence classes for reads and quantifies transcript abundances efficiently.

---

## [603] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am setting up an NGS quality control workflow for RNA-seq and need a FASTQ manipulation tool that scans reads from the end and trims them based on a minimum quality threshold.

**ASSISTANT:** You can use 'Trim By Quality'. It belongs to the Fastq Manipulation category and is tailored for NGS quality control and RNA-seq preprocessing, trimming reads from the end based on a minimum quality score and discarding sequences shorter than the specified minimum length.

---

## [604] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can you recommend a fast and scalable tool for pangenome analysis of bacterial genomes using GFF files?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use Panaroo Run, which is a fast and scalable tool specifically designed for bacterial pangenome analysis from GFF and alignment data.

---

## [605] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can you recommend a tool that can take FASTQ files and identify resistance and virulence markers using Ariba formatted databases?

**ASSISTANT:** Use 'Ariba Run'. It processes FASTQ reads directly against Ariba formatted databases to perform assembly and detection of resistance and virulence markers.

---

## [606] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have relatively short Illumina query sequences that I need to align to a large sequence database like the human reference genome. What tool is recommended?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Map with BWA for Illumina', a fast, lightweight mapper designed specifically to align relatively short sequence queries to large sequence databases such as the human reference genome.

---

## [607] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have aligned NGS sequencing data and need to call low-frequency variants efficiently using multithreading across multiple CPU cores. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Lofreq Callparallel. It is specifically designed for low-frequency variant calling and predicts variants using multiple processors in parallel.

---

## [608] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We have paired tumor and normal BAM files from a cancer genomics project and want to identify single nucleotide differences between them. What tool is designed for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Somatic Sniper'. It is specifically designed for cancer genomics and somatic variant detection to identify single nucleotide positions that differ between a tumor BAM and a matched normal BAM.

---

## [609] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am setting up a BLAST database construction pipeline in Galaxy using metagenomic FASTA files. What tool can I run before makeblastdb to avoid failure from strict warning handling?

**ASSISTANT:** Use 'fastaGroomerForMakeBlastdb'. It grooms FASTA sequences specifically for makeblastdb workflows, ensuring the input sequence formatting will not trigger the warnings that Galaxy treats as errors.

---

## [610] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am looking for tools in the Sequence Analysis category and noticed `aurora_skewer`. Is it indexed in the system?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, `aurora_skewer` is cataloged under the Sequence Analysis category in BioMate, although its registered entry currently has minimal documentation metadata.

---

## [611] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with RNA Pol II DamID-seq datasets processed through damidseq_pipeline and need to calculate average Pol II occupancy and FDR values. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Calculate polII occupancy (ChIP-seq)'. It is an Rscript tailored for RNA Pol II DamID datasets that processes `gatc.bedgraph` or `gatc.gff` files to calculate average Pol II occupancy and false discovery rates (FDR).

---

## [612] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am looking for a tool that can perform differential proportionality analysis on my logratio-transformed expression datasets across experimental groups. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Propr Propd'. It is specifically designed to perform differential proportionality analysis using logratio approaches on expression data.

---

## [613] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with bacterial genomic data and want to use RGI to predict antibiotic resistance, but I need to prepare the CARD database first. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Rgi Cardannotation`. It is designed to preprocess the CARD database specifically for RGI so you can predict antibiotic resistance from protein or nucleotide data.

---

## [614] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool provides a sigmoid function implementation for pharmacological modeling?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `sigmoid` tool, which provides sigmoid functions tailored for pharmacological modeling.

---

## [615] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool to compare a query sequence against a reference sequence to detect variations. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Biomni: Find Sequence Mutations' tool from the molecular_biology domain. It compares a query sequence against a reference sequence to identify mutations.

---

## [616] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with Coptr and need a component to map sequencing reads against our reference database. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Coptr Map`, which is specifically designed within the Coptr suite to map reads to the reference database.

---

## [617] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am designing a spatial ecological survey with in-situ sampling points and need to stratify aquatic ecosystem regions using shapefiles. Which tool matches this setup?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `AquaINFRA OGC API Processes`. It is tailored for ecology applications including spatial ecological surveys with in-situ sampling points and aquatic ecosystem monitoring with geographic stratification.

---

## [618] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have wide-format omics data and need to transform it into a long format where each unique variable and value combination is placed on its own line. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the **melt** function (from text/data manipulation libraries such as reshape). It transforms wide-format datasets into long format by placing each unique variable:value combination onto a single line.

---

## [619] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with RNA-Seq data from viroids (around 250-400 nt circular RNAs) and need a tool that handles everything from alignment to visualization. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend CircSeqAlignTk. It is specifically designed for end-to-end RNA-Seq data analysis of circular genome sequences, covering alignment to visualization, and primarily targets viroids consisting of 246–401 nt circular RNAs.

---

## [620] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have whole genome sequencing (WGS) data and suspect sample contamination. Is there a tool that can assess contamination using mitochondrial DNA?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use Haplocheck. It identifies contamination patterns in both mtDNA and WGS sequencing studies by analyzing mitochondrial DNA, and it can also serve as a proxy contamination tool for nDNA studies.

---

## [621] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have fungal ITS amplicon sequences containing neighbouring conserved ribosomal RNA genes. I need a tool to extract only the ITS1 and ITS2 variable regions for barcode analysis. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `ITSx`. It is an open-source software utility designed to extract the highly variable ITS1 and ITS2 subregions from ITS sequences by removing conserved flanking ribosomal genes.

---

## [622] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool from the SnpEff suite for genetic variant annotation and functional effect prediction on VCF files. What is available?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `Snpeff Download`, which is part of the SnpEff genetic variant annotation and functional effect prediction toolbox.

---

## [623] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have predicted protein sequences from a metagenomic assembly and want to annotate their functions using KEGG Orthology. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `KofamScan`. It is specifically designed for gene function annotation of predicted protein sequences from genomic or metagenomic assemblies using KEGG Orthology and hidden Markov models.

---

## [624] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have Illumina metagenomic sequencing data from an environmental microbial community and want to reconstruct SSU rRNAs to evaluate taxonomic composition. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `phyloflash` (available via nf-core). It is specifically built for Illumina (meta)genomic datasets to rapidly reconstruct SSU rRNAs and explore phylogenetic composition.

---

## [625] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to scan a large genome to find locally stable RNA secondary structures with a restricted base pair span. Which tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** RNALfold is specifically designed for this task. It computes locally stable RNA secondary structures with a defined maximal base pair span and has low memory and CPU requirements (O(n + L*L) memory and O(n*L*L) time), making genome-wide scans practical.

---

## [626] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with metagenomic assemblies (MAGs) and want to calculate polymorphic site rates over protein-coding genes. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Cmseq Polymut`, which is designed to calculate polymorphic site rates over protein-coding genes in assemblies and MAGs.

---

## [627] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting an RNA-seq study and want to detect differential transcript usage (DTU) as well as run a transcript usage QTL (tuQTL) analysis. Which tool provides frameworks for both?

**ASSISTANT:** DRIMSeq is designed for this purpose. It provides two frameworks: one for differential transcript usage analysis across experimental conditions and one for tuQTL analysis, both modeling transcript count data using a Dirichlet-multinomial distribution.

---

## [628] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with ChIP-seq data and need a statistical framework capable of performing peak detection with a flexible parametric mixture model for both one-sample and two-sample designs. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `MOSAiCS` (MOdel-based one and two Sample Analysis and Inference for ChIP-Seq Data). It provides a statistical framework that uses a flexible parametric mixture modeling approach to detect enriched peak regions in both one-sample and two-sample ChIP-seq experiments.

---

## [629] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am looking for an ontology manipulation tool to handle gene associations. What tool is available?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the `Gene Associations` tool, which is categorized under Ontology Manipulation.

---

## [630] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on transcriptomic RNA-seq data and need a tool to extract an Ig vector from my gene-to-isoform mapping to support isoform-level differential expression analysis. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the tool 'Get Ig vector from gene-isoform mapping for isoform level DE analysis'. It is designed for RNA-seq isoform-level differential expression workflows, such as RSEM quantification pipelines, using a two-column (isoform to gene) mapping.

---

## [631] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a de novo transcriptome assembly project and want to use an assembler pipeline from the SPAdes toolkit. Which tool is designated for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `rnaSPAdes`. It is the specialized pipeline within the SPAdes (St. Petersburg genome assembler) toolkit designed specifically for de novo RNA/transcriptome assembly.

---

## [632] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to perform a fast rigid alignment between microscopy images within the Biomni bioimaging framework. Which function should I call?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Biomni: Quick Rigid Registration' tool, which is designed for quick rigid registration within Biomni integration.

---

## [633] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have metagenomic sequencing data and need a tool to profile the presence, absence, and abundance of microbial pathways in my community. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Barplot (Metagenomics)` / `HUMAnN`. HUMAnN profiles the presence, absence, and abundance of microbial pathways in microbial communities from metagenomic or metatranscriptomic sequencing data.

---

## [634] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to render a guidetree produced by Clustal Omega. What tool is available for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `Clustalo Guidetree`, which renders guidetrees generated in Clustal Omega.

---

## [635] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on copy number variation (CNV) analysis and need to calculate read counts across genomic windows. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Hmmcopy Readcounter' tool, which provides the readCounter utility from HMMcopy to generate read counts in genomic windows for CNV analysis.

---

## [636] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have phylogenetic gene trees and need to calculate gene copy numbers across them. Is there a tool available for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'Gene Copy Number Finder' tool from the Phylogenetics category. It calculates copy numbers for genes directly from GeneTree inputs using the ETE Toolkit.

---

## [637] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I ran IsoEM2 on my two-condition RNA-seq experiment and obtained bootstrap samples. What tool should I run next to detect differentially expressed genes and isoforms?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `IsoDE2`. It is built specifically to take the bootstrap samples generated by IsoEM2 and calculate differential expression for both isoforms and genes between two conditions.

---

## [638] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to perform quality control on my BAM files to detect and estimate inter-sample DNA contamination. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Verifybamid Verifybamid', which is specifically designed for quality assessment to detect and estimate inter-sample DNA contamination from BAM files.

---

## [639] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to trim the 3' ends of my raw sequencing reads whenever uncalled 'N' bases appear or when the GC content gets too high. What tool fits this requirement?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use 'Sequence Content Trimmer'. It trims the 3' ends of reads based on the presence of specified bases, such as when 'N's are encountered or when GC content exceeds a defined threshold.

---

## [640] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing data from comparative microbial community studies. What tool can I use to plot rank abundance against cumulative read abundance?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'ampvis2 rank abundance plot'. It is designed for 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing, metagenomics, and microbial community analysis to generate rank abundance curves (rank abundance vs cumulative read abundance), with optional standard deviation intervals.

---

## [641] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can you suggest a tool listed in the platform under the Systems Biology section?

**ASSISTANT:** PITHYA is available in the platform's Systems Biology category.

---

## [642] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on standardized multi-omics metadata annotation and need a tool to retrieve an ontology directly by its identifier (for example, BioPAX ID 1522). Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Get ontology by id' tool from the Ontology Manipulation suite, which allows you to retrieve standardized biomedical ontologies by identifier.

---

## [643] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a multi-sample FASTA file and a plain text file listing specific sequence IDs (one per line) that I need to pull out for downstream analysis. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `seqtk_subseq`. It takes a newline-separated list of sequence IDs and an input FASTA file, extracting only the matching named sequences.

---

## [644] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can you recommend an R package for visualizing molecular effects in regulatory cascades via interactive browser displays?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Director, an R package specifically designed to streamline the visualization of molecular effects in regulatory cascades in a browser-enabled format.

---

## [645] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a transcriptomics tool to build a transferable gene expression model that can predict outcomes reliably across different expression platforms. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend the tool `TOP`. It is designed for transcriptomics gene expression analysis to construct transferable models across gene expression platforms for prospective experiments, achieving validation accuracy comparable to re-substituted models.

---

## [646] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have subcellular, molecule-resolved spatial transcriptomics data from 10X Xenium and Vizgen MERSCOPE. Which tool or data representation infrastructure should I use to handle these objects?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'MoleculeExperiment'. It provides dedicated infrastructure, data representation, and functions to create and manipulate objects of the MoleculeExperiment class for molecule-based spatial transcriptomics platforms like 10X Xenium, Nanostring CosMx SMI, and Vizgen MERSCOPE.

---

## [647] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a sequence analysis tool that allows gene selection for reference sets using regular expressions and has the Sharp and Li reference set pre-built. What tool fits this description?

**ASSISTANT:** The sequence analysis tool **gwvalue** matches your requirements. It uses regular expressions to include genes in reference sets and provides built-in reference sets from Sharp and Li, Nakamura and Tabata, and Sakai et al.

---

## [648] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have transcriptomics data and want to model network estimates for each individual sample rather than an aggregate network. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'lionessR'. It specifically provides network inference functionality via the `lioness` function in R to infer single-sample networks from gene expression profiles.

---

## [649] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with two-channel microarray data that suffers from noticeable spatial artifacts and intensity biases across the slides. Is there a tool specialized in addressing both of these biases simultaneously?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, `nnNorm` is specifically designed for this purpose. It is a bioinformatics package that detects and corrects for both spatial and intensity biases in two-channel microarray data using a normalization method based on robust neural network fitting.

---

## [650] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on comparative genomics and need an ultra-fast method to previsualize pairwise alignments and synteny across massive genomes, possibly around 20–30 Gb in size. What tool do you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend Chromeister. It is a heuristic tool tailored for the ultra-fast previsualization of pairwise genome comparisons and can scale up to enormous genomes (up to 30 thousand million base pairs), facilitating large-scale alignment visualization and synteny analysis.

---

## [651] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on an archaeal assembly and need a specialized tool to identify genomic islands. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Islandpath, which is designed specifically for genomic island prediction in bacterial and archaeal genomes.

---

## [652] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which workflow in transcriptomics should I use to select optimal reference genes and compute normalized relative quantities for real-time quantitative PCR data?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `normqpcr_normalized_relative_quantities` workflow. It is specifically designed within transcriptomics for the selection of optimal reference genes and the normalisation of real-time quantitative PCR data.

---

## [653] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a 16S rRNA amplicon microbial community profiling study and need a tool from the Mothur environment to check for chimeric sequences. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Chimera.check, which is part of the Mothur suite tailored for 16S rRNA amplicon and metagenomic microbial community profiling.

---

## [654] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Is there a tool named Consensus available under Variant Analysis?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, `Consensus` is available as a tool under the Variant Analysis module.

---

## [655] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Is there a tool available in the statistical domain that can run an automated SaQC quality control pipeline via a configuration file?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use the 'SaQC executor' tool. It executes the SaQC quality control pipeline on your uploaded data according to the parameters specified in your configuration file.

---

## [656] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have mapped read data in BAM format and need to sort the alignments. Which tool can I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'Sort (Assembly)' tool, which executes the samtools sort command to sort BAM datasets into coordinate or read name order.

---

## [657] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an aligned BAM file from a metagenomics sequencing run in Galaxy and I simply need to get a count of how many reads mapped to each reference contig or sequence for quality control. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Count mapped reads` (count_mapped_reads), a Galaxy utility developed by BASE2BIO. It takes a BAM input file and reports the exact number of reads mapped to each target sequence.

---

## [658] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to perform doublet scoring on my single-cell transcriptomics dataset. Which tool is designed for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'scds'. It is a dedicated module for doublet scoring in single-cell transcriptomics workflows.

---

## [659] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have FASTA genome assemblies of Haemophilus parasuis and need to determine their serotypes. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Hpsuissero, which is specifically designed for serotype prediction of Haemophilus parasuis assemblies from FASTA files.

---

## [660] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have transcriptomic data with target genes and need to link my table to known reference genes that interact with each gene of study. Which tool can perform this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use 'Join to Reference Gene Interactions'. It joins your data to a list of reference interacting genes based on a common key column matching your gene of study.

---

## [661] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have identified a query set of candidate genes from a variant analysis study and want to evaluate whether specific Gene Ontology terms are over- or under-represented compared to our background gene set. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Rank Terms' tool. It is designed for variant analysis and comparative genomics/transcriptomics to evaluate the over- or under-representation of Gene Ontology terms in a query set relative to a larger background dataset using a specified statistical test.

---

## [662] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have differential expression output from DESeq2 and edgeR and want to create interactive htmlwidgets plots viewable in a browser. Which workflow should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the Glimma workflow. It is designed for transcriptomics to produce interactive visualizations and htmlwidgets versions of popular RNA-seq analysis plots using output from limma, edgeR, or DESeq2.

---

## [663] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on systems biology and metabolic network reconstruction. Which tool can export my network into graph formats like GML or JsonGraph?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `Sbml2Graph`. It is designed for systems biology, metabolomics, and metabolic network analysis, and can export models into GML, JsonGraph, or tabulated edge and node lists.

---

## [664] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have raw mass spectrometry files in netCDF format from a metabolomics experiment and need to convert them to mzML for downstream processing. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `bioconductor-mzR convert`. It is specifically designed to convert mass spectrometry data files between supported formats (mzML, mzXML, netCDF) utilizing the Bioconductor `mzR` package.

---

## [665] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have nucleosome positioning data from MNase-seq and ChIP-seq sequencing experiments and want to derive an external potential energy function for sequence-specific nucleosome formation. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Derive Percus energy landscape' (categorized under Convert Formats). It is tailored for genomics and epigenomics studies using high-throughput sequencing data from ChIP-seq or MNase-seq to generate an external potential energy function.

---

## [666] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have bulk transcriptomic expression profiles from complex tissue mixtures and want to perform fully unsupervised deconvolution without relying on prior reference expression profiles. Which package should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `debCAM` R package. It provides functions to conduct fully unsupervised deconvolution of complex tissue mixture expression profiles using Convex Analysis of Mixtures (CAM).

---

## [667] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with cross-sectional tumor samples and want to reconstruct tumor progression models using Suppes-Bayes Causal Networks. What R package is designed for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the TRONCO (TRanslational ONCOlogy) R package. It provides algorithms to infer progression models using Suppes-Bayes Causal Networks from both ensembles of tumors (cross-sectional samples) and individual patient profiles (such as multi-region or single-cell data).

---

## [668] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a computational chemistry tool that can screen chemical structures in a file based on molecular weight, logP, and SMARTS patterns. What would you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend MolFilter. It is specifically designed to filter chemical compounds from a molecule input file using properties such as molecular weight, logP, SMARTS expressions, or similarity to query molecules.

---

## [669] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have sequencing reads in FASTQ format, but my downstream program only accepts FASTA sequences. Is there a tool to perform this conversion?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use the 'FASTQ to FASTA' tool. It converts FASTQ sequencing reads directly to FASTA sequences by removing the quality score information.

---

## [670] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have microbial community sequencing data and need to profile the presence, absence, and abundance of metabolic pathways. What tool should I look into?

**ASSISTANT:** You should look into HUMAnN (Regroup). It is designed to efficiently and accurately profile the presence/absence and abundance of microbial pathways within a community from metagenomic or metatranscriptomic sequencing data.

---

## [671] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am configuring reference datasets in Galaxy for genome and transcriptome assembly evaluation. Which tool under Data Managers allows me to manage BUSCO datasets and options?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the **Busco options** Data Manager tool to download, configure, and manage reference lineages and datasets for BUSCO in Galaxy.

---

## [672] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to perform taxonomic classification and species identification directly from microbial sequencing reads against a k-mer database generated with KMA. Which tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Kmerfinder'. It identifies the best species match for reads from FASTQ or FASTA inputs using a KMA-produced k-mer database and outputs taxonomic details.

---

## [673] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have discovered a single novel non-coding RNA sequence and need to automatically build an RNA family model. Which tool is tailored for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use RNAlien. It is specifically designed for the automatic construction of RNA family models starting from a single input sequence, suitable for ncRNA discovery and structural RNA analysis.

---

## [674] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool can I use to perform single-cell spatial analyses on imaging-based spatial proteomics data using Scimap?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Spatial Analysis Tools (Imaging), which performs various single-cell spatial analyses using Scimap across spatial transcriptomics, imaging-based spatial proteomics, and single-cell spatial analysis designs.

---

## [675] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have sequence analysis variant records in VCF format and need a standard tool to filter them. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'Filter vcf' tool, which is designed for sequence analysis workflows involving variant call format (VCF) datasets.

---

## [676] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to generate a custom organism transcriptome database in SQLite format for trips-viz visualization studies. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'create annotation in sqlite for trips-viz', which builds SQLite annotation databases from tab-separated GFF3 files for transcriptome annotation and visualization studies.

---

## [677] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a large FASTA dataset containing DNA marker sequences for metagenomics and need to sample a smaller fraction of the plain or aligned sequences. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `qiime2 rescript subsample-fasta`. It is designed to subsample a set of sequences (plain or aligned DNA) down to a specified fraction of the original dataset.

---

## [678] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with whole genome paired-end sequencing data and need a tool to discover novel transposable element insertions. What do you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend TEfinder. It is specifically designed for whole genome sequencing and population genomics studies using paired-end data to detect novel transposable element insertion events by analyzing discordant read pairs.

---

## [679] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We have an incomplete CRISPR perturbation profile along with log-odds differential gene expression data. We need to infer unobserved perturbations and reconstruct the network. Which tool should we use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **nempi**. It is designed for CRISPR, ATAC-seq, and classification tasks, taking an incomplete perturbation profile and differential gene expression log-odds to infer unobserved perturbations and augment observed ones via iterative network inference.

---

## [680] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have conducted a 16S rRNA marker gene survey and want to predict functional gene abundances normalized by sample totals. Which tool is designed for this?

**ASSISTANT:** The 'Predict Metagenome' tool is designed for this task. It applies to marker gene surveys (16S rRNA) and functional metagenomics, predicting functional profiles and normalizing each sample's total abundance to 1.

---

## [681] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I plan to map reads for circRNA, fusions, and splice junctions using segemehl align. What indexing step do I need beforehand?

**ASSISTANT:** You should run `Segemehl Index` to generate the reference genome index required prior to performing alignment with `segemehl align`.

---

## [682] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with genomic intervals and need a tool from Genomic Interval Operations to assess conservation scores across intervals. What tool is available?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'conservation' tool, which is designed within the Genomic Interval Operations category to analyze conservation across specified genomic intervals.

---

## [683] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We just sequenced several Escherichia coli isolates from a clinical outbreak and need to determine their serotype, including whether they are Verotoxigenic E. coli (VTEC). What tool should we use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'E coli Serotyper' tool, developed in alignment with the European Union Reference Laboratory (EU-RL) for Escherichia coli, including Verotoxigenic E. coli (VTEC).

---

## [684] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with transcriptomic and CRISPR dependency profiles and want to predict whether a cancer therapeutic directly targets a wild-type or mutated oncoprotein versus secondary targets. Which tool is tailored for this?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `DeepTarget`. It specializes in deep characterization of cancer drugs by integrating large-scale genetic (CRISPR) and pharmacological screens from the Broad Institute's Cancer Dependency Map to distinguish primary versus secondary targets.

---

## [685] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can you recommend an R package for converting positional annotations of single nucleotide variations (SNVs) between coding and genomic reference sequences?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend VarCon, an R package designed for functional genomics and alternative splicing analyses. It converts positional information from SNV annotations (referring to either coding sequence or reference genomic sequence) and retrieves the genomic reference sequence around the variant position.

---

## [686] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are analyzing sequencing coverage in a genomics study to identify mosaic copy number variation and need to estimate what percentage of cells carry the aneuploidy. What tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `MADSEQ`. It provides hierarchical Bayesian models specifically designed to detect mosaic aneuploidy, infer the specific type of aneuploidy, and quantify the fraction of aneuploid cells in the sample.

---

## [687] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have gene p-values resulting from an expression analysis and want to perform pathway analysis. Which tool can use these p-values directly?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'MLP' tool. It is designed for transcriptomics pathway analysis based directly on p-values associated with genes from gene expression analysis.

---

## [688] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can I use the 'Search in textfiles' tool to perform differential gene expression or variant calling?

**ASSISTANT:** No. 'Search in textfiles' is a general text processing utility that runs grep, and it is not designed for omics-specific analyses like differential expression or variant calling.

---

## [689] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have differential gene expression results from an experiment and want to compare them against known cellular perturbation profiles from the Connectivity Map. What tool fits this task?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use cTRAP. It compares differential gene expression results with known cellular perturbations (such as gene knock-down, overexpression, or small molecules) derived from the Connectivity Map to help infer potential causal mechanisms.

---

## [690] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have mapped Hi-C read alignments in BAM format and need to convert them into a genome contact map. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Pretextmap, which is specifically designed to convert SAM, BAM, CRAM, or pairs files into genome contact maps.

---

## [691] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have long-read sequencing data in FASTQ format and need to subsample it to generate read subsets for multiple assemblies. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Trycycler Subsample. It is designed to subsample a long-read sequencing FASTQ file specifically for generating multiple assemblies.

---

## [692] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a data source tool that allows me to query proteins via regex matching on disease annotations, organism names, and subcellular location annotations. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using the 'Uniprot Sparql' tool. It supports regular expression searches across protein annotations including disease annotation, organism name, and location annotation.

---

## [693] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working in an R environment and want to apply totalVI workflows for CITE-seq analysis while integrating with scvi-tools. What package is suitable?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `scviR` package. It defines R interfaces to `scvi-tools` and includes dedicated vignettes walking through the totalVI workflow for CITE-seq data analysis.

---

## [694] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are setting up a pathogen surveillance and bacterial strain differentiation pipeline and need a data manager to fetch DNAprints files for reference genomes and populate the vsnp_dnaprints table. Which tool handles this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the **vSNP DNAprints data manager**. It fetches the vSNP DNAprints file associated with each supported genome reference to populate the `vsnp_dnaprints` data table, leveraging reference entries from the `all_fasta` table for bacterial genomics and pathogen surveillance.

---

## [695] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have two protein structures and want to see where their conformations diverge. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `compare_protein_structures`. It is designed to compare two protein structures to identify structural differences and conformational changes.

---

## [696] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can you recommend an OpenMS TOPP tool for tandem mass spectrometry (MS/MS) peptide identification workflows?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `MascotAdapterOnline`. It is an OpenMS TOPP tool designed for proteomics applications, specifically tandem mass spectrometry (MS/MS) peptide identification workflows and data-dependent acquisition (DDA) proteomics experiments.

---

## [697] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with paired-end genomic sequencing data from ctDNA and need to identify gene fusions like EML4-ALK. What tool fits this use case?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `DNAfusion`. It is designed for GeneFusionDetection in genomics and sequencing to identify gene fusions (such as EML4-ALK) from paired-end sequencing data, particularly using position-deduplicated BAM files generated with AVENIO Oncology Analysis Software.

---

## [698] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Is there a Galaxy wrapper available that leverages Heng Li's seqtk toolkit for sequence analysis?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use `seqtk_seq`, which is a Galaxy tool relying on the seqtk toolkit developed by Heng Li at the Broad Institute.

---

## [699] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool can I use to integrate ChIP-seq peak data with a list of canonical gene annotations to find the closest peaks to each gene?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the tool "Analyse canonical genes against 'peak' data" (Sequence Analysis). It performs integrated analysis of peak sets against canonical genes for a specific organism and genome build to identify the nearest peaks to each canonical gene and vice versa.

---

## [700] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need an R/Bioconductor tool to test for Gene Ontology enrichment in my microarray study while addressing the hierarchical correlation structure of the GO graph. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `topGO`. It provides specialized algorithms and test statistics designed to test GO terms while accounting for GO graph topology and reducing redundancy caused by dependencies between parent and child terms.

---

## [701] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with whole exome sequencing alignment files in SAM format and need to adjust or recalculate the mapping quality scores after re-evaluating alignment confidence. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Update MAPQ score (SAM)' tool, which is specifically designed for genomics data like whole exome sequencing (WES) where mapping quality adjustments or remapping evaluations are needed.

---

## [702] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are processing amplicon sequencing data and need to soft-clip or hard-clip read alignments that overlap primer regions specified in a BED file. Is there a Samtools utility for this?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use Samtools Ampliconclip. It clips read alignments where they match regions defined in a BED file.

---

## [703] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on transcriptomics data from a factorial two-colour microarray experiment. Is there a dedicated R/software package for designing the experiment and running statistical differential expression analysis?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use `daMA`. It is specifically designed for the efficient design of factorial two-colour microarray experiments and provides functions for the statistical analysis of factorial microarray data (described in Bretz et al., 2003).

---

## [704] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have predicted a set of RNA-RNA interaction sites computationally and want to filter out false positives. Which tool is suitable?
