**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Evaluation of RRIs using CheRRI'. It evaluates whether interaction sites are likely to occur in nature and filters out false-positive RNA-RNA interactions generated experimentally or computationally.

---

## [705] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am looking for a bioinformatics statistical method that estimates correlation with less bias than standard sample correlation in the presence of measurement error.

**ASSISTANT:** You should consider `MeasurementError.cor`. It implements a two-stage measurement error model for correlation estimation that achieves smaller bias than standard sample correlation.

---

## [706] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which metagenomics tool can quickly calculate pairwise distances between query sequences and references for microbial strain differentiation?

**ASSISTANT:** Use 'mash dist'. It estimates the distance of query sequences against references using fasta/fastq or sketch (.msh) files, making it suitable for taxonomic profiling and strain identification/differentiation.

---

## [707] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I just completed a de novo RNA-seq assembly using Trinity. What tool can I run to assess the assembly quality and completeness statistics?

**ASSISTANT:** You should run `Trinity Stats`. It is designed for transcriptomics workflows focusing on de novo transcriptome assembly quality assessment, RNA-seq assembly evaluation, and transcriptome completeness validation.

---

## [708] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool in metabolomics to inspect missingness (counts and proportions) and deciles across distinct sample groups defined in metadata. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend the 'Intensity Check' tool. It is specifically designed to calculate the number and proportion of missing values, mean, standard deviation, deciles, and mean fold changes, either globally or stratified by sample groups provided in your sample metadata file.

---

## [709] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on marine in-situ data profiles from multiple platforms and need to aggregate and harmonize them with standardized vocabulary. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the **QCV harmonizer** tool (`qcv-ingester`) in the Ecology domain. It aggregates and harmonizes marine in-situ data (specifically designed for UseCase 2.1-BCG - Fair-Ease), converting individual or aggregated data profile files into a concatenated single file with harmonized vocabulary.

---

## [710] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to prepare a GTF annotation file so that I can convert genomic BAM alignments to transcriptomic coordinates using Mudskipper. Which tool should I use to build this index?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Mudskipper Index. It builds and stores the GTF index required to facilitate the conversion of genomic BAM/SAM files to transcriptomic BAM/SAM files.

---

## [711] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a list of UniProt accession numbers and need to query SRMAtlas for unique proteotypic peptides to set up a Selected Reaction Monitoring (SRM) method. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Get unique peptide SRM-MRM method' tool, which takes a list of UniProt accession numbers to retrieve unique proteotypic peptides and related information from SRMAtlas for SRM method building.

---

## [712] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have variant data from a whole exome sequencing study and need to annotate it with KEGG, Reactome, and GO gene sets. Which tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'SnpSift GeneSets', which annotates variants using MSigDB gene sets derived from Gene Ontology (GO), KEGG, and Reactome.

---

## [713] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I currently have my paired-end sequencing datasets organized inside a paired collection, but a downstream step requires them as two separate list collections. Which tool can do this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Unzip Collection' tool from Collection Operations, which splits a paired collection into separate lists.

---

## [714] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a ChIP-seq data analysis project. Is FSeq an available tool for this data type?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, FSeq is designated for ChIP-seq data analysis.

---

## [715] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I want to start analyzing my OTU table and sample metadata in ampvis2. Which tool should I use first?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'ampvis2 load'. It reads the OTU or ASV table and metadata and outputs an RDS data set, which is required before any other ampvis2 tools can be used.

---

## [716] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool to prepare and index a reference genome for a cell-free DNA (cfDNA) variant calling pipeline. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `GenerateIndex`. It is designed for genomics and cell-free DNA (cfDNA) analysis specifically as a preprocessing step for reference genome indexing in variant calling workflows.

---

## [717] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to perform quality control on my multi-sample RNA-seq experiment to verify if read coverage across transcripts is uniform or if there is severe 5' or 3' degradation bias. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `Rseqc (Sequence Analysis)` module for gene body read coverage. It scales all transcripts to 100 nt, computes read depth at each relative nucleotide position, and outputs a gene body coverage plot to assess uniformity and detect 5' or 3' coverage bias.

---

## [718] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on transcriptomics data and need a workflow that integrates Bioconductor packages like epitxdb, genomicranges, and ggplot2 to manage RNA modification information. Which workflow fits this requirement?

**ASSISTANT:** The EpiTxDb workflow is designed for this purpose. It operates within the transcriptomics domain and utilizes 'bioconductor/epitxdb', 'bioconductor/genomicranges', and 'bioconductor/ggplot2' to store and manage epitranscriptomic information.

---

## [719] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with 16S amplicon data and want to collapse/group the samples in my feature table based on sample metadata. What tool can I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `qiime2 feature-table group`. It is designed to group samples or features in a feature table using metadata to define the mapping of IDs to specific groups.

---

## [720] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool to automate sequence comparison against prototype strains and assign genotypes using pairwise distance thresholds. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using the R package rhinotypeR. It is designed to automate the comparison of sequence data against prototype strains and assign genotypes using predefined pairwise distance thresholds.

---

## [721] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with paired-end sequencing data and want to analyze a specific transposable element to find internal SNVs, deletions, and flanking sequences. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** TETyper is designed specifically for this purpose. It types a target transposable element (TE) using paired-end sequencing data by detecting single nucleotide variants (SNVs) and deletions within the TE, as well as identifying flanking sequences around it.

---

## [722] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am setting up antiSMASH inside a local conda/docker environment to screen bacterial genomes for biosynthetic gene clusters (BGCs) like NRPS. What tool module should I run to obtain the required reference databases?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Antismash Antismashlitedownloaddatabases`. This module downloads the necessary antiSMASH reference databases required for running genome-wide secondary metabolite identification in conda, Docker, or Singularity environments.

---

## [723] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have multiple BAM files from different sequencing lanes that need to be merged into a single BAM without losing read groups. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Merge BAM Files'. It utilizes the Picard merge command to combine multiple BAM files into a single BAM file while preserving BAM metadata such as read groups.

---

## [724] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on functional genomics data and need an R package capable of inferring functional gene networks where edges represent posterior beliefs of gene association types and nodes capture perturbation effects. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the PANR package. It provides S4 classes and methods specifically designed for inferring functional gene networks where edges encode posterior beliefs of gene association types and nodes encode perturbation effects.

---

## [725] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool for exploratory analysis, cell type identification, and dimensional reduction on my scRNA-seq dataset. What do you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** Seurat UMAP is well-suited for this task. It supports single-cell RNA sequencing workflows focusing on quality control, exploratory analysis, and resolving sources of heterogeneity such as cell types.

---

## [726] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a genomics preprocessing tool to obtain optimized combinations of DNA barcodes for multiplex sequencing that adhere to Illumina chemistry constraints. What do you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend 'DNABarcodeCompatibility'. It is designed to obtain optimized combinations of DNA barcodes for multiplex sequencing, pooling them according to Illumina chemistry constraints.

---

## [727] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I want to generate stacked barplots and Voronoi plots from single-cell spatial transcriptomics data using Scimap. Which tool provides this?

**ASSISTANT:** Use the 'Spatial plotting functions' tool. It utilizes Scimap to create Voronoi plots and stacked barplots from single-cell spatial data.

---

## [728] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with imaging mass spectrometry data from METASPACE and want to export it into AnnData for downstream single-cell/spatial analysis. Is there a dedicated tool for this?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use the 'Metaspace Converter' tool. It is designed to export METASPACE metabolomics and imaging mass spectrometry datasets into AnnData and SpatialData objects.

---

## [729] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a genomics workflow to evaluate my genome assemblies along with their GFF/GTF annotations using BUSCO and generate summary plots. What workflow is suitable?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Fasta Gxf Busco Plot' workflow. It runs BUSCO on genome assemblies and their annotations (in GFF/GFF3/GTF format) and creates summary plots using the BUSCO/generate_plot.py script.

---

## [730] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool that can perform logratio-based correlation analysis and calculate proportionality coefficients. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend "Propr Propr", which is designed to perform logratio-based correlation analysis to obtain proportionality and basis shrinkage partial correlation coefficients.

---

## [731] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to filter out samples from my microbiome feature table based on sample frequency and metadata. What QIIME 2 tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `qiime2 feature-table filter-samples`, which filters samples from a feature table based on frequency and/or metadata.

---

## [732] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an existing set of DNA barcodes and want to evaluate their minimum, maximum, and average pairwise distances. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the DNABarcodes package. In addition to creating barcodes, it specifically provides functionality to analyze existing barcodes with respect to their minimal, maximal, and average distances.

---

## [733] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are conducting an untargeted metabolomics study and need a tool to fetch and update metadata for entries in our mass spectrometry spectra dataset from online services. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend MSMetaEnhancer. It is designed for metabolomics and mass spectrometry, fetching and updating metadata entries in spectra datasets through a series of automated online conversions.

---

## [734] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are analyzing cancer single-cell RNA-seq datasets and need a method based on Bayesian non-negative matrix factorization for cell clustering and marker gene selection. What tool fits this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `ccfindR`. It is tailored for cancer single-cell transcriptomics clustering analyses and feature gene selection using Bayesian and maximum likelihood non-negative matrix factorization (NMF) algorithms.

---

## [735] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need an interactive interface to scan transcripts for miRNA target sites, visualize KdModels, and browse predicted repression data. What workflow do you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `scanMiRApp` workflow. It provides a Shiny interface to the scanMiR package, allowing users to scan transcripts and custom sequences for miRNA binding sites, inspect KdModels, and browse predicted repression values.

---

## [736] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to search a nucleic acid sequence database for homologous non-coding RNAs based on a consensus RNA secondary structure profile. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `cmsearch` from the INFERNAL software package. It is designed to take consensus RNA secondary structure profiles and search nucleic acid sequence databases to identify homologous RNAs or construct structure-based multiple sequence alignments.

---

## [737] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have metagenomic and metatranscriptomic sequencing datasets and need to profile the presence and abundance of microbial pathways. What tool should I look into?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use HUMAnN (available under Rename in Metagenomics). It is designed to efficiently and accurately profile the presence/absence and abundance of microbial pathways within a community.

---

## [738] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need an R package within the alabaster ecosystem that can save raw and indexed genomic file formats like BAM and VCF along with associated metadata. What package should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `alabaster.files`. It is designed specifically to save and load common bioinformatics file formats (such as BAM, VCF, BED, bigWig, bigBed, FASTA, and FASTQ) inside the alabaster framework, maintaining linkage to their corresponding indices and preserving file-level metadata.

---

## [739] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on contamination screening in a metagenomic dataset and need to exclude contaminant sequences identified via BLAST against a database. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `exclude_seqs_by_blast`, which is designed for contamination screening and sequence filtering in metagenomics and metabarcoding datasets by running BLAST against contaminant databases.

---

## [740] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have metagenomics data formatted as a BIOM table and a sample mapping file, and I need a script to aggregate or collapse samples together. What tool does this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use 'Collapse samples', which runs the script `collapse_samples.py` to collapse samples in both a BIOM table and its associated mapping file.

---

## [741] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to add provenance metadata notes directly into the header section of my aligned BAM file. Which tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `AddCommentsToBam`. It adds one or more comment lines (@CO) directly to the header of a specified BAM dataset.

---

## [742] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We conducted metabolic RNA labeling experiments using 4-thiouridine (4sU) to measure intracellular RNA dynamics and transcription rates. What Next-Generation sequencing tool is designed to map and identify these incorporation events?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Slamdunk`. It is a mapper specifically tailored for SLAM-seq data to uncover 4-thiouridine incorporation events from high-throughput sequencing and analyze intracellular RNA dynamics and stability.

---

## [743] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I conducted an isothermal titration calorimetry experiment and need a biochemistry tool to extract binding affinity and thermodynamic parameters. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the **Biomni: Analyze Itc Binding Thermodynamics** tool. It is tailored for biochemistry workflows to evaluate isothermal titration calorimetry (ITC) data and calculate binding affinity along with associated thermodynamic parameters.

---

## [744] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have two paired-end FASTQ files from an RNA-seq experiment, but after trimming, some forward and reverse reads lost their mates. Which tool can I use to re-synchronize them?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'De-interleave reads' tool (specifically `sync_reads` from b2b-utils). It takes a pair of forward and reverse FASTQ files and synchronizes them by outputting only the read pairs that appear in both files.

---

## [745] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We generated structural variant calls from long-read sequencing and need to benchmark them against a gold-standard baseline VCF to calculate recall and precision. What tool is designed for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Truvari Bench. It is specifically designed for structural variants (SVs) to compare a comparison set against a baseline set and calculate recall, precision, and f-measure.

---

## [746] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have metabolomics NMR spectra that need peak alignment using the Cluster-based Peak Alignment (CluPA) algorithm. Which tool can I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'NMR spectra alignment' tool in Metabolomics, which implements the Cluster-based Peak Alignment (CluPA) algorithm from the R 'speaq' package.

---

## [747] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a bioinformatics tool for multiple comparisons to estimate false discovery rates and q-values from p-values without making assumptions about the alternative distribution. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend the tool **LBE** (MultipleComparison). It provides an efficient procedure for estimating the proportion of true null hypotheses, the false discovery rate, and q-values based on the marginal distribution of p-values without assumptions for the alternative distribution.

---

## [748] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am planning an RNA-seq study and want to evaluate statistical power and determine the appropriate sample size for differential expression analysis. Which tool can help with this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'PROPER'. It provides simulation-based methods specifically designed for evaluating statistical power and sample size in RNA-seq differential expression experiments.

---

## [749] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to perform allelic expression analysis using Salmon and Swish (SEESAW design) on RNA-seq data. Which tool or package provides these methods?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use fishpond, which provides methods for differential transcript and gene expression analysis, including the SEESAW design (allelic expression analysis with Salmon and Swish).

---

## [750] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on cancer sequencing data and need a computationally efficient method to infer the clonal population structure of tumors. What tool do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend pyclonevi. It is a computationally efficient Bayesian statistical method specifically designed for inferring the clonal population structure of cancers.

---

## [751] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to model a protein-DNA and a protein-ligand complex without running local software. What web-based tool do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend the `AlphaFold 3 Server` (accessible at alphafoldserver.com). It is specifically well-suited for predicting structures of protein-ligand, protein-DNA, and protein-RNA complexes.

---

## [752] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which imaging tool can I use to apply mathematical expressions (such as addition or subtraction) across all pixels in my fluorescence microscopy images?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'Operate on pixels' tool. It applies mathematical expressions (add, subtract, multiply, etc.) to each pixel and handles overflow and underflow by clamping values to the legal minimum or maximum of the image data type.

---

## [753] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting an ecological biodiversity assessment and need to create a taxonomic coverage template by choosing taxonomic authorities to search taxa names against. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Make taxonomic coverage template`, an ecology tool that lets you select taxonomic authorities to search taxa names and generate the coverage template.

---

## [754] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have sequenced reads from CUT&RUN / CUT&Tag experiments in bedgraph format. Which peak-calling tool can process this directly?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **Seacr Callpeak**. It is specifically designed to call peaks using SEACR on sequenced reads formatted as bedgraph files, commonly used for CUT&Tag and CUT&RUN data.

---

## [755] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have ChIP-seq data across several biological experiments and need an epigenomics tool to extract common consensus genomic regions. What do you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend consensusSeekeR. It is designed for genomics and epigenomics studies (such as ChIP-seq) to compare genomic positions and ranges across multiple experiments and identify consensus regions.

---

## [756] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a large collection of FASTA genome assemblies with redundant strains. I need to identify highly similar genomes and retain only the best representative of each. What tool can do this?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using Drep Dereplicate (`drep`). It is built specifically to compare genome sets, detect highly similar genomes, and choose the optimal representative genome.

---

## [757] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Is there a tool that allows me to launch an interactive Shiny application to inspect copy number profiles, including probe-, gene-, and segment-level log2 ratios?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use CNViz. It takes probe, gene, and segment-level log2 copy number ratios and launches a Shiny app to visualize your sample's copy number profile.

---

## [758] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have run several structural variant callers in a genomics study and need to standardize their VCF outputs for comparison. What nf-core tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `svync`, an nf-core tool specifically designed to standardize VCF files produced by structural variant callers.

---

## [759] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with metabolomics mass spectrometry data that exhibits systematic mass drift post-acquisition. Is there a tool to perform mass calibration?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use ExternalCalibration. It supports both metabolomics and proteomics mass spectrometry experiments that require post-acquisition mass calibration to correct for systematic mass shifts.

---

## [760] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a reference genome FASTA file and a BED file containing coordinates of genomic regions. What tool can I use to pull out the nucleotide sequences for these intervals?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Bedtools Getfasta (`bedtools getfasta`). It is specifically designed to extract sequences from a FASTA file based on the intervals defined in a feature file (such as a BED file).

---

## [761] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have NOMe-seq GCH methylation footprinting data and want to test for differential footprints between disease and control conditions around known TF motifs. Which tool is specifically designed for this?

**ASSISTANT:** dinoR is designed for this exact use case. It evaluates significant differences in NOMe-seq footprints between two conditions across genomic regions of interest centered on landmarks such as transcription factor motifs.

---

## [762] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing mass spectrometry proteomics data and need a tool to calculate and visualize the limit of blank (LOB) and limit of detection (LOD). What do you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend MSstatsLOBD. It is specifically designed to calculate and visualize both the Limit of Blank (LOB) and Limit of Detection (LOD), particularly for proteomics, metabolomics, and transcriptomics assays.

---

## [763] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have protein and DNA/RNA sequences that I want to align against a single reference profile HMM. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'hmmalign'. It is designed for sequence analysis to align sequences individually against a single reference profile HMM and output the alignment in Stockholm format.

---

## [764] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a Data Managers tool to automatically retrieve the latest GTF annotation from Ensembl for my species and generate reference indexes specifically for ALFA analyses. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'ALFA indexes' data manager tool. It accepts a species name, downloads the latest GTF file from Ensembl, and executes `ALFA.py` to produce and store the required ALFA index files.

---

## [765] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have completed a proteomics enrichment analysis and want to summarize the redundant Gene Ontology terms using REVIGO. Is there a tool to format my data accordingly?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you should use the tool 'Prepare Data for REVIGO' in the Proteomics domain. It formats and pre-processes your proteomics functional enrichment output to be directly compatible with REVIGO semantic similarity analysis.

---

## [766] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am looking for a proteomics and synthetic biology tool suited for quality assessment of synthetic libraries generated via phage display directed evolution. What tool fits this study design?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `syndiva`. It is designed for proteomics and synthetic biology applications, specifically suitable for directed evolution studies using phage display and synthetic library quality assessment.

---

## [767] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with whole genome sequencing (WGS) data and need a fast and accurate small variant caller to identify germline variants and output VCF files. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `Strelka Germline` (Strelka2). It is a fast and accurate small variant caller optimized specifically for the analysis of germline variation from WGS data, generating standard VCF outputs.

---

## [768] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool in the transcriptomics domain to group expression profiles using density-based spatial clustering (DBSCAN) or agglomerative hierarchical approaches. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Cluster Analysis' tool (Transcriptomics). It provides density-based clustering via DBSCAN, agglomerative clustering, as well as K-means for cluster analysis of datasets.

---

## [769] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a pharmacogenetics project and need to compute summary statistics for our control gene using alignments in BAM format. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Pypgx Computecontrolstatistics`. It is designed specifically within pharmacogenetics workflows to compute summary statistics for a control gene directly from BAM files.

---

## [770] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am running a population genomics variant analysis and need windowed genomic summaries of nucleotide diversity. What tool would you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `GEMINI windower`, which is designed for population genomics and variant analysis studies requiring windowed genomic calculations.

---

## [771] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an uncharacterized protein sequence in FASTA format and need to search for homologous proteins across a reference database. Which tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Blast Blastp. It takes an amino acid query sequence and searches it against a protein database to find matching protein alignments.

---

## [772] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I ran GATK4 Mutect2 across multiple genomic intervals in parallel. Now I have several mutect stats files for each region that I need to combine into one. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Gatk4 Mergemutectstats'. It is specifically designed to merge Mutect2 stats generated across different intervals or regions into a single file.

---

## [773] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have spatial transcriptomics data that includes tissue coordinates and I want to generate a 2D spatial scatterplot. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Create spatial scatterplot' tool, which is designed for imaging and spatial transcriptomics experiments with tissue coordinates.

---

## [774] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I ran multiBamSummary across my BAM files and now have a compressed summary matrix. Which tool can I use to perform and visualize principal component analysis?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use deepTools plotPCA, which is designed to generate a principal component analysis (PCA) plot directly from the compressed matrix output of multiBamSummary or multiBigwigSummary.

---

## [775] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a community ecology dataset with presence-absence data and want to visualize correlation between species absences. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Statistics on presence-absence' tool. It is suited for community ecology and presence-absence data, allowing you to visualize correlation between species absences and assess numeric variable dispersion.

---

## [776] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a massive whole-genome VCF file and want to split it by specific genomic regions or chunks into multiple smaller VCF files to parallelize downstream variant processing. What tool fits this purpose?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Bcftools Pluginscatter`. It is designed specifically to split a VCF or BCF file by chunks or predefined genomic regions, generating multiple smaller VCF outputs for parallel processing.

---

## [777] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We need an accelerated tool compatible with STAR to align our sequencing reads against a reference genome FASTA. What tool fits this requirement?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Sentieon Staralign. It is designed to align reads to a reference genome using the Sentieon-optimized implementation of STAR.

---

## [778] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** My genome annotation GFF3 file contains mixed non-coding annotations and is missing explicit intron entries. I need a tool to standardize this file strictly for canonical protein-coding genes. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `CanonGFF3` from the AEGeAn toolkit. It specializes in cleaning up GFF3 files for canonical protein-coding genes, stripping away non-protein-coding features and automatically inferring implicit features like introns.

---

## [779] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a large multi-sample FASTA file of assembled transcripts and need to collapse redundant sequences into representative non-redundant transcripts. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use CD-HIT-EST. It is designed for sequence database redundancy reduction and clustering of nucleotide sequences such as transcripts and ESTs from FASTA files.

---

## [780] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have several sequencing archives in tar.gz and fastq.bz2 format. Is there a tool that can unpack these files and combine them together?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'Decompress an archive' tool. It decompresses archives in formats such as zip, gz, tar.gz, fastq.gz, fastq.bz2, or tar.bz2, and provides an option to merge all decompressed files into a single output file.

---

## [781] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a metagenomics tool to filter out rare or absent OTUs across my sample cohort based on minimum count thresholds. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `filter_by_metadata`. It is tailored for metagenomics workflows to set minimum counts across samples to keep OTUs, retaining by default only OTUs that are present in the samples.

---

## [782] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have run segmentation analysis on microarray copy number variation data using DNAcopy, but the results are segmented outputs for individual samples. I need to convert this into a single matrix structure where overlapping segments are rows and samples are columns so I can perform downstream downstream statistical analyses. Which tool handles this conversion?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **CNTools**. It is specifically designed to convert the output of segmentation analysis from DNAcopy into a unified matrix structure where overlapping segments are represented as rows and samples as columns.

---

## [783] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool for metagenomics and comparative genomics that allows taxonomic filtering of reference sequences for specific organism groups by checking curated accession number prefixes. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the tool `Filter RefSeq by taxonomy` (domain: Genome annotation). It is designed for taxonomic filtering of reference sequences in genomics, metagenomics, and comparative genomics projects.

---

## [784] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can you recommend a genomics tool specifically built to construct guidetrees for multiple sequence alignment (MSA) using a graph clustering approach?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `Magus Guidetree`, which performs guidetree generation for multiple sequence alignment using graph clustering.

---

## [785] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have SARS-CoV-2 genome assemblies and need to determine their outbreak lineages. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **Pangolin Run** (Phylogenetic Assignment of Named Global Outbreak LINeages), which is dedicated to assigning SARS-CoV-2 genome sequences to specific lineages.

---

## [786] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can you recommend a metagenomics tool tailored for distilling metabolic annotations from MAGs and VirSorter-identified viral contigs?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'DRAM distill'. DRAM (Distilled and Refined Annotation of Metabolism) is specifically built for annotating metagenome-assembled genomes (MAGs) and VirSorter-identified viral contigs across databases like UniRef90, PFAM, dbCAN, RefSeq viral, VOGDB, and MEROPS.

---

## [787] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can you recommend a bioinformatics tool that uses factor graph hierarchical ensembles for Gene Ontology annotation of protein coding genes?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `fgga`. It is specifically designed for the consistent cross-ontology annotation of protein coding genes using a hierarchical ensemble method based on factor graphs.

---

## [788] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with metatranscriptomic NGS reads and need a tool specifically designed to filter out rRNA reads using an approximate seed algorithm. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'sortmerna'. Its core algorithm uses approximate seeds for sensitive NGS read analysis, and its primary application is filtering rRNA from metatranscriptomic and metagenomic data.

---

## [789] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am performing ontology manipulation and need to retrieve the top-most root terms of my ontology hierarchy. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Get the root terms' tool, which falls under the Ontology Manipulation category.

---

## [790] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** In our post-mapping variant annotation workflow for whole exome sequencing, we need to sieve through predicted LoF variants to retain only those meeting strict transcript-level criteria. What tool fits this task?

**ASSISTANT:** GEMINI lof_sieve is recommended. It is built for genomics and variant annotation workflows specifically addressing loss-of-function variant prioritization and rare variant association studies.

---

## [791] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing project for microbial community analysis and need to cluster fragment sequences. Which tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** The `Cluster.fragments` tool under Metagenomics is suited for this task. It is designed for sequence clustering workflows in microbial ecology, metagenomics, and 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing, allowing clustering based on difference thresholds such as the `percent` and `diffs` parameters.

---

## [792] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a merged OpenMS proteomics identification file (idXML) and I need to split it back into the individual originating identification files. Which tool can perform this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `Openms Idripper`, which is specifically designed to split a merged identification file (idXML) into its originating identification files.

---

## [793] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have completed RNA-seq differential expression analysis and gene set enrichment, and I need a Bioconductor tool to generate interactive HTML reports summarizing the results for web viewing. What would you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend ReportingTools. It is designed to display reports of analysis results from sources such as microarray and RNA-seq sequencing data, including gene set enrichment and GO analyses, generating HTML pages viewable in web browsers like Safari.

---

## [794] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a reference FASTA file and need to build a sequence dictionary using GATK4. Which specific tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should run the Gatk4 Createsequencedictionary tool, which creates a sequence dictionary for a reference sequence.

---

## [795] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am looking for a SAM-based genomics tool for structural variant detection to validate candidate insertions in a case-control study. What tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the SAM tool 'Confirm or discard insertions', which is designed for structural variant detection in genomics, specifically for case-control studies with treatment and control samples.

---

## [796] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to inspect a CRAM file to see the breakdown of individual data series and content ID storage consumption. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Samtools Cramsize`. It specifically lists CRAM Content-ID and Data-Series sizes for a given CRAM alignment file.

---

## [797] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have aligned NGS reads from whole genome sequencing that need to be sorted by reference genome coordinates before downstream analysis. What tool is suited for this format conversion?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'MiModD Sort'. It is a format conversion tool designed for NGS mapping workflows (such as whole genome sequencing, exome sequencing, and targeted sequencing) to sort aligned read datasets by reference genome coordinates.

---

## [798] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on de novo eukaryotic genome annotation across multiple FASTA contigs and need a gene prediction tool that incorporates splice site modeling. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **Multi Fasta GlimmerHMM**. It is built for de novo gene annotation in eukaryotic genomic sequences supplied in multi-FASTA format and integrates GeneSplicer-adapted splice site models within a Generalized Hidden Markov Model (GHMM).

---

## [799] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need an R package/infrastructure backend to fetch and represent mass spectrometry data directly from the MetaboLights repository. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `MsBackendMetaboLights`. It provides dedicated infrastructure functionality to retrieve and represent mass spectrometry (MS) data directly from the MetaboLights repository.

---

## [800] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a sequence analysis tool that utilizes a greedy star-clustering algorithm similar to DNAclust or usearch. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use VSearch clustering. It is a sequence analysis tool that implements a single-pass, greedy star-clustering algorithm comparable to DNAclust, usearch, and sumaclust.

---

## [801] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We just ran a targeted exome sequencing project using hybrid-selection capture. Which tool can evaluate the coverage and performance metrics of this dataset?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use CollectHsMetrics. It computes metrics specifically designed to evaluate the coverage and performance of datasets generated through hybrid-selection (HS) capture.

---

## [802] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on bacterial genome annotation in Python and need a fast, reliable tool to predict protein-coding genes. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `Pyrodigal`. It is a Python module providing bindings to Prodigal, offering fast and reliable protein-coding gene prediction for prokaryotic genomes.

---

## [803] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are conducting a citizen science biodiversity survey where acoustic bat observations were recorded by volunteers and subsequently verified by expert validators. Which tool can help us combine both sets of records?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Tadarida identifications integration'. This tool is specifically designed for citizen science biodiversity surveys and participatory species identification validation workflows, allowing you to integrate identifications made by observers and validators to provide an input for synthesis of participations.

---

## [804] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool should I use to index my reference sequence in FASTA format?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Samtools faidx`, which runs the `samtools faidx` command specifically to index reference sequences in FASTA format.

---

## [805] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are conducting a bottom-up proteomics experiment with MS/MS data and need to run a database search to identify peptides and obtain raw search scores. Which tool fits this requirement?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use MSGF+ MSMS Search. It runs an MS/MS database search using the MSGFPlus engine and is specifically designed for bottom-up proteomics and peptide identification from tandem MS data.

---

## [806] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I performed ancient metagenomic taxonomic screening using MALT and need a tool to evaluate the alignment results for true positives. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Maltextract', a tool designed specifically for the evaluation of MALT results for true positives in ancient metagenomic taxonomic screening.

---

## [807] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with Galaxy Apollo Tools and need a web service utility to retrieve all available organisms. Which tool fits this task?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'List Organisms' tool from the Web Services category, which is designed as a utility tool for querying organisms within Apollo.

---

## [808] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool in systems biology to parse an SBML file and extract reactant lists for a specific set of reactions.

**ASSISTANT:** You should use GetReactantsFromReactions. It extracts reactant lists from an SBML model file given a list of reactions for metabolic network reconstruction and pathway analysis.

---

## [809] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an SDF file of small molecules named Trypsin_actives.sdf that I need to divide into 3 separate parts for parallel virtual screening. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `LigandFileSplitter`. When provided with `Trypsin_actives.sdf` and set to split into three parts, it will generate three output files named `Trypsin_actives_0.sdf`, `Trypsin_actives_1.sdf`, and `Trypsin_actives_2.sdf`.

---

## [810] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have generated a VCF file from an SNP discovery study and need to compute statistics on it. What tool is recommended?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use VCFtools Stats, which is designed for variant analysis to compute statistics directly on VCF files.

---

## [811] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am setting up a metagenomic contig and genome bin taxonomic annotation pipeline using the Contig Annotation Tool (CAT). Which component prepares the necessary reference databases?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `CAT prepare`. It is the dedicated tool within the CAT and BAT framework to set up and format the reference databases and taxonomy files required for contig and bin annotation.

---

## [812] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have genomic signal tracks in plain text ASCII WIG format, but I need them in binary BigWig format for high-performance visualization. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should run 'Ucsc Wigtobigwig', which is specifically designed to convert ASCII WIG files into binary BigWig format.

---

## [813] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on metabolite profile classification and protein expression classification, and I need a supervised classification tool that is fast and easy to interpret. What would you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend the 'Discriminant Analysis' machine learning tool. It supports linear and quadratic discriminant analysis (LDA/QDA), both of which are computationally fast, interpretable, and applicable to metabolomics profile classification, proteomics expression data, and genomics variant classification.

---

## [814] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on microbial community profiling using metagenomic sequencing and need a tool within the HUMAnN pipeline ecosystem to re-calculate and normalize pathway abundances. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the Renormalize tool. In the HUMAnN pipeline for metagenomic and metatranscriptomic profiling, Renormalize is designed to recalculate microbial pathway and community abundance metrics.

---

## [815] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am setting up a phylogenetic and comparative genomics workflow with Oxford Nanopore reads and a FASTA reference. I need a tool that maps the reads and preserves the sample identifier in the BAM output for PACU. What tool should I choose?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'PACU - mapping' from the Phylogenetics section. It handles ONT (or Illumina) reads, maps them to your FASTA reference, and attaches a custom tag derived from the FASTQ filename to the output BAM file to propagate the sample name to PACU.

---

## [816] source=multitask_sft_big

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, DockPoseClustering is designed specifically for this purpose. It offers clustering algorithms including `NEAREST_NEIGHBOR_CHAIN_WARD`, `CLINK_DEFAYS`, `CLINK_ALTHAUS`, `SLINK_SIBSON`, and `TRIVIAL_COMPLETE_LINKAGE` for clustering docked poses in computational chemistry workflows.

---

## [817] source=multitask_sft_big

## [821] source=multitask_sft_big

## [822] source=multitask_sft_big

## [824] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have multiplexed amplicon sequencing reads with separate barcode data, and I plan to run demultiplexing using `split_libraries_fastq.py`. Which tool can format my sequence and barcode files so they are compatible?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `Format Fastq sequences and barcode data` tool. It is designed to format FASTQ sequences and barcode data specifically so they are compatible with `split_libraries_fastq.py`.

## [825] source=multitask_sft_big

## [826] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I completed cross-validation on an imbalanced binary classification model for sequence motifs and want to plot the Precision-Recall curve and obtain the PR AUC. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Plot Precision-Recall Curve` under Sequence Analysis. It takes cross-validation predictions as input to generate the Precision-Recall (PR) Curve and compute the Area Under the Curve (AUC).

---

## [827] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a metabolic engineering project in synthetic biology and need to delete specific genes in an SBML biological model. What tool can I use?

## [828] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a Bayesian tool to statistically test whether mutational signature exposures (specifically using the Shiraishi model) differ between two treatment cohorts. What do you recommend?

## [830] source=multitask_sft_big

---

## [831] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have single RNA sequences and batch RNA sequence sets and need to predict their minimum free energy secondary structures. Which tool is suitable?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `RNAfold`. It is designed for RNA structural analysis and transcriptomics sequence analysis, supporting both single RNA sequence structure prediction and batch RNA structure prediction.

---

## [833] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an RNA-seq count matrix generated from featureCounts for a multi-group factorial experiment. Which tool can I use to perform differential expression analysis and generate interactive Glimma plots?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use limma. In transcriptomic workflows, limma accepts a count matrix (such as from featureCounts) along with optional gene metadata to run differential expression analyses for factorial and multi-group designs, outputting summary tables, standard plots, and interactive Glimma visualizations.

## [835] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am looking for a phylogenetic tool to detect whether specific lineages in my gene tree have experienced episodic bursts of positive selection at certain sites. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend HyPhy-aBSREL. It is specifically designed to test for episodic positive selection across lineages by inferring whether a proportion of sites along specific branches have undergone positive selection.

---

## [836] source=multitask_sft_big

**ASSISTANT:** No. GDAL Merge is a geospatial raster processing utility intended for image mosaicing and is not designed or applicable for omics or genomics data analysis.

## [837] source=multitask_sft_big

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the **Chip-Cluster** tool. It is specifically designed for multi-factor and comparative ChIP-seq studies to merge peaks from multiple experiments into multi-factor overlapping regions (MTLs), cluster each MTL by peak scores using user-defined K-means clustering, and generate a heatmap.

---

## [838] source=multitask_sft_big

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Multivcfanalyzer`. It is a SNP table generator specifically designed to work with outputs from GATK UnifiedGenotyper, with functionality geared for ancient DNA (aDNA).

**USER:** I am working on a transposon insertion sequencing (TraDIS) dataset to study bacterial gene essentiality and need to process reads into insertion counts. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Bio-TraDis reads to counts' tool. It is specifically designed for TraDIS and transposon insertion sequencing data in gene essentiality studies, automatically configuring read mapping parameters based on initial read length.

## [841] source=multitask_sft_big

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Deeptools Bigwigcompare`. It is designed to compare two bigWig files based on the number of mapped reads.

---

## [842] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I already generated initial cell type annotations for my single-cell RNA-seq dataset, but I want a post hoc classification tool to fine-tune these annotations using semi-supervised learning. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use scReClassify. It is specifically designed as a post hoc cell type classification tool for single-cell transcriptomics that fine-tunes cell type annotations from any prior classification procedure using the semi-supervised AdaSampling technique.

---

## [843] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to perform sequence analysis to identify word composition biases using baseline word frequencies generated by EMBOSS compseq. Which tool takes compseq output as a reference?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `oddcomp`. It takes dataset output produced by 'compseq' to set the minimum frequencies of words for sequence composition analysis.

## [844] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are building a large-scale genomic sequence analysis pipeline that requires indexed file access. Which tool should we use to compress our sorted genomic files for compatibility with indexing utilities like Tabix?

## [845] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing Affymetrix GeneChip transcriptomic microarray datasets and need a differential expression tool specifically tailored for Bayesian integrated analysis. What tool should I use?

## [847] source=multitask_sft_big

## [848] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a quality control and benchmarking software to quantitatively evaluate and visualize different RNA-seq quantification pipelines. What tool fits this task?

## [849] source=multitask_sft_big

## [856] source=multitask_sft_big

## [857] source=multitask_sft_big

## [858] source=multitask_sft_big

## [859] source=multitask_sft_big

## [860] source=multitask_sft_big

## [861] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have complex clinical patient records and want an unsupervised method to identify robust, hidden patient sub-phenotypes while overcoming typical clinical clustering limitations. What tool fits this task?

## [862] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are processing multiplex tissue microarray (TMA) images and need an automated way to detect both complete and incomplete tissue cores. What tool is suited for this?

---

## [863] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a proteomics project where I need to learn the cleavage specificity of a specific protease class from known cleavage sites and then predict cleavage sites in protein sequences. What tool supports this two-step process?

**ASSISTANT:** The **Protease prediction** tool is specifically designed for this. In the first step, it learns the cleavage specificity of a given protease class from known sites; in the second step, it uses that learned specificity to predict cleavage sites within protein sequences.

---

## [864] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with Affymetrix one-channel SNP microarray data and have access to reference samples with known genotype calls. What tool can train on these labels to classify my new samples?

---

## [865] source=multitask_sft_big

---

## [866] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool should I use to calculate alpha diversity, or within-sample diversity, using an OTU table from a microbial community analysis?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Run a core set of QIIME diversity analyses' tool, which is designed for metagenomics, 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing, and microbial community analysis to calculate alpha diversity using an OTU table.

---

## [867] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a pangenome variation graph and need to project or convert it into other graph formats such as GFA. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Odgi View', which is specifically designed to project variation and pangenome graphs into other formats.

---

## [868] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on transcriptomic gene expression data and need an R package that implements the UniBic biclustering algorithm introduced by Zhenjia Wang et al. in 2016. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the R package `runibic`. It implements the UniBic biclustering algorithm introduced by Zhenjia Wang et al. (2016) for analyzing gene expression data.

---

## [869] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** My sequencing facility returned FASTQ files with some corrupted and malformed lines that fail quality control checks. Is there a tool to clean or remove these uncompliant reads?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use **Wipertools Fastqwiper**. It is part of the wipertools suite and is designed specifically to fix or wipe out uncompliant and corrupted reads from FASTQ files.

---

## [870] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to visualize overlapping gene sets across 6 different experimental conditions. Venn diagrams are messy beyond 3 sets. Which package can help?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use ggupset. It generates UpSet plots, which are data visualisations designed for set intersections that extend effectively to far more than 3 sets compared to traditional Venn diagrams.

---

## [871] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have transcriptomic expression data from tumor and normal patient samples and need a GUI-based tool that uses disease ontology enrichment to identify distinguishing gene sets. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend SVMDO. It provides an easy-to-use GUI designed for transcriptomic data that detects tumor/normal sample discriminating gene sets from differentially expressed genes using an iterative filtering algorithm combined with disease ontology enrichment analysis.

---

## [872] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a tabular file containing PubMed IDs across multiple rows and need to automatically fetch and attach the corresponding abstracts. Which tool can do this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'abstracts_by_pmids' tool, which retrieves the abstracts for all PMIDs listed in each row of a table and appends them into additional columns.

---

## [873] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to remove specific sample groups and treatment conditions from my metabolomics study design file. What tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `Modify design file` tool. It is specifically suited for studies requiring the removal of specific sample groups or treatment conditions from a tab-separated design file with a `sampleID` column.

---

## [874] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a massive alignment of several hundred thousand protein sequences and need to build a maximum-likelihood phylogeny quickly. Which tool can scale to this size?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use FASTTREE (Phylogenetics). It is specifically built to infer approximately-maximum-likelihood trees and can handle alignments with up to a million sequences using manageable time and memory.

---

## [875] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool is recommended within Next Gen Mappers for aligning low-divergent Illumina 75bp sequencing reads to the human reference genome?

**ASSISTANT:** 'Map with BWA' is recommended, as its underlying bwa-aln algorithm is designed specifically for low-divergent reads up to 100bp mapping against large reference genomes.

---

## [876] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am setting up an OpenSWATH spectral library-based proteomics workflow and need to compress and cache mzML files for data preprocessing. What tool fits this task?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use OpenSwathMzMLFileCacher. It is designed for proteomics data preprocessing in OpenSWATH and spectral library-based workflows, using numpress compression to minimize mzML file sizes.

---

## [877] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which QIIME 2 tool should I use to calculate an alpha diversity metric across all samples in my metagenomics feature table?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'qiime2 diversity alpha', which computes a user-specified alpha diversity metric for all samples present in a feature table.

---

## [878] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an amino acid FASTA file containing translated protein sequences and need to generate comprehensive functional domain predictions and annotations. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Interproscan. It is designed specifically to produce protein annotations and functional predictions directly from an amino acids FASTA file.

---

## [879] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have bacterial whole-genome sequencing data from suspected E. coli isolates and need to perform serotype classification and strain typing. Which tool is recommended?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use ectyper. It is designed for bacterial genomics, specifically for bacterial strain typing and serotype classification of E. coli isolates.

---

## [880] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a directory of individual microscope image tiles from high-resolution tissue imaging. Which tool can assemble them into an OME-TIFF pyramid?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `raw2ometiff`, a Java application designed to convert a directory of tiles into an OME-TIFF pyramid.

---

## [881] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have raw FASTQ files from a shotgun metagenomics experiment and need to assign taxonomic labels to the reads using a reference database. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Kraken2. It is specifically designed to classify metagenomic sequence data (such as FASTQ files) against a reference database.

---

## [882] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to generate a comprehensive multi-track genomic visualization combining gene models, ideograms, alignment coverage/mismatches, and variants. What workflow fits this purpose?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `ggbio` workflow, which uses `bioconductor/ggbio` to build and navigate multi-track genomic visualizations integrating ideograms, gene models, alignment coverage, mismatches, and variants.

---

## [883] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need an R/Bioconductor infrastructure package to organize, store, and manage all components of a mass spectrometry proteomics experiment. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** MsExperiment is recommended. It provides lightweight and flexible containers designed specifically to store and manage all aspects related to complete mass spectrometry experiments.

---

## [884] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I only have target gene expression data from microarrays and want to infer target gene regulation by miRNAs without miRNA sequencing data. What tool can do this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use MiRaGE. It is designed for transcriptomics/genomics gene expression data and specifically contains functions for inferring target gene regulation by miRNA based solely on target gene expression profiles.

---

## [885] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have generated GC bias metrics for my ChIP-seq experiment using computeGCBias and need to remove GC-biased reads before calling peaks. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `correctGCBias`. It uses the output from `computeGCBias` to correct the BAM file before peak calling and downstream analysis.

---

## [886] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Is there a utility in the Tool Dependency Packages category specifically designated for processing SMILES to SMILES conversions?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use the 'SMILES to SMILES' tool, which is cataloged under Tool Dependency Packages for handling SMILES data.

---

## [887] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool from the MACS2 suite that can deduplicate my sequencing reads and output them as a BED file. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use MACS2 filterdup. It specifically removes duplicate reads and outputs the filtered alignments in BED format.

---

## [888] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have ancient DNA sequencing data and want to infer genetic sex by checking gonosomal versus autosomal coverage. What tool is built for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Sexdeterrmine'. It determines genetic sex in ancient DNA studies by calculating relative coverage on gonosomes versus autosomes with error bars.

---

## [889] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a pairwise comparative genomics study to evaluate gene order evolution between two species that share conserved, duplication-free gene content. Which tool can determine the rearrangement scenarios?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use CREx (Phylogenetics). It is specifically designed to compute rearrangement scenarios on pairs of gene orders with equal, duplication-free gene content, making it well-suited for synteny analysis and comparative genomics.

---

## [890] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a 16S rRNA microbial community study and need a Mothur tool for read quality assessment before downstream processing. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using 'Summary.qual' from the Mothur suite, which is specifically suited for quality control of sequencing reads in metagenomics and 16S rRNA gene sequencing studies.

---

## [891] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am performing genomic interval operations and need to convert my BED files into BigWig format for genome browser visualization. Which tool is designated for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'bed_to_bigwig' tool under Genomic Interval Operations to convert your BED interval files into the BigWig format.

---

## [892] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are preparing raw sequencing reads from single-end and paired-end genomic runs before assembly. What tool can handle formatting our FASTQ files?

## [893] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I processed my 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing data using the FROGS pipeline, but my downstream microbial community profiling tool requires a standard, generic BIOM file. What tool should I use?

---

## [894] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to test our CryoEM viewer pipeline and validate resolution assessments using synthetic data. Is there a tool to generate synthetic Cryo-EM MRC files?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use 'Generate Test CryoEM Map'. It generates synthetic test cryo-EM electron density map MRC files with realistic FSC data specifically for CryoEM viewer pipeline testing, visualization quality assessment, and resolution validation.

---

## [895] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which proteomics tool can I use to detect clustering of somatic amino acid mutations while accounting for protein structure?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `iPAC`. It is specifically designed to identify somatic amino acid mutation clustering within proteins while taking protein structure into consideration.

## [896] source=multitask_sft_big

---

## [897] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a list of Human Phenotype Ontology term identifiers (such as HP:0000118) and need a database tool to fetch their corresponding human-readable names. What tool can I use?

## [898] source=multitask_sft_big

---

## [899] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a reference genome in FASTA format and need to generate a GEM index to assess mappability. Which tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use Gem2 Gemindexer. It is built to create a GEM index directly from an input FASTA reference file.

## [900] source=multitask_sft_big

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Metaphlan Mergemetaphlantables`, which is specifically designed to merge output abundance tables from MetaPhlAn4.

---

## [901] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on proteomics data and need a tool that can take an idXML file or a text file of sequence-to-retention-time pairs for retention time modeling. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use RTModel. It operates in the proteomics domain and accepts idXML or .txt files containing peptide sequences with their corresponding retention times per line.

---

## [902] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have ecological survey data collected using road-based transect sampling (the Routier protocol). Which analysis tool is appropriate?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Advanced restitution: Routier or Pedestre protocols', which is explicitly designed to analyze observations from the Routier and Pedestre survey protocols.

---

## [903] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** In my 3D genomics pipeline, I need to account for proximity ligation effects by multiplying each entry of the expected matrix by a scaling factor. Which tool handles this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'hicTransform' from Sequence Analysis, which multiplies a scaling factor to each entry of the expected matrix to take care of proximity ligation.

---

## [904] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on comparative genomics and need to automatically identify genomic regions exhibiting statistically significant conserved gene content and syntenic order.

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using 'i-adhore', a wrapper for the i-ADHoRe software. It automatically detects genomic regions with statistically significant conserved gene content and order, making it suitable for comparative genomics and synteny analysis.

---

## [905] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an R function in my bioinformatics pipeline that is running very slowly. Which tool can compile my user-defined R function into OpenCL to run on a GPU or CPU?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `gpuMagic`. It is an infrastructure package that compiles user-defined R functions to run on OpenCL-compatible devices such as a CPU or GPU with little to no extra effort.

---

## [906] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with alignment chains before building nets and want to discard chains that won't make it into the final nets. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use chainPreNet. It specifically identifies and removes chains that have no chance of being netted, cleaning up the dataset prior to the netting step.

---

## [907] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I ran Bismark Methylation Caller on whole genome bisulfite sequencing (WGBS) data and need to convert the output into WIGGLE format. What tool should I use?

---

## [908] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have SAM and BAM alignment files from a hybrid capture experiment and need to generate hybrid-selection metrics. What tool should I use?

---

## [909] source=multitask_sft_big

---

## [910] source=multitask_sft_big

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Refine shape' tool from the Constructive Solid Geometry category. It refines a shape by limiting all edges to a specified length, generating a more detailed shape.

---

## [911] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are processing large-scale Illumina paired-end sequencing data and need a QC preprocessing tool that filters out read pairs lacking a minimum threshold of known bases. Which tool fits this requirement?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Prq', which is designed for Illumina paired-end sequencing QC and supports filtering pairs via 'seal.prq.min-bases-per-read'.

## [912] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have 16S amplicon sequencing reads and need to perform de novo clustering into operational taxonomic units (OTUs). Which sequence analysis tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `uclust`. It is specifically designed for de novo sequence clustering in amplicon sequencing, 16S rRNA gene analysis, and metagenomics workflows (e.g., de novo OTU clustering).

## [913] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have genomic region data in BED format and signal tracks in WIG format. I want to generate PNG visualizations. What tool can produce these images?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use 'chromograph' (nf-core), a python package designed to create PNG images from genetics data such as BED and WIG files.

---

## [914] source=multitask_sft_big

---

## [915] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are analyzing meiotic prophase immunofluorescence datasets and need an automated Bioconductor tool to identify cells marked by a synaptonemal complex protein. What package would you recommend?

It is specifically designed for automated, reproducible, and unbiased analysis of meiotic immunofluorescence datasets, with core functionality tailored to identify cells in meiotic prophase labelled by a synaptonemal complex protein.

## [916] source=multitask_sft_big

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'Fasta interactive input' tool (from Fasta Manipulation). It is specifically designed for manual sequence entry for small-scale analyses and quick validation of individual sequences across genomics, proteomics, and transcriptomics.

---

## [917] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have generated a structural multiple sequence alignment using Foldmason and need to render an LDDT-based visualization report. Which tool should I run?

## [919] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have aligned MNase-seq and ATAC-seq data and need to perform nucleosome mapping and identify nucleosome-depleted regions. What tool is suited for this?

---

## [920] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with single-cell RNA-seq data from the kallisto | bustools pipeline and need an R tool to convert the BUS output into a sparse gene count matrix. What do you recommend?

---

## [921] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have structural variant calls in a VCF and need to normalize the SVs into disjoint genomic intervals. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should run Truvari Segment, which specifically provides normalization of structural variants into disjointed genomic regions.

## [922] source=multitask_sft_big

## [923] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a binary multi-omics dataset and need to identify candidate genetic or epigenetic drivers that together show significant enrichment or skewness relative to an outcome vector. What tool can I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use CaDrA. It is designed to perform both stepwise and backward heuristic search for candidate (epi)genetic drivers based on binary multi-omics datasets to find features that are collectively skewed or enriched with respect to a target vector.

## [924] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have sequence files in GenBank and EMBL format that I need to filter and reformat into FASTA. Which EMBOSS tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Emboss Seqret. It is designed to read, convert, filter, and transform sequence files across formats like FASTA, GenBank, EMBL, and GFF.

---

## [925] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a tabulated variant marker table from a biparental cross and need to extract markers specific to parent refA and parent refH, optionally reformatting them into a 3-letter genotype encoding. Which tool does this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `VCFCarto`. It is tailored for variant analysis in biparental crosses, filtering tabulated marker sets down to the two parent lines (`refA` and `refH`) and allowing you to either retain the existing format or switch to a 3-letter format.

---

## [926] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have clustered my RNAz prediction hits and need a tool to annotate them using a BED file. What should I use?

---

## [927] source=multitask_sft_big

---

## [928] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have paired-end whole genome sequencing and metagenomics reads with insert sizes that permit overlap between mate pairs. Which tool can merge these read pairs into single reads?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use BBTools: BBMerge. It is designed for genomics, metagenomics, and whole genome sequencing to merge overlapping paired-end reads into single, longer reads.

## [929] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am building a BLAST-based sequence annotation workflow for metagenomic assemblies and viral contigs. Which virAnnot tool handles converting blast annotations and contig sequences into tabular output?

## [930] source=multitask_sft_big

## [931] source=multitask_sft_big

## [932] source=multitask_sft_big

## [934] source=multitask_sft_big

## [935] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a genomics and transcriptomics study and need a tool to create an archive for interactive visualization of multiple genomic tracks, potentially including protein features. What tool would you recommend?

## [936] source=multitask_sft_big

## [937] source=multitask_sft_big

## [938] source=multitask_sft_big

## [939] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have several BedGraph coverage files from ChIP-seq and ATAC-seq samples. What tool can I use to merge them for multi-sample comparative coverage analysis?

## [940] source=multitask_sft_big

## [941] source=multitask_sft_big

---

## [942] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on microbial community analysis using Mothur and need a tool to filter specific sequences or entries out of my distance matrix. What tool should I use?

---

## [943] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on paired-end genomic sequencing data for a tumor-normal comparative design. Which tool can I use to compare anomalous links between two mate-pair or paired-end datasets to find shared and sample-specific structural variants?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the tool **Compare (Genomic Interval Operations)**. It is specifically designed to compare filtered links between two anomalously mapped mate-pair/paired-end datasets (such as a sample versus reference design) and identify common and sample-specific structural variants based on overlaps between cluster coordinates and SV types.

---

## [944] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We need to score gene signatures in large single-cell transcriptomics datasets using the Mann-Whitney U statistic with low memory and runtime overhead. What tool would you recommend?

---

## [945] source=multitask_sft_big

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'gbaseinformationcontent' tool, which is cataloged under the Sequence Analysis category.

## [946] source=multitask_sft_big
