**ASSISTANT:** I recommend CGHcall. It is designed specifically for array CGH preprocessing and aberration calling using a six-state mixture model incorporating biological concepts. In addition to calling aberrations, it provides visualization capabilities for the generated profiles.

---

## [947] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing single-cell transcriptomics data and want to cluster T-cell and B-cell receptor repertoires by CDR sequence similarity. What tool can I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `ClustIRR`. It is specifically designed for single-cell transcriptomics data to analyze B- and T-cell receptor repertoires by grouping receptors based on CDR sequence similarity.

---

## [948] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a microbial genomics project and need a QIIME 2 RESCRIPt command to retrieve bacterial and viral genome metadata from the BV-BRC database. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `qiime2 rescript get-bv-brc-metadata`. This tool is designed to fetch metadata for specific data types from the Bacterial and Viral Bioinformatics Resource Center (BV-BRC).

---

## [949] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have multiple VCF files containing structural variant calls that I need to combine into a single dataset. What tool module does this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'Svdb Merge' module. It is specifically designed to merge structural variants across one or more VCF files.

---

## [950] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on cancer genomics data from matched tumor-normal whole exome sequencing. We suspect normal cell infiltration in our tumor biopsies. What tool can quantify this contamination?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using **tinc** (nf-core). It is specifically designed for cancer studies with matched tumor/normal bulk sequencing data (WGS/WES) and implements algorithms to estimate sample contamination, such as normal cells present within the tumor biopsy.

---

## [951] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have used several antimicrobial peptide prediction tools across my genomic data and want to aggregate their findings into a unified report with functional classifications. What tool does this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Ampcombi`. It parses and summarizes results from various antimicrobial peptide prediction tools and presents functional classifications.

---

## [952] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on sequencing data from a transposon mutagenesis study and need to trim off the transposon tags. Which sequence analysis tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'remove tradis tags' tool, which is designed for sequence analysis in genomics and transposon mutagenesis studies, specifically TraDIS experiments.

---

## [953] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working in proteomics and need to import and export MS/MS spectra from Mascot Generic Format (mgf) files using the Spectra Bioconductor framework. What workflow or backend provides this functionality?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the MsBackendMgf workflow. It provides a mass spectrometry data backend that adds Mascot Generic Format (mgf) import and export support to the Spectra Bioconductor package.

---

## [954] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool for pairwise sequence comparison and assembly validation in a comparative genomics study to visualize synteny and breakpoints against a reference genome.

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `Mummerplot`. It is tailored for comparative genomics studies, pairwise sequence comparisons, genome assembly validation, and synteny analysis, allowing you to highlight breakpoints relative to sequence ends.

---

## [955] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I used a sample regressor to predict numeric phenotypes from microbiome composition data in QIIME 2. Which tool should I use to plot predicted versus true values?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `qiime2 sample-classifier scatterplot`, which creates a 2D scatterplot with a linear regression line comparing predicted and true values for sample regressor outputs.

---

## [956] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a transcriptomics project and need a tool to compare lists of genes by analyzing and contrasting their functional profiles rather than just finding overlaps. What package would you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend the `goProfiles` package. It is designed for transcriptomics applications and implements statistical methods specifically for comparing gene lists based on their functional profiles using Gene Ontology annotations.

---

## [957] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are conducting SLAM-seq metabolic labeling experiments to study RNA stability and transcription rates. What Next Gen Mappers tool supports these experimental designs?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Alleyoop`. It is cataloged under Next Gen Mappers and supports transcriptomics and RNA dynamics, with suitability for SLAM-seq metabolic labeling experiments, RNA stability studies, and transcription rate measurements.

---

## [958] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on RNA-seq splice junction analysis and want to run the entire Portcullis pipeline without executing each stage manually. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Portcullis Full', which runs all Portcullis RNA-seq genome splice junction filtering and analysis steps in one go.

## [959] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on metabolic network annotation and curation in systems biology and need to format reactions and metabolites with R_ and M_ prefixes for an SBML model. What tool does this?

## [960] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with multiplexed tissue imaging data and need an automated method to segment both cell nuclei and whole cell boundaries. What tool do you recommend?

## [961] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with QIIME 2 metagenomics metadata and want to create a line plot to visualize two numeric metadata measures with optional grouping. What tool should I use?

## [969] source=multitask_sft_big

## [970] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a list of gene symbols from my screen and need to evaluate their druggability and see if any are classified as dark targets or well-studied clinical targets. Which BioMate tool can retrieve this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the Pharos / TCRD GraphQL API (wired via `ai_assistant.py _query_pharos`). It queries TCRD (Illuminating the Druggable Genome) to retrieve target development levels (Tclin, Tchem, Tbio, Tdark), protein families, novelty scores, and drug/ligand counts for gene symbols.

## [971] source=multitask_sft_big

---

## [972] source=multitask_sft_big

**ASSISTANT:** HUMID is designed for this task. It quickly and easily removes duplicate reads from FastQ files using clustering and Hamming distances, supporting data with or without UMIs.

---

## [973] source=multitask_sft_big

## [974] source=multitask_sft_big

---

## [975] source=multitask_sft_big

## [976] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing high-dimensional metabolomics and proteomics data where the number of variables exceeds sample size. Which tool provides PCA and PLS modeling for classification and feature selection?

## [977] source=multitask_sft_big

## [978] source=multitask_sft_big

## [979] source=multitask_sft_big

## [980] source=multitask_sft_big

---

## [981] source=multitask_sft_big

## [982] source=multitask_sft_big

## [983] source=multitask_sft_big

## [984] source=multitask_sft_big

## [985] source=multitask_sft_big

## [986] source=multitask_sft_big

## [994] source=multitask_sft_big

## [995] source=multitask_sft_big

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend Longstitch. It is specifically designed for genome assembly correction and scaffolding using long reads, leveraging Tigmint for misassembly cutting, ntLink for initial scaffolding, and ARKS for subsequent scaffolding.

## [996] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need an easy-to-use tool with a GUI wizard to preprocess dual color microarray data. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `genArise`. It is a software tool specifically designed for dual color microarray data that provides a Tk-based GUI wizard, allowing non-experienced users to perform basic preprocessing and analysis.

---

## [997] source=multitask_sft_big

## [998] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have several metagenome-assembled genomes (MAGs) and isolate genomes from a recent sequencing run. Which tool can I use to evaluate their genome quality?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `iASeq`. It fits a correlation motif model to multiple RNA-seq or ChIP-seq studies to boost the detection of allele-specific events and describe correlation patterns across studies.

## [1010] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1024] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool wraps the gprofiler2 R package API to handle cross-database identifier conversions for transcripts and genes?

**ASSISTANT:** The tool you need is **gProfiler Convert**. It provides an interface to the g:Profiler g:Convert service using the `gprofiler2` R package, leveraging Ensembl data to translate diverse gene, transcript, and protein identifiers.

---

## [1025] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with transcriptomics microarray data and want to infer cell type-specific expression without physically isolating the cell type first. Is there a tool for this?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use CellMapper. It infers cell type-specific expression using co-expression similarity with known marker genes from publicly available transcriptomic or microarray data, even when the cell type has not been physically isolated.

---

## [1026] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a de novo genomics project and need a tool to determine ploidy level and assess heterozygosity structure from k-mer counts. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend 'Smudgeplot' from the Assembly category. It is specifically designed for genomics workflows to assess genome assembly quality, evaluate ploidy levels, analyze heterozygosity structure, and investigate paralogs and duplications.

---

## [1027] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool in the Convert Formats section that can compute raw read counts, FPM, and FPKM for genes from an alignment and reference BED file, including support for strand-specific RNA-seq. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'FPKM Count' from the Convert Formats category. It takes a BAM file and reference gene model BED file to quantify raw read counts, FPM, and FPKM for each gene, with support for strand-specific RNA-seq data.

---

## [1028] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a regulatory element association analysis in epigenomics and need to locate features located near specific genomic intervals. Which tool fits this requirement?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `bedtools WindowBed` (Genomic Interval Operations). It is suited for proximity-based feature detection studies and regulatory element association analyses across genomics, epigenomics, and regulatory genomics.

---

## [1029] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have short sequence reads from ATAC-seq and ChIP-seq experiments and need to calculate and plot genomic signal profiles. What tool can produce summarized curves or heatmaps for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the recoup tool. It calculates and plots genomic signal profiles from Next Generation Sequencing short reads (such as ATACSeq and ChIPSeq), offering both summarized curve profiles and heatmap profiles.

---

## [1030] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have single-cell Hi-C matrices stored in a .scool file and need to normalize coverage across cells. HiCExplorer's hicNormalize is too slow for my cell count. What can I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `scHicNormalize`. It is a highly parallelized version of HiCExplorer's `hicNormalize` designed specifically for single-cell Hi-C `.scool` files, scaling the coverage of all matrices to the one with the lowest read count.

---

## [1031] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have chromatin accessibility sequencing data and need a dedicated quality control tool in the genomics and epigenomics domain. What tool fits this design?

**ASSISTANT:** ATACseqQC is recommended. It specializes in quality control for ATAC-seq data within genomics and epigenomics, providing guidelines and functions documented in the ATACseqQC Vignette.

---

## [1032] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to fetch a reference RAVmodel and register it into the Galaxy data table 'genomic_super_signature_ravmodels'. Which data manager tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Load GenomicSuperSignature RAVmodels' data manager tool. It fetches reference RAVmodels and automatically populates the 'genomic_super_signature_ravmodels' data table.

---

## [1033] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with RNA-Seq data and need an R package specifically built to identify differentially expressed genes. What tool would you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend DEGseq, an R package designed to identify differentially expressed genes from RNA-Seq data.

---

## [1034] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We want to infer sample-specific pathway deregulation scores from cancer transcriptomic expression profiles. What tool would you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** Pathifier is recommended for this task. It is specifically designed to quantify pathway deregulation at the individual sample level for cancer expression datasets.

---

## [1035] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need an R package in mathematical biology and comparative genomics to compute automorphisms on pairwise aligned DNA sequences across a population. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `GenomAutomorphism`. It is designed for comparative and functional genomics to compute automorphisms between pairwise aligned DNA sequences from genomic regions up to whole genomes in a given population.

---

## [1036] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with cytometry data and want an interactive Shiny app that wraps ggplot so I can set up plots with dropdowns and checkboxes while running built-in statistics. What tool fits this?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `plotGrouper`. It is a Shiny app-based GUI wrapper for ggplot tailored for cytometry data that includes built-in statistical analysis and allows you to configure plotting variables, graph types, and visual styles via dropdown menus and checkboxes.

---

## [1037] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have just completed an assembled genome and need to screen it for adaptor contamination using an NCBI utility. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Fcs Fcsadaptor`. It is designed to run NCBI's FCS adaptor on assembled genomes for quality control and contamination screening.

---

## [1038] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool to perform haplotype simulations of rare variant genetic data that faithfully emulate real genomic architecture. What tool fits this task?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use RAREsim. It is specifically developed to perform haplotype simulations of rare variant genetic data that emulate real genomic data.

---

## [1039] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have isotope-labeled mass spectrometry data from a quantitative proteomics experiment (SILAC/TMT). Which OpenMS tool should I use to link features across labeled channels?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `FeatureLinkerLabeled`. It is an OpenMS TOPP tool specifically tailored for isotope-labeled quantitative proteomics experiments (such as SILAC, TMT, and iTRAQ) and stable isotope labeling mass spectrometry studies.

---

## [1040] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have patient genomics and transcriptomics profiles along with clinical survival outcomes, and I want to discover distinct molecular patient subtypes. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `survtype`. It is specifically designed to analyze genomic and transcriptomic data associated with clinical survival information to identify patient subtypes that exhibit distinct molecular and clinical features.

---

## [1041] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have fluorescence microscopy images and need to quantify mitochondrial morphology and membrane potential. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `analyze_mitochondrial_morphology_and_potential`, a cell biology tool designed specifically to quantify metrics of mitochondrial morphology and membrane potential from fluorescence microscopy images.

---

## [1042] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have two gene expression profiles ranked by differential expression significance from different microarray platforms, and I want to assess whether they exhibit statistically significant concordance across thresholds. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use RRHO (Rank-Rank Hypergeometric Overlap). It is specifically designed to perform inference on the amount of agreement between two sorted lists using the Rank-Rank Hypergeometric Overlap test.

---

## [1043] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can you recommend a fast and user-friendly tool to predict antimicrobial peptides (AMPs) across a large set of protein sequences?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend ampir. It is a fast, user-friendly tool designed for predicting antimicrobial peptides (AMPs) from any size protein dataset using supervised statistical machine learning.

---

## [1044] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on ancient DNA sequencing data and need a utility that computes the percentage of endogenous DNA directly from mapping summary statistics. What tool can do this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use **Endorspy** (`endorS.py`). It is specifically designed to calculate endogenous DNA percentages from `samtools flagstat` output files and display the resulting statistics on screen.

---

## [1045] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have Illumina short-read RNA-Seq data and need an assembler tailored for de novo transcriptome assembly. What tool from the SPAdes suite should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `rnaspades`. It is part of the SPAdes (St. Petersburg genome assembler) suite and is specifically intended for transcriptome assembly from Illumina RNA-Seq short reads.

---

## [1046] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have fragmented short reads and an incomplete prokaryotic genome assembly. Which Sequence Analysis tool can predict genes in both?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use FragGeneScan from the Sequence Analysis category. It predicts fragmented genes in short reads and can also find prokaryotic genes in incomplete assemblies or complete genomes.

---

## [1047] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have contigs produced by fermi2 from high-coverage Illumina whole-genome resequencing that have been aligned to a reference genome with BWA. What tool can I use to call variants directly from these assembled contigs?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `fermikit-variants`. It is a de novo assembly-based variant calling pipeline tailored for deep Illumina resequencing data that calls variants from fermi2-assembled contigs aligned to a reference genome via BWA.

---

## [1048] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to generate synthetic sequencing reads from a FASTA reference genome to benchmark my pipeline. What tool should I choose?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Wgsim. It is specifically designed for simulating sequence reads from a reference genome provided in FASTA format.

---

## [1049] source=multitask_sft_big

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `MLST List` tool. It lists all scheme names used by MLST and provides an option to display the alleles for all MLST schemes.

---

## [1050] source=multitask_sft_big

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the `Collector’s curve` tool, which is available under the Fasta Manipulation tools.

---

## [1051] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am building a metabolomics spectral library matching pipeline and need a tool to compare and filter mass spectra based on their metadata attributes. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using the `matchms metadata match` Galaxy tool. It is designed for metabolomics and mass spectrometry workflows that require metadata-based spectral filtering, comparison, and spectral library matching.

---

## [1052] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have aligned BAM records and need a tool from the SAM suite that identifies duplicate molecules and flags them in the output. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `MarkDuplicatesWithMateCigar`. It inspects aligned records in SAM or BAM files to locate duplicate molecules and writes out all records with the duplicates appropriately flagged.

---

## [1053] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a general workflow utility step that can compute dynamic values or evaluate inline mathematical and logical expressions between pipeline steps. What tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Expression Tools`. It is categorized under Utilities specifically for evaluating expressions in workflows.

---

## [1054] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can RetroRules be used as part of an omics workflow for microbial community pathway abundance estimation?

**ASSISTANT:** No. RetroRules is designated strictly for computational chemistry and has no primary omics applicability. For microbial community pathway abundance profiling, dedicated omics tools such as HUMAnN should be used instead.

---

## [1055] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to predict the 3D structure of a complex involving a protein, RNA, a small-molecule ligand, and coordinating ions. What model should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use DeepMind's `AlphaFold 3`. It predicts structures of biomolecular complexes containing proteins, DNA, RNA, ligands, and ions, demonstrating a 50%+ improvement over AlphaFold 2.

---

## [1056] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with single-cell RNA sequencing data and need a tool to perform quality control filtering and normalization. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `Seurat`. Developed by the Satija Lab at NYGC, Seurat is designed specifically for single-cell RNA sequencing (scRNA-seq) workflows, including quality control, filtering, and normalization.

---

## [1057] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have ChIP-seq experiments across several biological conditions and need to identify differential binding events while keeping peak locations consistent across conditions. Which tool should I select?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use MultiGPS. It is specifically designed to analyze multi-condition ChIP-seq datasets, characterizing differential binding events while encouraging spatial consistency in binding event locations across conditions.

---

## [1058] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are setting up a genomic epidemiology pipeline for SARS-CoV-2 and need a tool to fetch the latest lineage classification models for pangolin. What data manager should we use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'PANGOlearn data manager'. It fetches updated models directly from the pangoLEARN repository for the pangolin SARS-CoV-2 lineage typing tool and updates the pangolearn data table.

---

## [1059] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a viral metagenomics study and need a tool for host prediction. What tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** PhaBOX cherry is recommended for viral metagenomic and viromic studies requiring host prediction. It works with viral sequences from environmental sampling and accepts either custom predicted protein sequences or runs default prediction via prodigal and diamond blastp.

---

## [1060] source=multitask_sft_big

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `mash paste` tool in the metagenomics toolkit to combine your Mash sketches.

---

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `hicap`, which is designed for in silico typing of the H. influenzae capsule locus.

---

## [1063] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have identified a list of affected KEGG compounds from a metabolomics study and want to identify impacted reactions, enzymes, modules, and pathways using label propagation on a network. What tool fits this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use FELLA. It is tailored for metabolomics data enrichment using KEGG entries and performs label propagation across a knowledge model network to pinpoint affected reactions, enzymes, modules, and pathways.

## [1064] source=multitask_sft_big

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, `Get.group` is available in Mothur for metagenomics and microbial ecology workflows, allowing you to manage and extract group-specific data within the Mothur suite.

---

## [1065] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool in sequence analysis that can map positions to their best hit between query and reference while permitting overlaps. What can I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use Delta-Filter, which maps each position of each query/reference to its best hit in the reference/query and allows reference/query overlaps.

---

## [1066] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have CNV call data and quantitative traits, and I want to run a probe-level or segment-level GWAS to link them. Which workflow should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `cnvranger_cnv_phenotype_gwas` workflow. It is specifically designed to perform probe-level or segment-level genome-wide association studies linking copy number variations to quantitative phenotypes.

---

## [1067] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a comparative analysis method to identify conditional gene essentiality between two conditions using permutation testing on read counts. What tool do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend TRANSIT Resampling. It is designed specifically for comparative analyses to determine conditional essentiality of genes using a permutation test to detect read-counts that differ significantly across conditions.

---

## [1068] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a utility to produce mock Illumina-style paired FASTQ files to test my pipeline structure. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Fq Generate. It generates pairs of FASTQ reads with headers formatted according to Illumina conventions, providing validly structured files with random sequence content.

## [1069] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on proteomics data and need a tool that can detect clustering of somatic mutations across the entire quaternary structure of protein complexes. What do you recommend?

## [1070] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have raw paired-end short DNA sequencing reads in FASTQ format and need an ultrafast de novo method to remove PCR duplicates without mapping first. What tool do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend FastUniq (Fastq Manipulation category). It is an ultrafast de novo tool tailored for removing duplicates from paired short DNA sequence reads in FASTQ format without requiring a reference genome.

## [1071] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to query metadata regarding the technical sequencing procedures applied to biosamples in a Beacon database. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** Use Beacon2 Runs. It is specifically designed to query the runs collection in the Beacon database for metadata regarding sequencing procedures across genomics, WGS, WES, and targeted sequencing studies.

## [1072] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have Oxford Nanopore reads from a microbial sequencing experiment and need an assembly pipeline. Is there a dedicated nf-core pipeline for this?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use dragonflye, which is an nf-core microbial assembly pipeline specifically designed for Nanopore reads.

## [1073] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need an R/Bioconductor package for genomics that provides direct programmatic access to samtools, bcftools, and tabix functionality for reading and manipulating indexed BAM, BCF, and tabix files. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Rsamtools. It provides an R interface to 'samtools', 'bcftools', and 'tabix' for manipulating SAM, FASTA, BCF, and indexed tabix files.

---

## [1074] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am performing targeted sequencing using BED-defined regions and need to call SNPs while accounting for background PCR and sequencing noise. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use DC Genotyper. It is designed for targeted sequencing with enrichment (BED-defined regions) to perform SNP detection by obtaining allele counts using igvtools and modelling background noise.

---

## [1075] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which deepTools utility is designed to calculate score values per genomic region to generate a matrix file for visualization?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Deeptools Computematrix'. It computes scores per genome region to produce the matrix needed by downstream deepTools plotting tools.

---

## [1076] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a bottom-up proteomics study and need to perform de novo peptide sequencing from tandem mass spectrometry data without relying on a prior protein sequence database. What tool is suitable?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `NovorAdapter`. It is designed for proteomics workflows, specifically for de novo peptide sequencing from tandem mass spectrometry data and bottom-up proteomics without a prior protein sequence database.

## [1077] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a tabular file containing numerical values from a microarray experiment, and I want to replace the row identifiers with new labels using an alias mapping file. Which tool does this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'Tabular Label Convert' tool. It takes a tabular format file of numerical values and converts row or column labels using an alias map, handling duplicate alias mappings via summary aggregation (mean, median, min, or max).

---

## [1078] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I ran my alignment through two different versions of a sequence processing tool and want to see the exact record-by-record differences between the two coordinate-sorted BAM files. What tool can print this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `bamUtil diff`. The diff option of bamUtil is designed specifically to print differences between two coordinate-sorted SAM/BAM files, making it ideal for comparing outputs across different tools or tool versions.

---

## [1079] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are generating real-time long reads from bacterial isolates and need to predict sequence types within minutes without waiting for read error correction. What tool can do this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use 'krocus'. It is specifically designed to predict sequence types directly from uncorrected long reads as the sequencing data is being produced, returning results in minutes.

---

**USER:** I want to perform high-dimensional biomarker discovery to find taxa that distinguish my metagenomic sample groups using LEfSe. Which tool formats the input data for this algorithm?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `A) Format Data for LEfSe`, which formats input feature tables specifically for the LDA Effect Size (LEfSe) pipeline.

## [1081] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have mutational data from multi-region whole-exome sequencing of patient tumors. Which tool can help me visualize mutational profiles and measure intratumor heterogeneity?

## [1082] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool under Sequence Analysis can I run to compute multiple physicochemical or sequence-based properties across a set of input protein sequences?

## [1083] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1084] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have cytometry data from cell-based assays and want to visualize cell populations mapped onto a Minimal Spanning Tree. What tool is appropriate?

## [1086] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1087] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1089] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with bio-ontologies and need to query the direct upstream/parent concepts of a specific Gene Ontology term from an OBO file. Which tool should I use?

## [1090] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1091] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1092] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** In my systems biology workflow, I need to generate carbon skeleton networks from SBML models and export them as GML or JsonGraph. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Sbml2CarbonSkeletonNet`. It operates on systems biology networks and can export carbon skeleton graphs into GML, JsonGraph, or tabulated node/edge lists.

## [1093] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am preparing raw DNA sequence reads from 454 pyrosequencing for de novo assembly and need a tool that can perform quality end-trimming and vector sequence removal. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend LUCY trimmer (Less Useful Chunks Yank). It is specifically suited for DNA sequencing assembly pipelines and 454 pyrosequencing workflows to prepare raw sequence fragments by performing quality end-trimming, vector/primer sequence removal, and contamination filtering.

## [1094] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are studying bacterial plasmids and need an in silico tool to predict plasmid mobility (whether it is conjugative, mobilizable, or non-mobilizable) and identify replicon and relaxase types. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend MOB-Typer (Sequence Analysis). It performs in silico plasmid typing, identifying replicon, relaxase, and mate-pair formation protein types, while predicting plasmid mobility (Conjugative, Mobilizable, Non-mobilizable) and potential host range.

---

## [1095] source=multitask_sft_big

**ASSISTANT:** Use `FTP Link for Bioimage Archive`. It retrieves images directly from the BioImage Archive based on an input accession and can optionally output the associated FTP links.

---

## [1096] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have generated a recalibration table for my VCF in GATK4 and need to apply the score cutoff to filter variants. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Gatk4 Applyvqsr`. It performs the second pass of Variant Quality Score Recalibration (VQSR) by applying a score cutoff to filter variants based on the recalibration table.

---

## [1097] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am running a whole genome sequencing variant calling pipeline and need to homogenize indel positions within homopolymer repeats across my aligned reads. What tool do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend **BamLeftAlign** (Sequence Analysis). It performs left realignment on alignments to consistently place indels located in homopolymer and microsatellite repeat regions at the same position, which is ideal for WGS and WES indel calling workflows.

---

## [1098] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with single-cell transcriptomics data and need a package that can detect changes in cell state while robustly quantifying cell type localization even when tissue architecture changes. What tool should I consider?

**ASSISTANT:** You should consider Statial. It is a single-cell software suite specifically designed to identify changes in cell state and provide robust quantification of cell type localisation that is invariant to changes in tissue structure.

---

## [1099] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have precomputed ligand-receptor interactions from my single-cell RNA-seq experiment and want an interactive Shiny interface to filter and analyze cell-cell communication. What tool should I look into?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use InterCellar. It is an R/Bioconductor package featuring a Shiny application that allows users to interactively analyze cell-cell communication from scRNA-seq datasets starting from precomputed ligand-receptor interactions.

---

## [1100] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a high-content screening study and need to import reference microscopy image datasets from the Image Data Resource directly into my Galaxy history. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Download IDR/OMERO' tool (Data Source). By default, it downloads a tarball containing individual images from IDR directly into your Galaxy history.

---

## [1101] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working in an R environment on Linux and want to align sequencing reads using BWA-MEM or BWA-backtrack directly within my R session. Is there a package available?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `Rbwa`. It provides an R wrapper for BWA alignment algorithms, supporting both BWA-MEM and BWA-backtrack, along with functions to build reference genome indexes directly from R.

## [1102] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a metagenomic study on antibiotic resistance gene mobility and need to annotate bacterial genomes for integron detection. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Integron Finder. It is tailored for genomics and metagenomics applications, specifically supporting bacterial genome annotation for integron detection.

---

## [1103] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have proteomics identification results and want to keep only peptide hits that contain variable modifications. What tool can do this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `IDFilter` from the Proteomics module. It is designed to filter your dataset to keep only peptide hits with variable modifications.

---

## [1104] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on genome assembly and need a tool to count k-mers and output the counts as a table. What do you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend Meryl, a k-mer counter in the assembly domain that extracts k-mer counts and outputs them in tabular format.

---

## [1105] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with RNA-Seq data and need a tool dedicated to gene fusion detection and fusion visualization. What tool fits this purpose?

**ASSISTANT:** Arriba (and the Arriba Visualisation tool) is designed specifically for detecting and visualizing gene fusions from RNA-Seq data.

---

## [1106] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need an assembly polisher that can utilize short reads and long reads (such as Illumina and Nanopore). What tool would you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend Hypo. It is an assembly polisher designed for polishing assemblies using short (and long) reads, supporting Illumina and Nanopore technologies.

---

## [1107] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on environmental metagenomic sequencing data and want to assemble higher quality viral contigs and genomes. Is there an nf-core tool suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use `cobrameta`. It is an nf-core tool specifically designed to obtain higher quality viral genomes assembled from metagenomic datasets.

---

## [1108] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I assembled metagenome-assembled genomes (MAGs) from an uncharacterized environment containing novel bacterial and archaeal clades. Which tool can assess completeness and contamination without depending heavily on taxonomic marker sets?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `CheckM2`. Unlike CheckM1, CheckM2 uses universally trained machine learning models applied regardless of taxonomic lineage to predict the completeness and contamination of genomic bins. This makes it particularly effective for novel lineages that have limited taxonomic representation.

---

## [1109] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am configuring a tool generator for presenting complex data transformation outputs in a clean, accessible manner. Which component should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `docker scriptrunner`. It is recommended for presenting complex outputs in an accessible manner.

---

## [1110] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with spatial transcriptomics data and need a tool to identify cross-regional cell type-specific differential signals between selected regions of interest.

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `RegionalST`. It is designed for spatial transcriptomics bioinformatics workflows to select regions of interest (ROIs) and identify cross-regional cell type-specific differential signals.

---

## [1111] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a forensics and population genetics project where I need to perform microsatellite detection and STR genotyping from sequencing data. Which tool fits this requirement?

**ASSISTANT:** The tool 'STR detection (Next Gen Mappers)' is well-suited for this task. Its primary omics applications include genomics, population genetics, and forensics, and it is specifically designed for short tandem repeat (STR) genotyping and microsatellite detection from sequencing data.

---

## [1112] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting an NMR-based metabolomics study and have nmrML files. I need a tool to automatically generate standardized ISA-Tab metadata files. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend 'nmrml2isa'. It is specifically designed for metabolomics workflows to automatically generate ISA-Tab metadata files directly from nmrML data files.

---

## [1113] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool in Bioconductor to harvest bioinformatic ontologies like cell types and anatomy using CRAN's ontologyIndex. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `ontoProc`. It supports the harvesting of diverse bioinformatic ontologies using the `ontologyIndex` package on CRAN and provides snapshots of terms for cells, cell lines, chemical compounds, and anatomy.

---

## [1114] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** My FASTA headers have long descriptive annotations after the sequence identifiers that are breaking my downstream tools. Is there a utility to strip everything after the first space?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `Remove Description`. It takes a FASTA file and removes the description field—defined as all characters after the first space in the header line until the carriage return—keeping only the FASTA ID (from the `>` to the first space).

---

## [1115] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am preparing sequences for an annotation pipeline and need to run repeat masking before submitting them to BLAST. Which tool can handle the second stage of WindowMasker?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use WindowMasker_ustat. It is designed for sequence analysis and genomics workflows, specifically running stage 2 of the WindowMasker analysis to identify repeats within input sequences prior to downstream tasks like BLAST searches.

---

## [1116] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can you recommend a cytometry tool for non-parametric gating and cell population identification?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend 'flowMeans'. It performs non-parametric flow cytometry data gating and identifies cell populations using change point detection based on segmented regression.

---

## [1117] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to export contact matrix data stored inside a .cool file into plain text format. Which tool can do this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Cooler Dump`. It extracts data directly from a cooler matrix file and outputs it to a text stream.

---

## [1118] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have glycan linear code representations containing uncertain leaves and ambiguous linkages. Which tool can resolve these fuzzy glycans into all possible unfuzzy structures?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the `GAP Permute Linearcode` web services tool. It is specifically designed for glycomics to convert fuzzy glycans to unfuzzy glycans by computing all permutations of linear code with uncertain leaves.

---

## [1119] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am looking for a machine learning tool in Galaxy to automate parameter tuning and hyperparameter optimization across general learning models. What tool is available?

**ASSISTANT:** `Generic Learner Hyperopt` is available for this purpose. It is categorized under Machine Learning and handles hyperparameter optimization for generic learning algorithms.

---

## [1120] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which utility in the wipertools suite should I call if I need to combine multiple fastqwiper summary logs into a single merged report?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Wipertools Reportgather`. It is specifically built for report merging and union operations on reports created by `wipertools_fastqwiper`.

---

## [1121] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an mzspeclib metabolomics library file and want to inspect its basic metadata, library type, and total spectra count. Which tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `mzspeclib describe`. This tool summarizes and describes mzspeclib files, giving you an overview of the content including spectrum count, library type, and related metadata.

## [1122] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool to filter and dereplicate my 16S amplicon reads before assessing microbial diversity. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'FROGS Pre-process', which specifically filters and dereplicates amplicons for diversity analysis in metagenomics and 16S/ITS rRNA studies.

## [1123] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on motif scanning in a non-profit academic ChIP-seq project. Which tool can I run, keeping in mind academic licensing constraints?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use FIMO in the ChIP-seq category. Note that FIMO is restricted strictly to non-commercial, educational, and research/non-profit use, which aligns with your academic project requirements.

## [1124] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have multiple transcript quantification output files from Salmon RNA-seq runs and need to combine them into a single matrix. What tool from the Salmon suite should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Salmon quantmerge'. It is designed specifically to merge multiple transcript abundance quantification outputs generated by Salmon into a consolidated table.

---

## [1125] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have tabular data and need to perform column selection and filtering before downstream analysis. What tool is suitable?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use 'csvtk-cut', which is designed for tabular data preprocessing requiring column selection and restructuring prior to downstream analysis.

---

## [1126] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have multiple VCF records from different sources that I need to combine into one dataset, either via full merge or set union. What sequence analysis tool does this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Combine Variants. It combines VCF records across different sources and supports both full merges (uniquifying overlapping samples) and set unions.

---

## [1127] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on AIRR-seq BCR data and need a tool to construct consensus reads that can filter out reads with minority primer sequences. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use pRESTO BuildConsensus. It is designed for immunogenomics and AIRR-seq (BCR/TCR sequencing) and allows you to set a minimum primer frequency threshold to assign a consensus primer and exclude minority primer sequences from consensus building.

---

## [1128] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have high-dimensional metabolomics data and want to conduct network analysis combined with dimension reduction and clustering. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `netboost`, which is tailored for boosting-supported network analysis, clustering, and dimension reduction in high-dimensional omics applications such as metabolomics.

---

## [1129] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with metagenomics data and want an R/phyloseq-based tool that can visualize composition both per ASV and aggregated at a higher taxonomic rank like Phylum. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `FROGSSTAT Phyloseq Composition Visualisation`. It leverages phyloseq and custom R functions to generate two visualization plots: one at the ASV level and another aggregated at a specified taxonomic rank (e.g., Phylum) after applying taxonomic filtering.

---

## [1130] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing study with multiple sample groups and want to evaluate sample clustering using various linkage methods. What tool can I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use 'FROGSSTAT Phyloseq Sample Clustering'. It uses phyloseq and a customized R function to construct clustering plots for cluster analysis with different linkage methods across multiple sample groups.

---

## [1131] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am processing metagenomic alignment files and need a tool that supports disabling lane mask filtering when --lane_mask_fp has a qiime_config default. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `filter_alignment`. It provides the `suppress lane mask filtering` option specifically designed to bypass lane-mask-based filtering under those configuration conditions.

---

## [1132] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with single-cell ATAC sequencing data and need a Bayesian dimension reduction and clustering tool that can perform analysis at the level of motifs, transcription factors, and pathways. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using **ATACCoGAPS**. It provides tools for running the Bayesian CoGAPS algorithm (Fertig et al, 2010) on single-cell ATAC-seq data and supports analyses at the level of genes, motifs, transcription factors (TFs), or pathways, as well as transfer learning.

---

## [1133] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an aligned SAM/BAM file, but I need to strip alignment information and restore the original base quality scores. What tool can I use for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use RevertSam. It is specifically designed to revert SAM or BAM files to a previous state by removing specified types of alignment information and restoring the original quality scores when they are available.

---

## [1134] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am looking for a structural variant caller that supports Oxford Nanopore and PacBio reads for both germline and somatic analysis. What tool do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `sniffles` (Sniffles2). It is an SV caller designed for long-read data that accurately identifies structural variants across germline, somatic, and population-level settings using PacBio or Oxford Nanopore reads.

---

## [1135] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** In a viral RNA-seq host-pathogen study, I need to enrich viral reads against a reference genome, but I must strip off the reference poly-A tail first. What tool does this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Read It and Keep`. It is designed for viromics, viral metagenomics, and viral RNA-seq host-pathogen studies, and it specifically removes terminal As to ensure the viral reference sequence contains no poly-A tail.

---

## [1136] source=multitask_sft_big

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'ccrepe' package. It is specifically designed to assess the significance of general similarity measures in compositional datasets, such as microbial abundance data.

## [1151] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a list of mouse Ensembl gene IDs (e.g., ENSMUSG...) that I need to map to their corresponding human orthologs. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **Biomni: Interspecies Gene Conversion**. It translates ENSEMBL gene IDs between different species using BioMart homology mapping.

## [1152] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a phylogenetics tool to split and parse gene tree representations containing branch lengths and bootstrap support values. What tool can I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `ETE GeneTree splitter`, which is tailored for phylogenetic analysis and supports gene trees formatted with branch lengths and node support values.

## [1153] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am setting up reference files for a 10x Genomics Space Ranger analysis and need to filter my GTF file. Which module should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **Spaceranger Mkgtf**. It is specifically designed to generate the filtered GTF file required by the 10x Genomics Space Ranger pipeline using the `spaceranger mkgtf` command.

---

## [1154] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to construct an SBML model directly from KEGG pathways for a specific organism. What tool can accomplish this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `Kegg2Sbml`. It is designed for Systems Biology to build SBML files directly from KEGG organism-specific pathways via the KEGG API.

---

## [1155] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a comparative sequence analysis study and want to evaluate correlations between feature occurrences across two DNA datasets at multiple resolution levels. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Compute P-values and Correlation Coefficients for Occurrences of Two Set of Features`. It applies multiscale wavelet analysis to compute correlation coefficient matrices and p-values across multiple scales for feature occurrences across two genomic datasets.

## [1156] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to build a TxDb object from genomic annotations for a specific genome assembly using UCSC or Ensembl sources. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'txdbmaker' (categorized under Annotation, DataImport, and Genetics), which is designed specifically for making TxDb objects from genomic annotations sourced from UCSC, Ensembl, or GFF files.

## [1157] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an edge-list representation of a weighted biological network and need to seriate and modularize it. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `NetSAM` (Network Seriation and Modularization). It takes an edge-list representation of weighted or unweighted networks and performs seriation and modularization analysis.

## [1158] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1160] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We need a machine learning-based somatic variant caller that applies convolutional neural networks to tumor and normal pileups. Which tool fits this description?

## [1161] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a label-free quantitative proteomics study with a two-group design. Which tool provides a robust linear mixed model framework to evaluate differential abundance from peptide or protein intensities?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `msqrob2`. It is designed specifically for assessing differential abundance in quantitative proteomics experiments using a robust linear mixed model framework. Its workflows can handle both raw peptide intensities and summarised protein expression values, and it supports label-free two-group experimental designs.

---

## [1162] source=multitask_sft_big

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `cpgreport`, a sequence analysis tool designed specifically for identifying and reporting CpG islands in nucleotide sequences.

---

## [1163] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on plant genome assembly annotation and need a tool to identify transposable element protein domains using the RepeatExplorer database (REXdb). What do you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using Domain based ANnotation of Transposable Elements - DANTE. It is designed for assembly workflows and annotates TE protein domains by querying the RepeatExplorer database (REXdb), which covers both Viridiplantae and Metazoa, using the LAST alignment program.

---

## [1164] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a large-scale double knock-out screen and need to infer complex signalling pathways from the perturbation data. Which tool is tailored for this?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend epiNEM. It is an extension of the original Nested Effects Models (NEM) specifically tailored towards large-scale double knock-out screens to infer complex network signalling pathways.

---

## [1165] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a text file where consecutive whitespace characters and repeated delimiter characters are causing parsing errors. Is there a simple text manipulation tool in Galaxy to collapse consecutive characters?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'Condense' tool from the Text Manipulation suite. It is designed specifically to condense consecutive characters of a specified type into a single instance.

---

## [1166] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have genome annotation files in GFF and GTF formats, and I need a straightforward way to convert them into TSV files. Which AGAT tool can do this?

---

## [1167] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We have RNA-seq read counts and want to diagnose GC-content and length biases while performing within-lane and between-lane normalization. Which workflow is suitable?

---

## [1168] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have pre-clinical drug combination screening data and need to calculate the degree of synergy using Loewe, Bliss, and ZIP models. What software tool should I use?

---

## [1169] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am setting up a bacteriophage genome annotation pipeline and need a Data Manager to fetch the pharokka reference database. What tool is available for this?

---

## [1170] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have pepXML search results from proteomics mass spectrometry and need a tool that can parse them into a peptide-spectrum-matching tabular file and filter PSMs by FDR. Any recommendations?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `pepXMLTab`. It is specifically designed for proteomics and metabolomics to parse pepXML files generated across different search engines into tabular peptide-spectrum-matching (PSM) format, and it includes built-in functionality to filter PSMs based on FDR.

---

## [1171] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have 16S rRNA gene amplicon sequencing data from a comparative study with categorical variables and want to run a Multivariate Analysis of Variance (MANOVA). What tool would you suggest?

---

## [1172] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing nucleosome mapping data from an MNase-seq experiment and need to calculate statistics across gene annotations. Which tool is suitable?

---

## [1173] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am performing quality control on gene expression microarrays and need to generate a spatial image of the array to check for surface artifacts. What tool do you recommend?

## [1174] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1175] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am processing CyCIF images and need a module to subtract autofluorescence on pre-stitched multi-channel TIFFs scaled by exposure time. What tool handles this?

---

## [1176] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on an epigenetics analysis and need a tool to generate violin plots. Which tool fits this requirement?

---

## [1177] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on GWAS prioritization and want to incorporate pleiotropy information alongside functional annotations, preferably with an interactive visualization feature. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **GPA**. It provides a statistical framework to prioritize GWAS results by integrating pleiotropy information and annotation data, and it includes **ShinyGPA** for interactive visualization.

---

## [1178] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a bacterial genome assembly and antimicrobial resistance genomics project in Galaxy and need a tool to extract JSON outputs from multiple tools. Which tool is designed for this?

---

## [1179] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a genome-wide association study and need a method based on a parametric semi-supervised mixture model to detect marker effects without strictly distinguishing between main and interactive effects. Which tool fits this requirement?

---

## [1180] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a high-content microscopy experiment where multiple adjacent fields of view were acquired for each sample. Which module should I use to assemble them into a single image grid?
