---

## [1181] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have called ChIP-seq peaks using MACS and need a tool to create pie charts showing their genomic distribution. What tool should I look for?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `Pieplots MACS`, which is tailored for ChIP-seq workflows to generate pie plot summaries of MACS peak distributions.

---

## [1182] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I want to separate transcriptional from post-transcriptional regulation using standard RNA-seq data by comparing mature and pre-mRNA levels. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `eisaR`. It implements Exon-Intron Split Analysis (EISA) on standard RNA-seq data, measuring changes in mature RNA and pre-mRNA reads across experimental conditions to quantify transcriptional and post-transcriptional gene regulation.

---

## [1183] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a vertebrate whole-genome assembly and need to locate telomeric regions using standard repeat sequences. What lightweight tool can I use for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `seqtk_telo` from the Sequence Analysis suite. It specifically identifies telomeres in genomic sequences using the default vertebrate repeat motif `CCCTAA`, or a custom repeat sequence if specified.

---

## [1184] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am browsing graphics processing tools and need one for smoothing filters. Which tool matches this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `Filter - Smooth`, which is listed under the Graphics category.

---

## [1185] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with flow cytometry bead standard datasets and need an automated package to analyze the beads for signal normalization. What Bioconductor tool is designed for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'flowBeads'. This package extends flowCore specifically for cytometry bead data and aims to automate bead analysis for normalisation.

---

## [1186] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with spatially resolved transcriptomics generated from serial cryosections coupled to high-throughput RNA sequencing. Which R tool can I use to reconstruct 3D spatial gene expression patterns?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `tomoseqr`. It is an R package specifically developed for analyzing Tomo-seq data (genome-wide RNA tomography combining cryosectioning with high-throughput sequencing) to reconstruct 3D gene expression patterns.

---

## [1187] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have DESeq2 differential expression results from several independent transcriptomic studies. Is there a tool that integrates them into a meta-analysis?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use the `RNA-seq data meta-analysis` tool. It takes results from multiple DESeq2 analyses and conducts a meta-analysis across independent studies.

---

## [1188] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I want to import pre-computed RNA-seq gene and exon count matrices and RangedSummarizedExperiment objects from the recount project into R. Which tool is recommended?

**ASSISTANT:** The Bioconductor package `recount` is recommended. It allows you to explore and download RangedSummarizedExperiment objects at the gene, exon, or exon-exon junction levels, alongside raw counts and phenotype data from the recount resource.

---

## [1189] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on microbial community profiling from metagenomic sequencing data and need to profile pathway presence, absence, and abundance. What tool is suitable?

**ASSISTANT:** You should consider `Regroup` from the HUMAnN pipeline. It is specifically designed for metagenomic and metatranscriptomic data to profile the presence/absence and abundance of microbial pathways in microbial community profiling studies.

---

## [1190] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** What tool can I use to quickly get an alignment count summary grouped by bitwise FLAG categories from an aligned BAM file?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use Samtools Flagstat. It parses BAM, SAM, or CRAM alignment files and outputs the counts of alignments corresponding to each FLAG type.

---

## [1191] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am setting up a 16S rRNA gene sequencing preprocessing pipeline and need a tool to assess sequence counts during the initial quality control step. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'Count the sequences' tool, which is suited for initial data assessment and sequence quality control in 16S rRNA gene sequencing, metabarcoding, and metagenomics workflows.

---

## [1192] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have Staphylococcus phage genome sequencing data and want to extract all Receptor Binding Proteins (RBPs) while identifying polymorphisms. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use PhageSNP. It is designed for phage genomics to extract Receptor Binding Proteins (RBPs) and uncover inherent polymorphisms in Staphylococcus phage genomes.

---

## [1193] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** In my metagenomics data analysis, I need to summarize my table such that I get the number of unique observation IDs per sample instead of the overall observation count. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the tool 'Summarize sample or observation data'. It is designed specifically to present counts as the number of unique observation IDs per sample.

---

## [1194] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to query metadata and search sequencing datasets from the Sequence Read Archive (SRA) programmatically. What tool can I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `SRAdb`. It is designed to query the Sequence Read Archive (SRA), the public repository containing next-generation sequencing platform data.

---

## [1195] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a proteomics project and need a tool to export OpenSWATH results that have undergone semi-supervised learning and scoring. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'PyProphet export', which handles exporting OpenSWATH results following semi-supervised learning and scoring in proteomics.

---

## [1196] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working in R and want to save Biostrings XStringSets to disk in a structured format with metadata that is more portable across systems than raw RDS serialization. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `alabaster.string`. It is designed to save Biostrings objects (such as XStringSets) to file artifacts and load them back into memory, providing a portable, metadata-associated alternative to standard RDS serialization.

---

## [1197] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have sequencing data in FASTQ format from a metagenomics project and need to organize it by sequence length before quality control. Which tool fits this requirement?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use 'SeqKit sort', which sorts FASTA and FASTQ sequences by length, sequence, id, or name, and is suited for genomics, transcriptomics, and metagenomics workflows.

---

## [1198] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a comparative microbial community study using 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing across multiple experimental groups. Which tool should I use to build a random forest classification model?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Classify.rf. It is designed for metagenomics, 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing, and microbial community analysis to perform random forest classification on multi-group comparative datasets.

---

## [1199] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with mass spectrometry imaging data in metabolomics and need a tool to visualize mass spectra from my Cardinal MSImageSet RData file. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'MSI plot spectra' tool, which is designed for metabolomics mass spectrometry imaging workflows and accepts Cardinal MSImageSet files saved as RData.

---

## [1200] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have stored Galaxy history export archives containing datasets_attrs and jobs_attrs.txt files and want a quick way to view their job and dataset attributes in an HTML table. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use History Metadata Extractor, which extracts information from 'jobs_attrs.txt' and 'datasets_attrs' files in exported histories and presents the metadata in an HTML table.

---

## [1201] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool should I use if I want to perform gene-level differential expression analysis specifically utilizing RNA-seq exon count data?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `EBSEA` (Differential Expression by Exon Counts), as it specifically calculates gene differential expression based on RNA-seq exon-level counts.

---

## [1202] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** In a bioengineering project, I need to model trajectories of neural activity and decode behavioral variables. Is there a dedicated tool for this?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use **Biomni: Decode Behavior From Neural Trajectories**, which is designed specifically within bioengineering to model neural activity trajectories and decode behavioral variables.

---

## [1203] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to perform comparative genomics between two bacterial strains by aligning both forward and reverse strands of two FASTA files and visualizing synteny with a dotplot. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `MUMmer dotplot`. It takes two FASTA files, compares both strands using MUMmer 3 algorithms (`mummer`, `nucmer`, or `promer`), and visualizes the results with `mummerplot`.

---

## [1204] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have MeDIP-seq data and need a package for inferential analysis to detect differential methylation at CpG sites. What tool fits this purpose?

**ASSISTANT:** SIMD is suitable for this task. It provides an inferential analysis method based on an EM algorithm specifically for detecting differentially expressed CpG sites in MeDIP-seq data.

---

## [1205] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to analyze copy numbers for full-length SMN1 and SMN2 from whole-genome sequencing BAM files. What tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Smncopynumbercaller. It is designed to call the copy number of full-length SMN1, full-length SMN2, and SMN2Δ7–8 (SMN2 with an Exon 7-8 deletion) from whole-genome sequencing (WGS) BAM or CRAM files.

---

## [1206] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on transcriptomics data and need an algorithm to discover novel network structures by combining multiple complementary data sources. What tool can I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use pandaR. It executes the PANDA algorithm, which discovers novel network structures by combining complementary information from multiple data sources in transcriptomics.

---

## [1207] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a visualization tool for my ChIP-seq data to show chromatin modification enrichment across genomic features of variable lengths without length bias. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `CHROMATRA T`. It visualizes chromatin modification enrichment across genomic features (such as all transcripts) in an unbiased manner by accounting for feature length, and can group features by a secondary characteristic.

---

## [1208] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing DNA methylation microarray data and need to remove technical batch effects without overcorrecting and wiping out biological variation. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** Harman is well-suited for this task. It operates on PCA and constrained optimization to maximize batch effect correction in epigenomics and microarray datasets while maintaining a strict constraint against overcorrection.

---

## [1209] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a new reference genome in FASTA format and I want to construct an index specifically for BWA-MEME. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Bwameme Index' tool, which is designed to create a BWA-MEME index from your reference genome fasta file.

---

## [1210] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** My pipeline outputs TB-Profiler results in JSON format, but I need them as a spreadsheet table. Is there a dedicated utility for this?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use the `Convert tbprofiler json report to tabular` tool in the Sequence Analysis category to parse your JSON report directly into a tabular format.

---

## [1211] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are designing a metabolic engineering pipeline to develop biosensors for small molecule detection. Which software can isolate the relevant biosensor pathway from RetroPath2.0 output and produce an interactive visualization?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `rp2biosensor`. It is specifically tailored for synthetic biology pathway design and biosensor development workflows, extracting the compound-to-biosensor subnetwork from RetroPath2.0 outputs and rendering an interactive web page.

---

## [1212] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are studying tumor gene expression profiles with only a small number of normal tissue samples available, and we want to detect genes where expression is lost in subsets of tumors. Which tool fits this scenario?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `receptLoss`. It is specifically designed to identify genes whose expression is lost in subsets of tumors relative to normal tissue, and it is particularly well-suited when the number of normal tissue samples is small.

---

## [1213] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to perform cancer patient clustering by integrating multiple layers of omics data using affinity network fusion. What package should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'ANF' (Affinity Network Fusion). It is specifically designed for complex patient clustering by integrating multi-omic datasets through affinity network fusion.

---

## [1214] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am designing a transcriptomics study using gene expression microarrays and need to estimate the appropriate sample size. Which tool can help calculate and visualize this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'ssize', a software package specifically designed for computing and displaying sample size information for gene expression microarray experiments in transcriptomics.

---

## [1215] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing RNA-seq data and need to identify splice junctions from my aligned BAM files to feed into Leafcutter. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Regtools Junctionsextract`. It extracts exon-exon junctions from RNA-seq BAM files and outputs them as a BED12 file, which is specifically designed for downstream splicing analysis workflows like Leafcutter.

---

## [1216] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to convert my OTU table between different BIOM formats for a metagenomic study. What tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'Convert' tool from the Metagenomics category, designed specifically to convert between BIOM table formats and standardize OTU tables.

---

## [1217] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a set of ChIP-seq peak coordinates and want to identify the nearest transcription start site from an annotation file. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Bedtools Closest`. For each feature in an input set A (such as your ChIP-seq peaks), it identifies the closest genomic feature (upstream or downstream) in input set B (such as your gene annotations).

---

## [1218] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to prepare and download the genome resource files required to run the STAR-Fusion caller. Which tool can handle this setup step?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Starfusion Build'. This tool downloads the STAR-Fusion genome resource package necessary for running the STAR-Fusion caller.

---

## [1219] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool can I use to compute coverage across the genome for alignment files from my ChIP-seq and RNA-seq experiments?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `IGVtools count`. It computes average feature density over a specified window size across the genome, commonly used for computing coverage from alignment files and counting hits in ChIP-seq experiments.

---

## [1220] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have genotyping data stored in GDS format and want an array-like interface in R to subset and manipulate nodes without loading entire datasets into memory. What package should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `GDSArray` package. It provides the `GDSArray` class, which wraps nodes in GDS files into a matrix-like/array-like representation suitable for subsetting and mathematical transformations.

---

## [1221] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with microarray data and need a bioinformatics tool that can handle preprocessing for both individual single arrays and array batches. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `frma`. It is designed for bioinformatics preprocessing and analysis across single microarrays as well as batches of microarrays.

---

## [1222] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can you suggest an R/software package to assess genomic instability from single-cell transcriptomics data?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend the genomicInstability package. It is specifically designed for transcriptomics and single-cell transcriptomics to run genomic instability analysis (GIA) from scRNA-Seq data using the aREA algorithm.

---

## [1223] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a bioengineering project and need to retrieve standard baseline parameters for a whole-cell model. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `default_whole_cell_model`, which provides the default parameters for whole cell modeling in bioengineering applications.

---

## [1224] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a Picard tool to modify the sample name inside my VCF file. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the tool 'Picard Renamesampleinvcf' (picard/renamesampleinvcf), which changes the name of a sample in a VCF file.

---

## [1225] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have long DNA sequences and metagenome-assembled genomes (MAGs) that need taxonomic assignment. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Catpack Contigs'. It is designed for taxonomic classification of long DNA sequences and metagenome-assembled genomes, such as assembled contigs and MAGs/bins.

---

## [1226] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have sequencing reads from a mixed sample and need to separate them by mapping simultaneously against multiple reference genomes. Which tool can handle this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Bbmap Bbsplit. It is specifically designed to split sequencing reads by mapping them to multiple reference genomes simultaneously.

---

## [1227] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a case-control GWAS with unrelated subjects and need a model-based method to estimate ancestry from a large autosomal SNP genotype dataset. What tool fits this criteria?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using `Admixture`. It is specifically designed to estimate ancestry in a model-based manner from large autosomal SNP genotype datasets of unrelated individuals, such as those in case-control association studies.

---

## [1228] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have clustered and taxonomically annotated OTUs from a 16S rRNA gene amplicon study. I need a tool to assess taxonomic metrics and produce an HTML report on affiliation quality. What FROGS module does this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'FROGS Affiliations stat' tool. It computes multiple taxonomic metrics and generates an interactive HTML report detailing OTUs, their assigned taxonomies, and the overall affiliation quality.

---

## [1229] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have metagenomic contig assemblies and need a tool to identify and predict full-length antibiotic resistance genes. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Fargene. It is specifically designed as an antibiotic resistance gene (ARG) identifier that processes metagenomic contigs and longer sequences to predict and deliver full-length antibiotic resistance genes.

---

## [1230] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing short-read sequencing data and need an integrated structural variant caller that can detect deletions, tandem duplications, inversions, and translocations at single-nucleotide resolution. What tool fits this description?

**ASSISTANT:** Delly fits your requirements. It is an integrated structural variant prediction method designed to discover, genotype, and visualize deletions, tandem duplications, inversions, and translocations at single-nucleotide resolution in short-read massively parallel sequencing data.

---

## [1231] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool in cell biology to analyze flow cytometry data and quantify distinct cell populations based on surface markers. What can I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `Biomni: Analyze Flow Cytometry Immunophenotyping`. It is specifically designed to analyze flow cytometry data to identify and quantify specific cell populations according to surface marker profiles.

---

## [1232] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am looking for a tool from the LocARNA suite to compare two RNA sequences and calculate arc and base match probabilities while taking secondary structure into account. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the LocARNA Pairwise Probability Aligner. It is designed for simultaneous RNA folding and alignment, computing base and arc match probabilities for pairwise RNA sequence comparisons with structural considerations.

---

## [1233] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I generated multiple sequence alignments using two different alignment programs and want to compare the two MSAs directly to evaluate them. What tool can I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use **Tcoffee Alncompare**. It is specifically designed to compare two alternative multiple sequence alignments (MSAs) to evaluate their differences.

---

## [1234] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on sequence analysis and need a tool to scan genomic sequences for short tandem repeat motifs of specific lengths and output them in BED format if they exceed a repeat threshold. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'STR to bed' tool from the Sequence Analysis suite. It outputs BED features for every motif matching the selected length(s) that satisfies or exceeds the minimum required repeat count.

---

## [1235] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to benchmark a single-cell analysis method by simulating realistic single-cell and spatial omics data derived from real reference data parameters. Which tool can generate this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'scDesign3'. It is a statistical simulator that learns interpretable parameters from real data to generate realistic single-cell and spatial omics datasets across varied cell states, experimental designs, and feature modalities.

---

## [1236] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool to perform sensitive protein sequence searching based on pairwise alignment of hidden Markov models. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend Hhsuite Hhblits, which provides fast and sensitive protein sequence searching based on the pairwise alignment of hidden Markov models (HMMs).

---

## [1237] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a quality control tool to visualize and chart base qualities cycle by cycle from my aligned sequencing data. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `MeanQualityByCycle`. It charts the distribution of base qualities by cycle within reads supplied in a SAM or BAM dataset.

---

## [1238] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have individual gVCF files from multiple samples and need to merge them to perform joint variant calling. Which tool is designed for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **Glnexus**. It is specifically designed to merge gVCF files and perform joint variant calling across samples.

---

## [1239] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on targeted proteomics data from SRM and PRM experiments and need a tool that can perform retention time normalization, specifically one that can evaluate and pick peptides according to peak shape.

**ASSISTANT:** You should use OpenSwathRTNormalizer. It is designed for proteomics workflows, including targeted Selected Reaction Monitoring (SRM) and Parallel Reaction Monitoring (PRM) experiments, and supports choosing optimal peptides based on peak shape for RT normalization.

---

## [1240] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have multiple sequence alignments and need a sequence analysis tool to generate a profile HMM from each alignment. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'hmmbuild' from the Sequence Analysis category. It takes multiple sequence alignments from an input file and builds a profile HMM for each.

---

## [1241] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a proteomics project and need a tool for peptide-centric quantification where MS/MS identifications are already available to guide targeted feature extraction. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend FeatureFinderIdentification from OpenMS (TOPP). It is specifically tailored for proteomics workflows requiring peptide-centric quantification and targeted feature extraction based on available MS/MS identifications.

---

## [1242] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have gene expression data and want to perform unsupervised clustering across my samples using either K-means or DBSCAN. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the Cluster Analysis (Transcriptomics) tool. It is specifically designed for transcriptomics and gene expression analysis to perform unsupervised discovery of patterns across samples using K-means, agglomerative clustering, or DBSCAN.

---

## [1243] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool in the single-cell domain that helps identify sources of cellular heterogeneity and prepare data for cross-dataset integration. What tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Seurat Run Dimensional Reduction`. It is designed within the Seurat single-cell framework to help users identify and interpret sources of heterogeneity from single-cell transcriptomic measurements and integrate diverse single-cell data types.

---

## [1244] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need an nf-core tool to derive copy number profiles from tumour sequencing data while accounting for aneuploidy and normal tissue contamination. What tool fits this need?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'ascat' (available in nf-core). ASCAT derives copy number profiles of tumour cells while explicitly accounting for normal cell admixture and tumour aneuploidy.

---

## [1245] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have annotation files in GTF and GFF formats and need to convert them to BED. Which BEDOPS tool does this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use Bedops Convert2Bed, which converts BAM, GFF, GTF, GVF, and PSL files into BED format.

---

## [1246] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing cancer RNA-seq data and need both breakpoint detection and complete transcript reconstruction to predict the protein-level effect of gene fusions. What tool fits this need?

**ASSISTANT:** Defuse Trinity is suitable for this purpose. It combines deFuse fusion breakpoint detection with Trinity de novo assembly to provide the full sequence of the chimeric transcript, enabling downstream assessment of protein coding effects.

---

## [1247] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Some reads in my BAM file extend past the reference chromosome boundaries, and unmapped reads have non-zero MAPQ values. Is there a tool that fixes these specific issues?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you should use Picard CleanSam. It soft-clips alignments that extend beyond the end of the reference and resets the MAPQ of unmapped reads to 0.

---

## [1248] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have microarray gene expression profiling data and need to perform sample clustering based on expression patterns. Which tool is tailored for this?

**ASSISTANT:** Cluster 3 is suitable for your analysis. Its primary omics focus is microarray gene expression profiling, specifically designed for sample clustering based on expression patterns and gene co-expression analysis.

---

## [1249] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am designing a lab automation script and need a helper utility that supports asynchronous execution. What tool do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using `run_async`, a tool in the `lab_automation` category designed as a helper for running asynchronous operations.

---

## [1250] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am performing sequence analysis to quantify genome coverage across my samples, but I need a tool that accounts for whether my contigs came from individual sample assemblies or a co-assembly. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `CoverM genome`. It is specifically designed for genome-level coverage calculations in sequence analysis, and accounting for whether contigs were produced via co-assembly or individual assembly is a key factor when using it.

---

## [1251] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have identified structural variants in a VCF file and need to query an existing structural variant database to see if these variants have been observed before. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Svdb Query' (from svdb), which allows you to query a structural variant database using a VCF file as the input query.

---

## [1252] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on transcriptomics data and have generated two separate sliding window sets from two experimental conditions. What tool can I use to combine them for differential expression analysis?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'merge sliding windows clusters' tool (under Convert Formats). It is designed to merge two different sliding windows sets into one for comparative gene expression and differential expression studies.

---

## [1253] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting RNA structural analysis and need a tool for Hidden Markov Model comparison studies and RNA family annotation. What do you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend 'hmmcv'. It is designed for RNA structural analysis and comparative genomics, specifically for Hidden Markov Model comparison studies and RNA family annotation and classification.

---

## [1254] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** In the Biomni molecular biology suite, which tool can tell me if a specific genomic coordinate or position falls within any predefined region?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `Biomni: Is Position Covered` tool, which is designed specifically to check if a position is covered by any region.

---

## [1255] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have paired-end Illumina reads from a metagenomic library and want to generate consensus sequences from overlapping reads or merge non-overlapping ones. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Illuminapairedend`. It is designed to align paired-end reads from Illumina platforms, outputting a consensus sequence with quality scores if reads overlap, or concatenating them if they do not.

---

## [1256] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with spatial transcriptomics data and need to identify spatial domains with smooth boundaries across neighboring locations. Can you suggest a suitable tool?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `smoothclust`. It is designed for spatial domain segmentation and spatially-aware clustering in spatial transcriptomics data. It creates spatial domains with smooth boundaries by smoothing gene expression profiles across neighboring spatial coordinates prior to unsupervised clustering.

---

## [1257] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool can I use to convert chemical data stored in Chemical Markup Language (CML) into InChI identifiers?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'CML to InChI' tool under Tool Dependency Packages to convert CML records into InChI format.

---

## [1258] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on sequence analysis for metagenomic assemblies and need a tool to calculate contig-level coverage metrics while considering whether samples were assembled individually or co-assembled. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use CoverM contig. It is designed for contig sequence analysis and is useful to know whether contigs were generated across all samples together (co-assembly) or on each sample individually (individual assembly).

---

## [1259] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I just received raw sequencing reads from a genomics experiment and want to perform quality control by strictly checking that the FASTQ files are properly formatted. Which tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'FastQ Validator' tool. It validates the format of FASTQ files prior to downstream analyses and is built on LibStatGen: FASTQ from the libStatGen library.

---

## [1260] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1265] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1268] source=multitask_sft_big

It is a probabilistic modelling pipeline specifically designed for structure probing experiments to compute per-nucleotide posterior probabilities of modification, supporting multiple experimental replicates and empirically correcting for coverage variations.

## [1269] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1270] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1282] source=multitask_sft_big

It is specifically designed for epigenomics ChIP-seq analyses to locate transcription factor (TF) high accumulation DNA zones—genomic regions characterized by a dense presence of different transcription factors—starting from datasets of TF binding region positions.

## [1284] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1305] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to perform a genome-wide scan for local RNA secondary structure and thermodynamics on very long transcripts without hitting severe memory bottlenecks. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** For genome-wide RNA secondary structure scanning and local structure prediction in long RNAs, RNAplfold is recommended. It computes local base pair probabilities restricted to a maximal span L, using O(n + L^2) memory for a sequence of length n.

## [1307] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1308] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which genomics tool can decompose a tumor mutation catalog into known signatures using quadratic programming?

**ASSISTANT:** decompTumor2Sig is designed for this task. It applies quadratic programming to refit mutation catalogs from individual tumor samples against given mutational signatures (Alexandrov or Shiraishi models) to compute the corresponding signature weights.

## [1309] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1310] source=multitask_sft_big

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend the `Multilevel` tool. It provides the `withinVariation` function specifically designed for metabolomics studies with repeated measures or cross-over designs to decompose within-subject variation in the data matrix prior to multivariate analysis.

---

## [1311] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on morphological phenotyping from cell imaging data and need to calculate size-dependent texture spectrums. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the MeasureGranularity tool. It is suited for microscopy and cell imaging in morphological phenotyping and high-content screening assays, measuring texture by fitting structuring elements of increasing size into the image.

## [1315] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am processing multi-sample PCR amplicon metagenomics data and need to filter out PCR-generated hybrid artifacts where incomplete extensions primed onto similar sequences. Which tool should I use?

---

## [1316] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1317] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1318] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1319] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1321] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1322] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1324] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1325] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1327] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1334] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1335] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1336] source=multitask_sft_big

## [1337] source=multitask_sft_big
