## [1338] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on regulatory genomics and transcription factor binding site analysis. I have a list of aligned binding sequences and need to generate a motif file in MEME format. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use 'create-motif-file'. It generates a MEME motif format file directly from either raw sequence lists or FASTA-formatted sequences.

---

## [1339] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which bioengineering simulation tool can model the full CRISPR-Cas9 editing pipeline from gRNA design through delivery to final analysis?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Biomni: Perform Crispr Cas9 Genome Editing', which simulates the complete CRISPR-Cas9 process including guide RNA design, delivery, and analysis.

---

## [1340] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have ChIP-Seq differential binding signatures and want to analyze gene regulatory network enrichment to identify cofactors. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'vulcan'. It combines ChIP-Seq differential binding signatures with gene regulatory network analysis to infer significantly enriched cofactors.

---

## [1341] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Is there a literature search tool that allows me to query Google Scholar and fetch the top result?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use the `Query Scholar` tool in the literature domain. It queries Google Scholar using your specified search term and returns the first search result.

---

## [1342] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool to evaluate my genome assembly's nucleotide composition, specifically calculating metrics like GC percentage and the AT/GC ratio for classification. What tool can I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the `NucleScore tool`. It runs `nucleScore.pl`, a Perl script designed to extract nucleotide data such as GC percent and AT/GC ratio for genome assembly quality assessment and classification.

---

## [1343] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to evaluate a structural variation pipeline by generating simulated genomic duplications and deletions. Which tool fits this requirement?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Simulate CNV`. It is a variant analysis CNV simulator designed to generate copy number variations, including genomic duplications and deletions.

---

## [1344] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are benchmarking low-coverage imputation and need to calculate the genotyping error rate at both the marker level and the sample level. Which GLIMPSE tool is designed for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **Glimpse2 Concordance**. It is specifically designed to compute genotyping error rates at the sample or marker level in low-coverage imputation pipelines.

---

## [1345] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We pooled samples from multiple individuals for barcoded single-cell sequencing, and we have reference genotyping data for each sample. Which tool can assign single-cell barcodes to individual donors and identify multiplets?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Popscle Demuxlet`. It is specifically designed to deconvolute sample identities and identify multiplets in pooled, barcoded single-cell sequencing data using external genotyping data.

---

## [1346] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on an integrative human genomics study and need a tool to discover statistical associations between two human genomic datasets (such as expression and copy number). What tool would you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using SIM. It is specifically designed to find associations between two human genomic datasets, following workflows demonstrated in the SIM vignette for bioinformatics analyses.

---

## [1347] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a statistical classification tool in bioinformatics that evaluates relative feature rankings rather than absolute values, specifically using pairs of features.

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `switchBox`. It provides classification methods based on comparisons of feature pairs (TSP) and supports decision rules such as majority wins for bioinformatics data.

---

## [1348] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I want to detect post-transcriptional modifications from high-throughput sequencing data and need a framework where I can define new classes for custom detection strategies. What tool fits this?

**ASSISTANT:** RNAmodR is well-suited for this. It provides classes and workflows for detecting post-transcriptional modifications and explicitly supports creating new classes for new detection strategies.

---

## [1349] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have genomic signal track data formatted as a bedGraph file and need to convert it into a bigWig file. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `Ucsc Bedgraphtobigwig` converter tool, which is designed specifically to convert bedGraph files into bigWig format.

---

## [1350] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which RNA tool can I use to load 10x single-cell expression data into a sparse Matrix Market format organized by cell barcodes and genes?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use "DropletUtils Read10x" from the RNA tools. It generates raw expression quantification as a sparse matrix in Matrix Market format with barcodes/cells as rows and genes as columns.

---

## [1351] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a list of UIDs in an Entrez database and need a data source tool to retrieve linked records across another Entrez database or check if a relationship exists between them. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use NCBI ELink. It is designed to take a list of UIDs from an Entrez database to find linked UIDs in other Entrez databases, find related UIDs with relevancy scores within the same database, or check for the existence of specific links.

---

## [1352] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to perform de novo gene prediction on a newly sequenced bacterial genome using an interpolated context model (ICM). Which program should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Glimmer3'. It is the main program for prokaryotic gene prediction (bacteria and archaea) based on an interpolated context model (ICM).

---

## [1353] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool categorized under Sequence Analysis that is named kdnapenny. Is that available?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, `kdnapenny` is available under the Sequence Analysis category.

---

## [1354] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have raw long reads from an Oxford Nanopore run that still contain barcode and adapter sequences. What tool should I use to trim adapters and split reads by barcode?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **Porechop**. It is specifically designed for adapter removal and demultiplexing of Oxford Nanopore reads.

---

## [1355] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Could you recommend a tool cataloged under Sequence Analysis named biosed?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, `biosed` is a dedicated tool categorized under Sequence Analysis for sequence editing and processing tasks.

---

## [1356] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool designed for genomics and transcriptomics data to evaluate whether allelic imbalance is confounding our differential expression results. What would you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `tadar`. It is specifically designed for genomics and transcriptomics data to standardise the analysis of Differential Allelic Representation (DAR), preventing allelic representation biases from distorting gene and transcript classification in differential expression studies.

---

## [1357] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have high-dimensional metabolomics and transcriptomics data from a controlled multifactorial experimental design. What R package provides GLM-based ASCA/APCA modeling for such data?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `limpca`. It provides linear models for high-dimensional designed data by applying a GLM version of ASCA and APCA to multivariate profiles, supporting both transcriptomics and metabolomics experiments.

---

## [1358] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I performed a PARIS proximity-ligation experiment and have split-aligned read data. What software package should I use to analyze and cluster these RNA duplex interactions?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'DuplexDiscovereR', a package specifically built to analyze and cluster data from proximity ligation and cross-linking protocols like PARIS using chimerically or split-aligned reads.

---

## [1359] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with large-scale genomic datasets in R and need an interface that supports hierarchical, scalable array-oriented storage across platforms. Which tool fits this requirement?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `gdsfmt`. It provides a high-level R interface to CoreArray Genomic Data Structure (GDS) files, which are portable across platforms and feature a hierarchical structure to store scalable array-oriented datasets with metadata.

---

## [1360] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing data from an experiment with multiple technical replicates per sample. Which tool can I use to aggregate the read counts across replicates into mean OTU abundances?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use 'ampvis2 merge replicates'. It is designed for metagenomics and 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing data to aggregate read counts across replicate samples by computing the mean abundances of OTUs.

---

## [1361] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with NanoString GeoMx spatial transcriptomics data and need an R/Bioconductor tool to read DCC and PKC files, perform quality control, and normalize the data. What tool is designed for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **GeomxTools**. It is specifically designed for NanoString Technologies GeoMx data, providing capabilities to read DCC and PKC files into an ExpressionSet-derived object, alongside built-in quality control and normalization routines.

---

## [1362] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool can I use to merge genomic interval datasets obtained from different sources or processing steps in my genomics/epigenomics project?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'Concatenate (Genomic Interval Operations)' tool. It is specifically suited for genomics, epigenomics, or any omics with genomic interval data to merge interval datasets originating from different sources or processing steps.

---

## [1363] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a genome annotation utility to locate both CRISPR arrays and their associated Cas proteins directly from bacterial DNA sequences. What tool is available?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use CRISPRCasFinder. It is a Perl script specifically designed to identify CRISPR arrays and associated Cas proteins from DNA sequences using default parameters.

---

## [1364] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on gene regulation analysis and need a tool that can score enhancer–gene interactions using the Activity-By-Contact (ABC) model and calculate transcription factor affinities at the gene level. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend Stare. It is a framework specifically designed for genomics and gene regulation to score enhancer–gene interactions using the Activity-By-Contact (ABC) model and derive transcription factor affinities at the gene level.

---

## [1365] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can you recommend a sequence analysis tool from the catalog named ktrnascan_se?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, `ktrnascan_se` is cataloged under the Sequence Analysis category.

---

## [1366] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I opened my gene count table in Microsoft Excel, and gene symbols such as SEPT9 were automatically converted to dates like 9-Sep. Which tool can fix this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `Recover gene symbols` tool (under Text Manipulation). It provides an after-the-fact remedy specifically designed to convert date formats like '9-Sep' back into their original gene symbols (such as SEPT9).

---

## [1367] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which QIIME 2 command should I use to filter amplicon or metagenomics features from my abundance table using frequency thresholds or metadata?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `qiime2 feature-table filter-features`. It filters features from a feature table based on frequency and/or metadata.

---

## [1368] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to search a collection of nucleotide queries against a target nucleotide sequence database. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should run `nhmmer`. It specifically searches nucleotide query files (`<queryfile>`) against nucleotide sequence databases (`<seqdb>`).

---

## [1369] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am preparing alignment and variant files for sequence analysis and need a tool that can sort the records by coordinate start position. What tool is suitable?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **IGVtools sort**. It is designed for sequence analysis, genomics, and variant calling workflows to sort input files by start position.

---

## [1370] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have targeted sequencing data and need to calculate sample coverage and read count binning for copy number variation detection. Which CNVkit command should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `CNVkit Coverage` (or invoke it via the `coverage` or `batch` commands in CNVkit). It calculates read coverage across bins for targeted sequencing or WGS/WES and outputs read count summary statistics.

---

## [1371] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** What tools or Bioconductor packages are executed within the mobilerna pipeline?

**ASSISTANT:** The `mobilerna` pipeline utilizes `bioconductor/mobilerna` and `bioconductor/dplyr` to carry out the identification, quantification, and data processing of mobile sRNAs.

---

## [1372] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool to scan nucleotide sequences for potential intramolecular G-quadruplexes, including non-canonical ones that have imperfect G-runs with mismatches or bulges.

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `pqsfinder`. It detects DNA and RNA sequence patterns likely to form intramolecular G-quadruplexes (G4) and is specifically designed to identify G4 structures with imperfect G-runs containing bulges or mismatches, as well as those with long loops.

---

## [1373] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with several Oxford Nanopore and PacBio long-read sequencing runs. I need to compare their read length distributions, quality, and cumulative yield across runs. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use NanoComp. It is designed to compare multiple runs of long-read sequencing data and alignments, producing length and quality plots along with cumulative yield curves.

---

## [1374] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have high-energy diffraction microscopy (HEDM) data saved in a compressed numpy file (.npz) and I need to convert it into a tessellation raster (.tesr) file for Neper. What tool do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend the tool 'HEDM to tesr'. It creates a Neper-compatible `.tesr` format file directly from an HEDM numpy compressed file.

---

## [1375] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to perform bifurcation analysis on a dynamical model for a synthetic gene circuit and plot the bifurcation diagram. What tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use analyze_bifurcation_diagram. It performs bifurcation analysis on dynamical systems and generates bifurcation diagrams in the synthetic biology domain.

---

## [1376] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which phylogenetic tool supports setting alternative starting tree runs via -N and bootstopping tests like autoMR or autoFC?

**ASSISTANT:** The tool ConcatPhyl provides these features. It allows you to specify the number of alternative runs on distinct starting trees using the `-N|#` option, initiate bootstrap analysis using `-b`, and select bootstopping criteria such as autoMR, autoMRE, autoMRE_IGN, or autoFC.

---

## [1377] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can you recommend a tool associated with bcftools to call regions of homozygosity and detect potential uniparental disomy (UPD)?

**ASSISTANT:** Rhocall Viz is recommended for this purpose. It is designed to call regions of homozygosity (ROH) and make tentative uniparental disomy (UPD) calls.

---

## [1378] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a proteomics tool to perform quality control of raw mass spectrometry data and benchmark our MS acquisition parameters. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** TICCalculator is recommended for this task. It belongs to proteomics and mass spectrometry omics workflows and is specifically suitable for quality control of mass spectrometry raw data and benchmarking of MS acquisition parameters.

---

## [1379] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** In QIIME 2 metagenomics workflows, which tool should I use to inspect, filter, and export my sample metadata interactively?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'qiime2 metadata tabulate', which generates a tabular view of metadata supporting interactive filtering, sorting, and exporting.

---

## [1380] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with metabolomics data involving repeated measures and need to perform an analysis of variance using mixed models. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `mixmodel` tool. In the metabolomics domain, this module is specifically designed to perform analysis of variance for repeated measures using mixed models.

---

## [1381] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have high-throughput sequencing data from a chromatin immunoprecipitation (ChIP-Seq) experiment to study genome-wide protein-DNA interactions. Which tool can I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `MACS2` (Sequence Analysis), which is designed for analyzing chromatin immunoprecipitation followed by high-throughput sequencing (ChIP-Seq) data to study genome-wide protein-DNA interactions.

---

## [1382] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We have run Delly on a cohort of whole-genome sequencing samples and now need to combine the individual structural variant calls across the whole group. What tool should we run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Delly merge' (Variant Analysis). It is designed specifically for multi-sample structural variant discovery and cohort-based SV analysis to merge and consolidate candidate SV sites across samples.

---

## [1383] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have single-cell transcriptomics data from a multi-condition experiment (treatment vs. control) and need to perform dimension reduction and differential expression using a latent embedding multivariate regression model. What tool fits this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `lemur`. It fits a latent embedding multivariate regression (LEMUR) model to multi-condition single-cell transcriptomics data, accommodating treatment vs. control as well as more complex experimental designs for dimension reduction and differential expression.

---

## [1384] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have high-dimensional flow cytometry marker expression data and want to run a kernel-based differential expression analysis. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** cytoKernel is well-suited for this analysis. It implements a kernel-based score test specifically designed to detect differentially expressed features in cytometry and high-dimensional single-cell datasets.

---

## [1385] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a collection of mass spectrometry data files that need quality control and validation before running our downstream proteomics workflows. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `FileInfo`. It is specifically designed for quality control and validation of proteomics and mass spectrometry data files prior to downstream analysis.

---

## [1386] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We have two replicate ATAC-seq experiments and need to evaluate the consistency of our called peak sets. Which tool is standard for measuring peak reproducibility?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Idr (Irreproducible Discovery Rate). It specifically measures reproducibility across ChIP-seq and ATAC-seq peak datasets.

---

## [1387] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a multi-molecule datafile and need a computational chemistry tool to screen for specific substructures using Open Babel. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend the Substructure Search tool. It uses Open Babel FastSearch Index and molecular fingerprints to search multi-molecule datafiles.

---

## [1388] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I ran purge_dups to identify duplicate contigs in my genome assembly. Which tool can I use to visualize the read coverage histogram and evaluate the coverage cutoffs?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Purgedups Histplot`. It generates a read coverage histogram plotting the read depth and cutoff thresholds directly from a purge_dups statistics file.

---

## [1389] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are analyzing ancient DNA sequencing data and looking for a Java-based alternative to mapDamage to evaluate post-mortem damage patterns. What tool do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `damageprofiler`. It is a Java-based tool developed to determine damage patterns on ancient DNA, serving as a replacement for mapDamage.

---

## [1390] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a visualization utility in the platform to generate graphical plots and charts from my data. What tool fits this category?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'Charts' tool, which is cataloged under Visualization for plotting and chart generation.

---

## [1391] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am preparing a BAM file for sensitive low-frequency variant and indel calling. Which tool can insert indel quality scores directly into my alignment file?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Lofreq Indelqual`, which is designed to insert indel qualities into a BAM file.

---

## [1392] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have multiple sequencing datasets that I need to align against a single shared reference genome, and I want the results saved into a single combined output file. Which tool fits this requirement?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `MiModD Read Alignment`. It aligns sequenced reads from an arbitrary number of input datasets against a common reference genome and saves the aligned results into a single, potentially multi-sample output dataset.

---

## [1393] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool should I use to profile cis-natural antisense transcripts (cis-NATs) from strand-specific RNA sequencing data?

**ASSISTANT:** NASTIseq is recommended. It is specifically designed for transcriptomics and strand-specific RNA sequencing (ssRNA-seq) to detect and profile cis-natural antisense transcripts (cis-NATs)—pairs of RNA molecules transcribed from complementary loci.

---

## [1394] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with paired-end sequencing data from a Micro-C chromosome conformation capture protocol. Which tool is specifically built to construct the contact matrix?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `hicBuildMatrixMicroC`, which is designed for 3D genomics and chromosome conformation capture workflows, specifically for Micro-C experiments with paired-end sequencing.

---

## [1395] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are running metagenomic sequencing on wastewater containing multiple SARS-CoV-2 variants and have generated BAM files aligned to Hu-1. Which tool can deconvolve the mixture to estimate variant proportions?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Freyja: Demix`. It is specifically designed to recover relative lineage abundances from mixed SARS-CoV-2 sequencing samples provided as BAM files aligned to the Hu-1 reference.

---

## [1396] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with raw mass spectrometry files from a Thermo Fisher instrument on a Linux environment. Which tool can I use to parse these files using Thermo's library?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `thermorawfileparser`. It is a wrapper around the .NET (C#) ThermoFisher ThermoRawFileReader library designed specifically for running on Linux with Mono, making it suitable for Thermo Fisher mass spectrometry and proteomics data processing workflows.

---

## [1397] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I want to present multiple layers of data onto the leaves of a dendrogram by combining unrooted and force-directed graph algorithms. Which bioinformatics package supports this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `TreeAndLeaf`. It combines unrooted and force-directed graph algorithms to layout binary trees, specifically designed to represent multiple layers of information onto dendrogram leaves.

---

## [1398] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have interval files from a microarray and ChIP-seq experiment in Bed and BedGraph formats. I need to convert them into Wig files for genome-wide coverage visualization. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Bed/BedGraph/GFF to Wig (Convert Formats)'. It converts interval formats like Bed, BedGraph, or GFF to Wig format by mapping the value of each interval into the resulting Wig file.

---

## [1399] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool that can take my genome FASTA/index and compute genome-wide mappability files exported as bedgraph or CSV.

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using `Genmap Map`. It is specifically designed to create mappability files for a genome from index and FASTA files, with support for outputs like bedgraph and csv.

---

## [1400] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on microbial ecology and metagenomics data processing within the Mothur ecosystem. Is there a dedicated Mothur tool for tracking and counting sequence abundances?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use the Mothur tool `Count.seqs`, which is designed for microbial ecology and metagenomics workflows within the Mothur software suite.

---

## [1401] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an annotation file with Ensembl IDs and unique FeatureIDs from an omics experiment and need to convert them into human-readable gene symbols. Which tool does this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Map ENSEMBLIDs to Gene Symbols.'. It takes your file containing FeatureIDs and Ensembl IDs and retrieves human-readable gene symbols using the mygene service.

---

## [1402] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to build a custom BLAST reference database from multiple FASTA files for sequence homology searches in my genomics project. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'NCBI BLAST+ makeblastdb'. It creates a BLAST database from one or more FASTA files and/or existing BLAST databases.

---

## [1403] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with scRNA-seq data and need an algorithm that can reliably detect very rare cell types that typically get lost or treated as outliers in standard clustering workflows. What tool fits this?

**ASSISTANT:** Cluster Inspection using RaceID is well-suited for this task. RaceID is specifically developed for single-cell RNA-sequencing data to identify cell types, with a design tailored to detect rare cells that represent outliers in conventional clustering approaches.

---

## [1404] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on transcriptomic microarray data involving a 3-group comparison. Is there a tool that performs hybrid multiple testing while incorporating method selection and assumption evaluations?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use HybridMTest. It is specifically designed for transcriptomics and microarray studies, performing hybrid multiple testing that incorporates method selection and assumption evaluations (for example, in 3-group comparison analyses) using empirical Bayes probability (EBP) estimates.

---

## [1405] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a systems biology model and need to merge compartments. My entity identifiers have compartment suffixes like xxx_y. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use DegreeWeighting. It merges compartments using identifiers when a compartment suffix is present in the format "xxx_y" (where "xxx" is the base identifier and "y" is the compartment label), or using names if they are consistent and unambiguous across compartments.

---

## [1406] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a tabular dataset that I need to convert into GeoJSON format, and I want all table columns mapped into the feature properties. Which tool handles this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `Table to GeoJSON`. By default, this tool transfers all columns from your input table into the properties field of the GeoJSON output.

---

## [1407] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a multi-sample structural variant VCF and I need to count how many instances of each SVTYPE are present in each sample. Which tool does this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Svtk Countsvtypes`. It counts the instances of each structural variant type (SVTYPE) observed across each sample in a VCF.

---

## [1408] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working in sequence analysis and need a tool to incorporate a newly sequenced bacterial isolate into our cg/wgMLST database. What tool is available for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the tool 'Add strain to cg/wgMLST database', which is designed under the Sequence Analysis category specifically for adding strains into cg/wgMLST databases.

---

## [1409] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with ontology files and need to integrate imported ontologies into my main ontology. Which tool accomplishes this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `Merge the imported ontologies` under Ontology Manipulation, which merges resolvable imported ontologies into the main ontology to output a new ontology.

---

## [1410] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool can I run to align OTUs with Mafft and generate a rooted tree using FastTree and Phangorn for a 16S amplicon dataset?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use FROGS Tree, which is designed for OTU multiple alignment with Mafft and rooted phylogenetic tree creation using FastTree and Phangorn.

---

## [1411] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool from the MIRA v4.0 suite to filter my sequencing reads based on exact k-mer matching against a specific bait sequence file. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use MIRA v4.0 mirabait. It is specifically designed to filter input reads based on whether they share perfect k-mer matches (default 31-mers) with your bait file.

---

## [1412] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool to fetch and extract the clean text content from an online webpage URL for a literature search task. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the tool 'Biomni: Extract Url Content (literature)'. It uses requests and BeautifulSoup to extract text content directly from a webpage URL.

---

## [1413] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with transcriptomics data and need to build single-gene classifiers that remain robust even if there are outliers or uncharacterized sample subgroups. What tool should I look into?

**ASSISTANT:** You should consider `messina`. It provides algorithms specifically designed for constructing optimally robust single-gene classifiers and identifying differential expression when outliers or unknown sample subgroups are present in transcriptomics data.

---

## [1414] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool to rapidly annotate a bacterial genome and generate files suitable for submission to GenBank or ENA. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Prokka. It is designed to rapidly annotate bacterial, archaeal, and viral genomes and produces output files that require only minor tweaking for submission to GenBank, ENA, or DDBJ.

---

## [1415] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to determine optimal parameters for loop calling in my Hi-C dataset using CTCF peak data. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `hicHyperoptDetectLoops`. It computes optimal parameters for loop calling using a protein peak data file in BED format, such as CTCF or cohesin ChIP-seq peaks.

---

## [1416] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have single-end RNA-seq alignments in SAM format, and my downstream analysis requires retaining only uniquely mapping reads. Which tool fits this requirement?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use **BWASplitSAM**. It is designed for single-end transcriptomics/RNA-seq SAM files and filters the alignment so that each SAM file is subset into only uniquely mapping reads.

---

## [1417] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a computational chemistry tool that reads SD-files and extracts molecular descriptors/features for QSAR modeling from training set response values stored in SD-properties. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'InputReader'. It is designed for computational chemistry and chemoinformatics workflows to read SD-files, extract response values from SD-properties for training sets, and generate features for QSAR analysis.

---

## [1418] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are designing a neoantigen discovery workflow and need to generate variant peptides from missense and frameshift variants while reporting stop codon readthrough. What tool fits this need?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend 'Ensembl Variant Report'. It falls under Variant Analysis (with applicability in proteomics, genomics, and immunoproteomics) and reports variant peptides from snpEff missense and frameshift variants. It specifically allows and reports stop codon readthrough, making it suitable for neoantigen discovery workflows.

---

## [1419] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have multiple metabolite annotation tables generated by different software tools that I need to merge, filter, and organize into a unified dataset. What tool fits this task?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `MetMashR`. It is specifically built to merge, filter, sort, organize, and integrate metabolite annotation tables originating from various annotation tools.

---

## [1420] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We just finished assembling a draft genome for an unannotated insect species and want to annotate protein-coding genes using curated proteomes from related model organisms. Which tool is suitable for this fast cross-species alignment?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **Miniprot align**. It is tailored for genomics and comparative gene annotation to rapidly align known protein sequences against a target genome, accounting for affine gap penalties, splicing, and potential frameshifts.

---

## [1421] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a conservation genetics project with multi-population defined cohort structures and need to calculate population statistics in a RAD-seq pipeline. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Stacks: populations`. It is specifically designed for population genomics, evolutionary genomics, and conservation genetics studies with defined population structure, executing in place of `genotypes` when processing cohorts with a population map.

---

## [1422] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on clinical genomics and transcriptomics datasets and need an R tool to build publication-quality Kaplan-Meier plots featuring risk tables, median survival times, and log-rank p-values. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `survminer`. It is a ggplot2-based survival analysis visualization tool designed specifically to produce publication-ready Kaplan-Meier plots with risk tables, median survival annotations, log-rank p-values, and forest plots for Cox models.

---

## [1423] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with single-cell RNA-seq data from multiple samples and need a tool to preprocess the counts, identify cellular heterogeneity, and integrate the datasets. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `Seurat Preprocessing`. It is designed specifically for single-cell transcriptomics (scRNA-seq) experiments to help users identify and interpret sources of heterogeneity and integrate multi-sample scRNA-seq datasets.

---

## [1424] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am looking for a tool under the Sequence Analysis section to generate summary reports from hamronize results. What tool is available?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `hamronize_summarize`, which is categorized under Sequence Analysis for summarizing hamronize data.

---

## [1425] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I ran multiple different biclustering algorithms on my omics dataset and want to combine their results into an ensemble molecular signature. What tool supports this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `mosbi` (Molecular signature Identification from Biclustering). It provides standardized interfaces for biclustering outputs and implements a multi-algorithm ensemble approach to derive consensus molecular signatures.

---

## [1426] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have generated genome visualization tracks in Galaxy and need a tool to produce a standalone, interactive JBrowse view from the data directory. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'JBrowse - Data Directory to Standalone' under the Sequence Analysis category in Galaxy, which packages a prepared JBrowse data directory into a standalone viewer.

---

## [1427] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am setting up a reference server for ribosomal RNA filtering. Which data manager tool can I use to download reference databases for SortMeRNA?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `Download SortMeRNA` data manager tool (under the Data Managers category) to download and index reference rRNA databases for SortMeRNA.

---

## [1428] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a quantitative microscopy analysis workflow and need to extract label-based morphological features from my segmentation masks. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Extract image features' tool. It is suited for quantitative imaging workflows, microscopy, and histopathology to extract morphological features directly from label maps, with the option to incorporate pixel intensities from a matching intensity image.

---

## [1429] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting a comparative microbiome study with unequal sequencing depth across samples using 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing. Which tool can generate multiple rarefied OTU tables while retaining taxonomy?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use multiple_rarefactions. It is suited for comparative microbiome studies with unequal sequencing depth and can output rarefied OTU tables that preserve taxonomic (lineage) information if it is present in your input OTU table.

---

## [1430] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have raw cDNA sequencing reads produced on an Oxford Nanopore device. I need a tool that can identify cDNA primers, orient the reads properly, and trim off flanking adapter sequences. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using `Pychopper`. It is specifically designed for Nanopore cDNA sequencing data to identify, orient, and trim cDNA reads.

---

## [1431] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which metagenomics visualization tool allows me to set a uniform ymax value across alpha diversity rarefaction curves for cross-study comparison?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `make_rarefaction_plots`. It generates alpha diversity rarefaction curves and includes a `ymax` setting (defaulting to `none`) specifically designed to align y-axis scales between different microbial community analyses.

---

## [1432] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I conducted a large survey study and now need to select an optimal subset of samples for subsequent tiered downstream analyses to save costs. What tool is suited for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use microPITA (available via the Run tool in Web Services), which is designed specifically for selecting samples from survey studies within tiered-study designs.

---

## [1433] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am benchmarking several taxonomic classifiers against reference labels for our 16S rRNA gene sequencing data. Which QIIME 2 tool should I use to assess classification accuracy?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `qiime2 rescript evaluate-classifications`. It is suited for benchmarking taxonomic classifiers in metagenomics, 16S rRNA gene sequencing, and ITS profiling by comparing predicted taxonomies to true taxonomic labels.

---

## [1434] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am looking for tools classified under Sequence Analysis in the system catalog.

**ASSISTANT:** One tool listed under Sequence Analysis is 'wobble'. However, note that its documented parameter details in the registry are minimal, so you may need to check your specific environment installation for supported inputs.

---

## [1435] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am looking for a bioinformatics software tool geared toward investigating disease-associated SNPs that lie in non-coding genomic regions. Is SNPhood relevant?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, SNPhood is explicitly designed around the context of disease-associated SNPs located in non-coding genomic regions that affect regulatory functions.

---

## [1436] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a command-line tool for sequence analysis to filter reads by GC content, minimum length, quality, and crop bases from the head or tail. What tool provides these options?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `NanoFilt`. It offers parameters for filtering by quality (`-q`), length (`-l`, `--maxlength`), GC content (`--minGC`, `--maxGC`), as well as trimming read ends (`--headcrop`, `--tailcrop`).

---

## [1437] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I ran multiple gene prediction runs with BRAKER and need a combinatorial tool to combine the predictions based on extrinsic evidence. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use TSEBRA. It is a combinatorial tool developed specifically to combine BRAKER predictions and select transcripts based on support by extrinsic evidence.

---

## [1438] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a tool or library that allows me to programmatically upload and download research data directly to and from my Open Science Framework (OSF) projects. What do you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using 'Osfclient Fetch'. It is a Python library and command-line client designed specifically for up- and downloading files to and from Open Science Framework projects.

---

## [1439] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I'm setting up a ribosome profiling workflow and need to generate a chromosome sizes file directly from my reference FASTA file. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the **Get Chromosome Sizes** tool. It generates a chromosome sizes file from a provided FASTA file, which is specifically needed for building ribosome profiles and genome coordinate mapping.

---

## [1440] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have ecological survey data gathered under the 'Pedestre' protocol and need to conduct a raw observation analysis. Which tool handles this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Advanced restitution: raw approach', as it is specifically designed to analyze observations made using the 'Pedestre' and 'Routier' protocols.

---

## [1441] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on metabolic network curation and quality control for an SBML model. Which tool can check if all the reactions in my model are elementally and chemically balanced?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `SbmlCheckBalance`. It is designed for systems biology and metabolic modeling quality control to verify the balance of all reactions in an SBML model.

---

## [1442] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have mass-spectroscopy data and want to run a standard label-free quantification (LFQ) proteomics pipeline. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Maxquant Lfq' tool, which is designed to run standard proteomics data analysis with MaxQuant and is dedicated to label-free quantification.

---

## [1443] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have paired-end FASTQ reads from an Escherichia coli isolate and need to identify its STEC serotype. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend using Stecfinder, which is designed to serotype Shiga toxin-producing Escherichia coli (STEC) samples directly from paired-end FASTQ reads or assemblies.

---

## [1444] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a reference genome in FASTA format and need to build a GEM index to assess genomics mappability. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Gem3 Gem3Indexer`. It is designed specifically to create a GEM index directly from a FASTA file for genomics and mappability workflows.

---

## [1445] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a systems biology project and need a tool to construct transition systems from our model specifications. What tool is available for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the "eBCSgen transition system generator", which is designed for systems biology to generate transition systems.

---

## [1446] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to set up contamination detection using FCS-GX for our genome assembly quality control. How can I obtain the necessary reference database?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'NCBI FCS GX database downloader' tool. It downloads the curated reference database (approximately 470 GiB, including a JSON manifest) required by NCBI FCS GX for contamination screening.

---

## [1447] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on transcriptomics RNA-seq data and need a tool to compare different differential expression analysis methods. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'compcodeR', which provides functionality specifically for comparing results obtained by different differential expression methods on RNA-seq data.

---

## [1448] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need an R package that offers a unified API for reading raw mass spectrometry data and identification files in mzML and mzIdentML formats. What should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `mzR`. It provides a unified API to common file formats and parsers for mass spectrometry data, including mzML and mzIdentML via a subset of the ProteoWizard library.

---

## [1449] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We performed metagene analysis on matched Ribo-seq and RNA-seq data comparing wildtype and mutant conditions. Which tool will calculate Ribo and RNA-Seq counts and call differentially translated candidates?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `Differential Translation Analysis` tool. It processes the R data file from Metagene analysis to obtain Ribo and RNA-Seq counts and identify top candidates for differential translation using baySeq.

---

## [1450] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an environmental DNA survey with 16S rRNA marker gene amplicon sequencing data and want to predict metagenomic functional gene family abundances. Which tool fits this?

**ASSISTANT:** The `Normalize` tool from the PICRUSt project is specifically suited for this design. Its primary application is predicting unobserved character states and gene family abundances in a microbial community from phylogenetic marker gene data like 16S rRNA surveys.

---

## [1451] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a comparative metagenomics project with 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing data and need to generate pie charts showing taxonomic profiles across multiple samples. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `make_pie_charts`. It is designed for metagenomics, 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing, and taxonomic profiling across multiple samples, allowing you to represent category abundances while grouping minor categories into an 'other' category.

---

## [1452] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have multiple sorted BED files from multi-sample ATAC-seq peak calling and want to identify overlapping intervals across them. Which tool fits this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Intersect multiple sorted BED files' (under Convert Formats). It is specifically designed for multi-sample genomic feature comparison in genomics, epigenomics, ChIP-seq, and ATAC-seq to identify common intervals across multiple sorted BED files.

---

## [1453] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have long-read PacBio sequencing data for a bacterial genome assembly and need a tool to trim, circularize, and orient the assembly. What tool does Berokka suggest trying first?

**ASSISTANT:** Berokka notes that there is already an established tool called Circlator to trim, circularise, and orient genome assemblies, and recommends trying Circlator first for these tasks.

---

## [1454] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have sequencing data and need a tool specifically designed to filter and prioritize genetic variants. What tool is available for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use 'Variant selection with KGGSeq'. It utilizes KGGSeq specifically to filter and prioritize genetic variants from sequencing datasets.

---

## [1455] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have run ustacks on my samples and want to generate summary statistics on the detected stacks. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Stacks: statistics`, which takes the output of `ustacks` (or `pstacks`) and produces a report containing various statistics about the detected stacks across your samples.

---

## [1456] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing output from a PhysiCell multicellular modeling simulation and need a way to see what variables were tracked along with their units of measurement. Which tool can export this mapping?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `pcdl_get_unit_dict`. It is designed for PhysiCell model output analysis and computational biology simulations. It exports a CSV file that lists all tracked variables across metadata, cell, and microenvironment categories, mapping each variable to its respective unit.

---

## [1457] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have multiple haplotype sequences from a population genetics study and want to build a haplotype network. Which tool can generate this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use **Haplophyle**, a variant analysis tool specifically designed to create haplotype networks from haplotype sequences.

---

## [1458] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an annotated genome in GTF format and need to extract all ribosomal RNA transcript annotations. Which tool is tailored for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Rrnatranscripts, which is dedicated to ribosomal RNA extraction directly from GTF files.

---

## [1459] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have metagenomic FASTQ sequence reads and want to perform taxonomic profiling by querying them against a database. What KMCP tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Kmcp Search`. It is specifically designed to search metagenomic sequences (such as FASTQ reads) against a reference database for taxonomic profiling and classification.

---

## [1460] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Can you recommend a preprocessing software package to perform model-based background correction on Illumina BeadArray microarray data?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend MBCB. It is a preprocessing package that provides a model-based background correction method designed specifically for Illumina BeadArray data.

---

## [1461] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to run operations and queries on genomic intervals across multi-sample datasets. What tool can compile or execute these queries in Galaxy?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the GMLQ Query Editor tool. It is designed for genomic interval queries, operations, and large-scale multi-sample genomic data processing, taking GMQL queries either as plain text or from Galaxy history to compile or execute them.

---

## [1462] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which tool can I use to assess cassette behavior and identify differentially abundant cassettes in a pooled CRISPR/Cas9 screen?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `gCrisprTools`. It provides methods for interrogating library and cassette behavior within an experiment and identifying differentially abundant targets in pooled CRISPR/Cas9 or shRNA screens.

---

## [1463] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have an online PDF of a research paper and want to extract its raw text content directly using its web link. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `extract_pdf_content`. It is designed for literature processing and extracts the text content of a PDF file given its URL.

---

## [1464] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working with genetic variability data stored in SeqArray format. What genomics tool provides an interface to this fast-access format for performing common operations and variant analyses?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use SeqVarTools. It is designed specifically for genomics and SNP analysis, providing an interface to the fast-access storage format for VCF data implemented in SeqArray alongside tools for common operations and analyses.

---

## [1465] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have fasta and fastq reads that need to be aligned against a target reference indexed with lastdb. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use 'Last Lastal', which aligns FASTA or FASTQ query sequences against target sequences indexed with lastdb.

---

## [1466] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a memory-efficient and ultrafast short read aligner to map RNA-seq reads directly to a transcriptome. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Bowtie Transcriptome Alignment`. Bowtie, developed by Ben Langmead and Cole Trapnell, is an ultrafast, memory-efficient aligner tailored for short read sequences.

---

## [1467] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which Bioconductor module should I look for if I need to run GWAS.BAYES?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the `gwas_bayes` tool, which corresponds to the Bioconductor module GWAS.BAYES.

---

## [1468] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I assembled transcripts de novo from my RNA-Seq data using Trinity. Which tool should I use to identify candidate coding regions within these assembled sequences?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `TransDecoder`. It is specifically designed to identify candidate coding regions within transcript sequences generated by de novo RNA-Seq assemblers like Trinity.

---

## [1469] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working in a metagenomics analysis pipeline and need a tool to create a preferences file. What tool is available?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the 'make_prefs_file' tool under the Metagenomics category to generate your preferences file.

---

## [1470] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on genomic variation data and have a list of SNP association p-values. I want to compute a combined p-value for curated gene sets. What package fits this use case?

**ASSISTANT:** The `cpvSNP` package is designed for this task. It operates under GeneSetEnrichment, Genetics, and GenomicVariation contexts to combine SNP-level association p-values directly into gene set p-values.

---

## [1471] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have RNA-seq differential expression results and want to avoid relying solely on a single gene set enrichment method like GSEA or camera. Is there an ensemble approach available?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, you can use EGSEA. It combines twelve prominent gene set enrichment algorithms into an ensemble analysis, calculating collective significance scores to provide a robust, consensus-driven functional enrichment assessment.

---

## [1472] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on high-content screening fluorescence microscopy images and need to enhance the intensity of specific cellular structures relative to the rest of the image before identifying objects. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the EnhanceOrSuppressFeatures tool. It applies image processing filters to enhance or suppress pixel intensities relative to the background and outputs a grayscale image designed for downstream object identification using an Identify module.

---

## [1473] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am planning a high-throughput virtual screening (HTVS) project to dock small molecules against both proteins and RNA targets. Which open-source tool fits this?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `rDock`. It is an open-source docking software package specifically designed for High Throughput Virtual Screening (HTVS) campaigns and binding mode prediction studies for small molecules targeting proteins and nucleic acids.

---

## [1474] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I ran a series of fluorogenic peptide cleavage assays in the lab and need an automated tool to evaluate the reaction rates. What would you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `analyze_protease_kinetics`. It is specifically designed within biochemistry workflows to analyze protease kinetics data derived from fluorogenic peptide cleavage assays.

---

## [1475] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are planning a telomere-to-telomere de novo genome assembly using a combination of PacBio HiFi and Oxford Nanopore sequencing reads. Which tool is designed specifically for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Verkko. It is a hybrid genome assembly pipeline specifically developed for telomere-to-telomere assembly combining PacBio HiFi and Oxford Nanopore reads.

---

## [1476] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to compare a query sequence against a known reference sequence to identify mutations. What tool can perform this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use find_sequence_mutations, which compares a query sequence against a reference sequence to identify mutations.

---

## [1477] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on an image registration pipeline in Galaxy and need a tool to merge an elastic transformation with a raw transformation into a single raw transformation. What tool can do this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the tool 'Compose a raw and an elastic transformation'. It is designed to compose one raw transformation and one elastic transformation into a single raw transformation.

---

## [1478] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am conducting an RNA structural biology study to identify alternative secondary conformations within a set energy window above the MFE. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use RNAsubopt. In its default `-e` mode, it is specifically designed for RNA secondary structure prediction studies to output all alternative suboptimal conformations within an energy range above the minimum free energy.

---

## [1479] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have raw amplicon sequencing data from a metagenomics experiment and need a modular pipeline to process it all the way to taxonomic classification. What tool fits this need?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use BioMaS (Bioinformatic analysis of Metagenomic AmpliconS), which provides a modular workflow designed to take raw metagenomic amplicon data through to taxonomy classification.

---

## [1480] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We ran a CRISPR/Cas9 editing validation experiment using targeted amplicon sequencing and need to detect insertions and deletions by comparing the reads to reference targets. What tool fits this need?

**ASSISTANT:** AGEseq is suited for this task. It is designed for amplicon sequencing and targeted mutagenesis/CRISPR validation studies to compare amplicon sequences with expected target sequences and locate insertion/deletion sites.

---

## [1481] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have multiple sequence alignments in FASTA format that I need to convert into sequential Phylip format for my downstream phylogeny software. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use **Fasta2Phylip**, which is specifically designed to convert a FASTA file into a sequential Phylip format suitable for phylogenetic software.

---

## [1482] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a fast small variant caller for germline variant detection using long-read sequencing data (BAM/CRAM format). What do you suggest?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend Clair3. It is specifically designed for germline small variant calling (SNPs and indels) from long-read BAM/CRAM files.

---

## [1483] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which Bioconductor tool can I use to visualize an ExpressionSet or SummarizedExperiment using t-SNE, spectral maps, or linear discriminant analysis?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `esetVis`. It provides utility functions specifically designed for the visualization of `ExpressionSet` or `SummarizedExperiment` Bioconductor objects using spectral maps, t-SNE, and linear discriminant analysis.

---

## [1484] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on sequence analysis tasks involving Snippy alignments and need a tool to clean the full alignment. What tool is available?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `snippy-clean_full_aln`, which is designated for sequence analysis tasks specifically involving Snippy full alignment outputs.

---

## [1485] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We are conducting a transposon insertion sequencing (TraDIS) screen and have strand-specific nucleotide read counts that need to be aggregated to gene insertion data. What tool should we run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Bio-TraDis counts to gene insertion data`. It is specifically designed for functional genomics and TraDIS studies to convert nucleotide-level read counts from both strands into gene insertion data.

---

## [1486] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a GATK or Picard utility to assign all reads within an alignment file to a single new read group. What tool can do this?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use `Gatk4 Addorreplacereadgroups`. It is designed to assign all the reads in a file to a single new read-group.

---

## [1487] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** Which data management tool should I use to install or configure reference datasets for AMRFinderPlus?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `amrfinderplus_datamanager` from the Data Managers section to set up and manage data for AMRFinderPlus.

---

## [1488] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** We generated paired-end RNA-seq data from a non-model organism lacking a reference genome. Which tool can perform de novo transcript assembly in a single execution?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend **Trinity**. It is a de novo transcript assembler that takes RNA-seq data as input and executes all key stages—Inchworm, Chrysalis, and Butterfly—in a single pass, making it well-suited for non-model organism transcriptomics.

---

## [1489] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need to rapidly screen a set of query sequencing reads against a large database to assess containment and potential contamination. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use Mash Screen. It is designed specifically to screen query sequences against large reference sequence databases for containment, contamination detection, and taxonomic assignment.

---

## [1490] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am working on a metabolomics data quality control pipeline and need to verify that my mzspeclib file conforms to specification and has no syntax or content errors. What tool should I run?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'mzspeclib validate' tool. It checks mzspeclib files for format and content correctness against the mzspeclib specification and reports any errors or warnings.

---

## [1491] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have multiple gene clusters derived from a proteomics experiment and want to compare enriched functional categories across all clusters simultaneously. What tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Biological theme comparison' tool. Powered by R's clusterProfiler, it is designed for comparative enrichment analysis across multiple gene clusters across proteomics, transcriptomics, and genomics datasets.

---

## [1492] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a pileup file generated from SAM alignment data and want to filter out low-quality positions and identify sequence variants meeting specific read depth and base quality cutoffs. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'Filter pileup' tool (from the SAM section). It is designed to identify sequence variants and genomic positions covered by a specific number of reads with base qualities above your chosen threshold.

---

## [1493] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I need a quality control tool to filter out low-abundance k-mers from my high-throughput sequence read files. What tool would you recommend?

**ASSISTANT:** I recommend `Khmer Trimlowabund`. It is a genomics quality control and read filtering tool from the khmer suite specifically designed to remove low-abundance k-mers from sequence files.

---

## [1494] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am curating a genome-scale metabolic reconstruction saved in SBML format and need to check every reaction for elemental mass balance and valid chemical formula syntax. Which tool is designed for this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `SbmlCheckBalance`. It evaluates each reaction in an SBML model to verify chemical formula syntax and check whether atom counts are balanced across reactants and products.

## [1495] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have a list of Ensembl gene IDs and need to quickly map them to gene symbols, Entrez IDs, MIM numbers, and UniProt accessions. What live API tool can handle this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use the 'mygene.info' live API tool. It provides REST-based gene information queries with sub-second latency to cross-reference symbols, Entrez, Ensembl, MIM, and UniProt identifiers.

---

## [1496] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** My paired-end alignment has missing mate coordinates and incorrect mate flags. Which SAM tool can populate these fields from a name-sorted alignment?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Samtools fixmate`. It is designed specifically to fill in mate coordinates, ISIZE, and mate-related flags from name-sorted alignment files.

---

## [1497] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have genomic coverage tracks from ChIP-seq and ATAC-seq saved in BigWig format, but my downstream application requires fixed-step Wiggle files. Which tool should I use?

**ASSISTANT:** Use the "BigWig to Wig" tool under the Convert Formats category. It converts BigWig files to fixed-step Wiggle format and allows you to specify the desired resolution via bin size.

---

## [1498] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I have three gene lists from different differential expression comparisons and I want to generate an overlap figure in PDF format. Which tool fits this requirement?

**ASSISTANT:** You can use the `Venn Diagram` tool (Graphics). It generates Venn diagrams for one, two, or three sets and outputs the visualization directly as a PDF file, making it ideal for comparative analysis of gene lists or filtering results.

---

## [1499] source=multitask_sft_big

**USER:** I am analyzing 16S rRNA amplicon sequencing data and need to group sequences based on their taxonomic classification hierarchy rather than de novo distance clustering. Which Mothur module handles this?

**ASSISTANT:** You should use `Phylotype` from the Mothur suite. It bins sequences according to their assigned taxonomy (phylotypes) rather than unconstrained sequence distance clustering.
